# Yogabhāṣya (Vyāsa's commentary on the Yoga Sūtra)

`src:yoga-bhasya` · `skeleton` · confidence high

**Alternate titles:** Vyāsabhāṣya, Sāṃkhyapravacana (colophon title), Pātañjala Yogaśāstra (sūtra and bhāṣya together)
**Original title:** योगभाष्य
**Language:** Sanskrit
**Family:** vedic
**Lineages:** [Pātañjala Yoga (the Yoga darśana)](../lineages/patanjala-yoga.md)
**Genre:** bhāṣya
**Commentary on:** [Yoga Sūtra of Patañjali](yoga-sutra.md)
**Authors:** 
  - [Vyāsa (Kṛṣṇa Dvaipāyana)](../teachers/vyasa.md) — role: commentator; attribution: traditional
  - [Patañjali](../teachers/patanjali.md) — role: author; attribution: disputed
**Attribution:** tradition: By Vyāsa, identified with Veda-Vyāsa and with Bādarāyaṇa (Vijñānabhikṣu, Yogavārttika intro. and 1.1; Nārāyaṇatīrtha, Sūtrārthabodhinī intro.).; scholarly: The author's identity is unknown; Maas (following Bronkhorst and others) holds that sūtra and bhāṣya are one work by Patañjali; older scholarship placed the bhāṣya well after the sūtras. The bhāṣya cites 'Patañjali' by name once (3.44), a point used in this debate.; confidence: disputed
**Dates:** Scholarly account: c. 325–425 CE if one work with the sūtras (Maas); older estimates placed it later (6th–8th c.); (confidence moderate)
**Structure:** running prose commentary on all 195 sūtras, headed by a maṅgala verse to the lord of serpents; each pāda closes 'iti śrīpātañjale sāṃkhyapravacane yogaśāstre ... vyāsabhāṣye'
**Availability:** digitized-original

The oldest and authoritative commentary. It defines yoga as samādhi and names the five levels of mind (1.1); analyzes the vṛttis, the kleśas and their states (with a fifth, 'burnt-seed' state), karma and its fruition; gives the fourfold scheme modelled on medicine (2.15) and the sevenfold final insight (2.27); lists seated postures (2.46); explains saṃyama and the powers, the four kinds of yogin and the invitation of the celestials (3.51), and a detailed cosmology (3.26); and argues against Buddhist views of a momentary mind and of objects as mind-only (1.32, 4.14–24).
**Editions / translations:** 
  - kind: original; name: Ānandāśrama Sanskrit Series 47 (ed. K. Ś. Āgāśe, Pune 1904); GRETIL e-text; licence: public-domain edition; GRETIL research use
  - kind: original; name: Pātañjaladarśana with Vyāsa's bhāṣya and Vijñānabhikṣu's Yogavārttika, ed. Jīvānanda Vidyāsāgara (Calcutta); e-Bhāratī Sampat e-text; licence: public-domain edition
**Commentaries on this text:** [Bhāsvatī](bhasvati.md), [Tattvavaiśāradī](tattvavaisaradi.md), [Pātañjalayogaśāstravivaraṇa (Yogasūtrabhāṣyavivaraṇa)](yoga-sutra-bhasya-vivarana.md), [Yogavārttika](yogavarttika.md)

## Teachings (207: text-verified 204, skeleton 3)

### 1.1 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

'Now' (atha) marks that a topic is taken up (adhikāra): the treatise called 'the teaching of yoga' is to be understood as the subject in hand. Yoga is samādhi, and samādhi is a property of the mind (citta) at every level (sārvabhauma). The levels of the mind (citta-bhūmi) are: scattered (kṣipta), stupefied (mūḍha), distracted (vikṣipta), one-pointed (ekāgra) and stilled (niruddha). In a distracted mind, samādhi that has become subordinate to the distraction does not count on the side of yoga. But in the one-pointed mind, the samādhi that illumines the real object (sadbhūta artha), wears away the afflictions (kleśa), loosens the bonds of karma and turns the mind toward stilling (nirodha) is called samprajñāta yoga. That samādhi is accompanied by reasoning (vitarka), by reflection (vicāra), by joy (ānanda) or by I-am-ness (asmitā) — Vyāsa says he will set this out later. In the stilling of all activities, samādhi is asamprajñāta.

> athety ayam adhikārārthaḥ /
yogānuśāsanaṃ śāstram adhikṛtaṃ veditavyam /
yogaḥ samādhiḥ /
sa ca sārvabhaumaś cittasya dharmaḥ /
kṣiptaṃ mūḍhaṃ vikṣiptam ekāgraṃ niruddham iti cittabhūmayaḥ /
tatra vikṣipte cetasi vikṣepopasarjanībhūtaḥ samādhir na yogapakṣe vartate /
yas tv ekāgre cetasi sadbhūtam arthaṃ pradyotayati kṣiṇoti ca kleśān karmabandhanāni ślathayati nirodham abhimukhaṃ karoti sa saṃprajñāto yoga ity ākhyāyate /
sa ca vitarkānugato vicārānugata ānandānugato 'smitānugata ity upariṣṭān nivedayiṣyāmaḥ /
sarvavṛttinirodhe tv asaṃprajñātaḥ samādhiḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, practice_

### 1.2 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Because the word 'all' (sarva) is not taken in the sūtra, samprajñāta too is called yoga. The mind (citta) has three guṇas, because its nature is illumination (prakhyā), activity (pravṛtti) and steadiness/inertia (sthiti). Its sattva, whose form is illumination, when mixed with rajas and tamas becomes fond of lordship (aiśvarya) and sense-objects. The same, pierced by tamas, goes toward what is not dharma (adharma), ignorance (ajñāna), non-dispassion (avairāgya) and non-lordship (anaiśvarya). The same, when the covering of delusion has been worn away, shining in every direction and touched only by a measure of rajas, goes toward dharma, knowledge (jñāna), dispassion (vairāgya) and lordship. The same, freed from even a trace of the taint of rajas, established in its own form, reduced to the discernment of the difference between sattva and puruṣa, goes to the meditation of the cloud of dharma (dharmamegha-dhyāna); meditators call this the supreme discerning insight (prasaṃkhyāna). The power of consciousness (citi-śakti) is unchanging (apariṇāminī), does not pass over into anything (apratisaṃkramā), shows the objects (darśita-viṣayā), is pure and unending; but this discernment (vivekakhyāti) is of the nature of the sattva-guṇa and so is the opposite of it. The mind, dispassionate even toward that, stills that discernment too; in that state it holds only impressions (saṃskāra) — that is seedless samādhi (nirbīja). Nothing is fully known there, hence it is asamprajñāta. Thus yoga, the stilling of the mind's activities, is twofold.

> sarvaśabdāgrahaṇāt saṃprajñāto 'pi yoga ity ākhyāyate /
cittaṃ hi prakhyāpravṛttisthitiśīlatvāt triguṇam /
prakhyārūpaṃ hi cittasattvaṃ rajastamobhyāṃ saṃsṛṣṭam aiśvaryaviṣayapriyaṃ bhavati /
tad eva tamasānuviddham adharmājñānāvairāgyānaiśvaryopagaṃ bhavati /
tad eva prakṣīṇamohāvaraṇaṃ sarvataḥ pradyotamānam anuviddhaṃ rajomātrayā dharmajñānavairāgyaiśvaryopagaṃ bhavati /
tad eva rajoleśamalāpetaṃ svarūpapratiṣṭhaṃ sattvapuruṣānyatākhyātimātraṃ dharmameghadhyānopagaṃ bhavati /
tat paraṃ prasaṃkhyānam ity ācakṣate dhyāyinaḥ /
citiśaktir apariṇāminy apratisaṃkramā darśitaviṣayā śuddhā cānantā ca sattvaguṇātmikā ceyam ato viparītā vivekakhyātir iti /
atas tasyāṃ viraktaṃ cittaṃ tām api khyātiṃ niruṇaddhi /
tadavasthaṃ saṃskāropagaṃ bhavati /
sa nirbījaḥ samādhiḥ /
na tatra kiṃcit saṃprajñāyata ity asaṃprajñātaḥ /
dvividhaḥ sa yogaś cittavṛttinirodha iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, ultimate_

### 1.3 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

At that time the power of consciousness (citi-śakti) is established in its own form, as in kaivalya. But when the mind is in the emergent state (vyutthāna), though the power of consciousness is still present, it is not so established.

> svarūpapratiṣṭhā tadānīṃ citiśaktir yathā kaivalye /
vyutthānacitte tu sati tathāpi bhavantī na tathā

_level: ultimate · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate_

terms: [citi-śakti](../terms/citi-sakti.md), [svarūpa-pratiṣṭhā](../terms/svarupa-pratistha.md), [kaivalya](../terms/kaivalya.md), [vyutthāna](../terms/vyutthana.md) · concepts: [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md), [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md)

### 1.4 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

In emergence (vyutthāna), the puruṣa has an activity not distinguished from whatever activities the mind has. So a sūtra [quoted] says: 'Seeing is one alone; discernment (khyāti) alone is seeing.' The mind is like a magnet (ayaskānta-maṇi): it helps merely by being near, and by being seen it becomes the possession of the puruṣa, its master. Therefore the beginningless connection of the puruṣa with the mind is the cause of the awareness of the mind's activities.

> vyutthāne yāś cittavṛttayas tadaviśiṣṭavṛttiḥ puruṣaḥ /
tathā ca sūtram ekam eva darśanaṃ khyātir eva darśanam iti /
cittam ayaskāntamaṇikalpaṃ saṃnidhimātropakāri dṛśyatvena svaṃ bhavati puruṣasya svāminaḥ /
tasmāc cittavṛttibodhe puruṣasyānādiḥ saṃbandho hetuḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [vṛtti-sārūpya](../terms/vrtti-sarupya.md), [vyutthāna](../terms/vyutthana.md), [citta](../terms/citta.md), [puruṣa](../terms/purusa.md), [dṛśya](../terms/drsya.md), [saṃyoga](../terms/samyoga.md) · concepts: [The seer and the seen (draṣṭṛ and dṛśya)](../concepts/seer-and-seen.md), [The witness (the seer as pratisaṃvedin)](../concepts/witness.md), [The conjunction of seer and seen (saṃyoga)](../concepts/samyoga.md)

### 1.5 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Afflicted (kliṣṭa) activities are those caused by the afflictions (kleśa), which become the field (kṣetra) in which the store of karma (karmāśaya) is heaped up. Unafflicted (akliṣṭa) activities have discernment (khyāti) as their object and oppose the dominion of the guṇas. Even unafflicted ones that occur within the stream of afflicted ones remain unafflicted; in the gaps of the afflicted, unafflicted ones arise, and in the gaps of the unafflicted, afflicted ones. Impressions (saṃskāra) of the same kind are produced by the activities, and activities by the impressions: so the wheel of activity and impression (vṛtti-saṃskāra-cakra) turns without ceasing. The mind so constituted, once its task is complete, either stays on as if it were the self (ātmakalpena) or goes to dissolution (pralaya). Thus the activities are fivefold, afflicted and unafflicted.

> kleśahetukāḥ karmāśayapracaye kṣetrībhūtāḥ kliṣṭāḥ /
khyātiviṣayā guṇādhikāravirodhinyo 'kliṣṭāḥ /
kliṣṭapravāhapatitā apy akliṣṭāḥ /
kliṣṭacchidreṣv apy akliṣṭā bhavanti /
akliṣṭacchideṣu kliṣṭā iti /
tathājātīyakāḥ saṃskārā vṛttibhir eva kriyante /
saṃskāraiś ca vṛttaya iti /
evaṃ vṛttisaṃskāracakram aniśam āvartate /
tad evaṃbhūtaṃ cittam avasitādhikāram ātmakalpena vyavatiṣṭhate pralayaṃ vā gacchatīti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

terms: [kliṣṭa](../terms/klista.md), [akliṣṭa](../terms/aklista.md), [vṛtti](../terms/vrtti.md), [karmāśaya](../terms/karmasaya.md), [saṃskāra](../terms/samskara.md), [kleśa](../terms/klesa.md) · concepts: [The five activities of the mind (pañca vṛttayaḥ)](../concepts/five-vrttis.md), [The store of karma (karmāśaya)](../concepts/karmasaya.md), [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md), [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../concepts/five-klesas.md) · obstacles: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../obstacles/five-klesas.md)

### 1.7 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-7"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Perception is the mind's activity in which, coloured by an external thing through the channel of the senses, it takes as object a thing that is both general and particular, chiefly ascertaining the particular. Its fruit is the puruṣa's undifferentiated awareness of the mind's activity; the puruṣa is the witness of cognitions (pratisaṃvedin), to be established later. Inference is the activity that chiefly ascertains the general: its object is the relation of the inferable that runs through things of the same class and is absent from those of a different class. Example: the moon and stars move, because they reach other places, like Caitra; the Vindhya mountain does not reach other places and does not move. Testimony (āgama): a thing seen or inferred by a trustworthy person (āpta) is communicated by words so as to pass into another's understanding; the hearer's activity that has that thing as its object, arising from the words, is āgama. A testimony floats (plavate, is unreliable) when the speaker's meaning is not to be trusted and he has neither seen nor inferred it; with an original speaker who has seen or inferred the thing, it would not float.

> indriyapraṇālikayā cittasya bāhyavastūparāgāt tadviṣayā sāmānyaviśeṣātmano 'rthasya viśeṣāvadhāraṇapradhānā vṛttiḥ pratyakṣaṃ pramāṇam /
phalam aviśiṣṭaḥ pauruṣeyaś cittavṛttibodhaḥ /
pratisaṃvedī puruṣa ity upariṣṭād upapādayiṣyāmaḥ /
anumeyasya tulyajātīyeṣv anuvṛtto bhinnajātīyebhyo vyāvṛttaḥ saṃbandho yas tadviṣayā sāmānyāvadhāraṇapradhānā vṛttir anumānam /
yathā deśāntaraprāpter gatimac candratārakaṃ caitravat, vindhyaś cāprāptir agatiḥ /
āptena dṛṣṭo 'numito vārthaḥ paratra svabodhasaṃkrāntaye śabdenopadiśyate, śabdāt tadarthaviṣayā vṛttiḥ śrotur āgamaḥ /
yasyāśraddheyārtho vaktā na dṛṣṭānumitārthaḥ sa āgamaḥ plavate /
mūlavaktari tu dṛṣṭānumitārthe nirviplavaḥ syāt

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [pratyakṣa](../terms/pratyaksa.md), [anumāna](../terms/anumana.md), [āgama](../terms/agama.md), [pramāṇa](../terms/pramana.md), [pratisaṃvedin](../terms/pratisamvedin.md) · concepts: [Means of valid knowledge (pramāṇa)](../concepts/pramana.md), [The witness (the seer as pratisaṃvedin)](../concepts/witness.md) · disputes: [How many means of valid knowledge (pramāṇa) are there?](../debates/number-of-pramanas.md)

### 1.8 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-8"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Why is error not valid cognition (pramāṇa)? Because it is sublated (bādhyate) by valid cognition, valid cognition having a real object. Here the sublation of the invalid by the valid is seen: for instance, seeing two moons is sublated by the seeing of one moon, which has a real object. This error has five joints (pañcaparvā): it is avidyā. Ignorance (avidyā), I-am-ness (asmitā), attachment (rāga), aversion (dveṣa) and clinging to life (abhiniveśa) are the afflictions (kleśa). These same, by their own names, are darkness (tamas), delusion (moha), great delusion (mahāmoha), gloom (tāmisra) and blind gloom (andhatāmisra). They will be set out on the occasion of the taints of the mind (citta-mala).

> sa kasmān na pramāṇam /
yataḥ pramāṇena bādhyate /
bhūtārthaviṣayatvāt pramāṇasya /
tatra pramāṇena bādhanam apramāṇasya dṛṣṭam /
tadyathā dvicandradarśanaṃ sadviṣayeṇaikacandradarśanena bādhyata iti /
seyaṃ pañcaparvā bhavaty avidyā /
avidyāsmitārāgadveṣābhiniveśāḥ kleśā iti /
eta eva svasaṃjñābhis tamo moho mahāmohas tāmisro 'ndhatāmisra iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

terms: [viparyaya](../terms/viparyaya.md), [avidyā](../terms/avidya.md), [asmitā](../terms/asmita.md), [rāga](../terms/raga.md), [dveṣa](../terms/dvesa.md), [abhiniveśa](../terms/abhinivesa.md), [kleśa](../terms/klesa.md) · concepts: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../concepts/five-klesas.md), [The five activities of the mind (pañca vṛttayaḥ)](../concepts/five-vrttis.md) · obstacles: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../obstacles/five-klesas.md), [Ignorance (avidyā)](../obstacles/avidya.md)

### 1.9 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-9"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

It does not rise to valid cognition and does not rise to error. Though empty of a real thing, ordinary usage (vyavahāra) resting on the power of verbal knowledge is seen. Example: 'consciousness (caitanya) is the puruṣa's own nature'. When the puruṣa is consciousness (cit) itself, what is being said of what, and by what? Yet an activity occurs in the statement, as in 'Caitra's cow'. Likewise, in 'the puruṣa, having had the property of a thing denied, is without action': 'the arrow stands', 'will stand', 'stood' — when movement ceases, only the meaning of the verbal root is understood. And in 'the puruṣa is of the nature of non-origination', what is understood is only the absence of origination, not a property that belongs to the puruṣa. So that property is a construction (vikalpita), and ordinary usage exists through it.

> sa na pramāṇopārohī /
na viparyayopārohī ca /
vastuśūnyatve 'pi śabdajñānamāhātmyanibandhano vyavahāro dṛśyate /
tad yathā caitanyaṃ puruṣasya svarūpam iti /
yadā citir eva puruṣas tadā kim atra kena vyapadiśyate /
bhavati ca vyapadeśe vṛttiḥ /
yathā caitrasya gaur iti /
tathā pratiṣiddhavastudharmo niṣkriyaḥ puruṣaḥ, tiṣṭhati bāṇaḥ sthāsyati sthita iti /
gatinivṛttau dhātvarthamātraṃ gamyate /
tathānutpattidharmā puruṣa iti, utpattidharmasyābhāvamātram avagamyate na puruṣānvayī dharmaḥ /
tasmād vikalpitaḥ sa dharmas tena cāsti vyavahāra iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, sound-language_

terms: [vikalpa](../terms/vikalpa.md), [śabda-jñāna](../terms/sabda-jnana.md), [vastu-śūnya](../terms/vastu-sunya.md) · concepts: [The five activities of the mind (pañca vṛttayaḥ)](../concepts/five-vrttis.md), [Word, meaning and cognition](../concepts/word-meaning-cognition.md)

### 1.10 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-10"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Sleep is a particular kind of cognition (pratyaya-viśeṣa), because on waking it is recollected (pratyavamarśa). How? One says: 'I slept happily; my mind is clear; it makes my understanding lucid.' Or: 'I slept in pain (duḥkham); my mind, dull (styāna), wanders unsteady.' Or: 'I slept deeply, stupefied (mūḍha); my limbs are heavy; my mind is weary; it stays sluggish, as if robbed.' This recollection by one awake would not occur if there were no experience of a cognition, and the memories resting on it and having it as object would not occur either. So sleep is a particular cognition. And like the other cognitions, it is to be stilled in samādhi.

> sā ca saṃprabodhe pratyavamarśāt pratyayaviśeṣaḥ /
katham, sukham aham asvāpsam /
prasannaṃ me manaḥ /
prajñāṃ me viśāradīkaroti /
duḥkham aham asvāpsam /
styānaṃ me mano bhramaty anavasthitam gāḍhaṃ mūḍho 'ham asvāpsam /
gurūṇi me gātrāṇi /
klāntaṃ me cittam /
alasaṃ muṣitam iva tiṣṭhatīti /
sa khalv ayaṃ prabuddhasya pratyavamarśo na syād asati pratyayānubhave tadāśritāḥ smṛtayś ca tadviṣayā na syuḥ /
tasmāt pratyayaviśeṣo nidrā /
sā ca samādhāv itarapratyayavan niroddhavyeti

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [nidrā](../terms/nidra.md), [styāna](../terms/styana.md), [mūḍha](../terms/mudha.md) · concepts: [The five activities of the mind (pañca vṛttayaḥ)](../concepts/five-vrttis.md), [Deep sleep](../concepts/deep-sleep.md) · obstacles: [Dullness (styāna)](../obstacles/styana.md)

### 1.11 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-11"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Does the mind remember the cognition or the object? A cognition coloured by the grasped (grāhya) shines with the double form of grasped and grasping and starts an impression (saṃskāra) of its own kind. When that impression is made manifest by its own manifester it produces a memory of just that form, consisting of both grasped and grasping. In the original cognition, the understanding (buddhi) has the form of grasping first; in memory the form of the grasped comes first. Memory is twofold: of what was cultivated (bhāvita-smartavya) and of what was not cultivated (abhāvita-smartavya); in dream the first, in the waking time the second. All memories arise from the experience of valid cognition, error, conceptual construction, sleep and memory. All these activities are of the nature of pleasure, pain and delusion (sukha-duḥkha-moha), which are to be explained under the afflictions (kleśa): attachment (rāga) follows on pleasure, aversion (dveṣa) follows on pain, and delusion (moha) is ignorance (avidyā). All these activities are to be stilled; when they are stilled, samādhi comes about, either samprajñāta or asamprajñāta.

> kiṃ pratyayasya cittaṃ smaraty āhosvid viṣayasyeti /
grāhyoparaktaḥ pratyayo grāhyagrahaṇobhayākāranirbhāsas tajjātīyakaṃ saṃskāram ārabhate /
sa saṃskāraḥ svavyañjakāñjanas tadākārām eva grāhyagrahaṇobhayātmikāṃ smṛtiṃ janayati /
tatra grahaṇākārapūrvā buddhiḥ /
grāhyākārapūrvā smṛtiḥ /
sā ca dvayī --- bhāvitasmartavyā cābhāvitasmartavyā ca /
svapne bhāvitasmartavyā /
jāgratsamaye tv abhāvitasmartavyeti /
sarvāḥ smṛtayaḥ pramāṇaviparyayavikalpanidrāsmṛtīnām anubhavāt prabhavanti /
sarvāś caitā vṛttayaḥ sukhaduḥkhamohātmikāḥ /
sukhaduḥkhamohāś ca kleśeṣu vyākhyeyāḥ /
sukhānuśayī rāgaḥ /
duḥkhānuśayī dveṣaḥ /
mohaḥ punar avidyeti /
etāḥ sarvā vṛttayo niroddhavyāḥ /
āsāṃ nirodhe saṃprajñāto vā samādhir bhavaty asaṃprajñāto veti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [smṛti](../terms/smrti.md), [saṃskāra](../terms/samskara.md), [rāga](../terms/raga.md), [dveṣa](../terms/dvesa.md), [avidyā](../terms/avidya.md), [vṛtti](../terms/vrtti.md) · concepts: [The five activities of the mind (pañca vṛttayaḥ)](../concepts/five-vrttis.md), [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md), [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../concepts/five-klesas.md) · obstacles: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../obstacles/five-klesas.md)

### 1.12 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-12"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The river of the mind (citta-nadī) flows in both directions: it flows toward the good (kalyāṇa) and toward evil (pāpa). The one that inclines toward kaivalya, with its depth toward discernment (viveka), carries toward the good; the one that inclines toward saṃsāra, with its depth toward non-discernment, carries toward evil. Of these, by dispassion the stream toward objects is made barren (khilīkriyate); by the practice of the vision of discernment (viveka-darśana) the stream of discernment is opened. So the stilling of the mind's activities depends on both.

> cittanadī nāmobhayatovāhinī vahati kalyāṇāya vahati pāpāya ca /
yā tu kaivalyaprāgbhārā vivekaviṣayanimnā sā kalyāṇavahā /
saṃsāraprāgbhārāvivekaviṣayaniṃnā pāpavahā /
tatra vairāgyeṇa viṣayasrotaḥ khilīkriyate /
vivekadarśanābhyāsena vivekasrota udghāṭyata ity ubhayādhīnaś cittavṛttinirodhaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [citta-nadī](../terms/citta-nadi.md), [abhyāsa](../terms/abhyasa.md), [vairāgya](../terms/vairagya.md), [viveka-khyāti](../terms/viveka-khyati.md) · concepts: [Practice and dispassion (abhyāsa and vairāgya)](../concepts/abhyasa-vairagya.md), [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md) · practices: [Practice (abhyāsa)](../practices/abhyasa.md), [Dispassion (vairāgya)](../practices/vairagya.md)

### 1.13 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-13"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Stability (sthiti) is the peaceful flow (praśānta-vāhitā) of a mind without activities. Endeavour (prayatna), vigour (vīrya) and enthusiasm (utsāha) are for that. Practice (abhyāsa) is the carrying out of the means to it, done out of the wish to accomplish it.

> cittasyāvṛttikasya praśāntavāhitā sthitiḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [abhyāsa](../terms/abhyasa.md), [sthiti](../terms/sthiti.md), [praśānta-vāhitā](../terms/prasanta-vahita.md), [vīrya](../terms/virya.md), [utsāha](../terms/utsaha.md) · concepts: [Practice and dispassion (abhyāsa and vairāgya)](../concepts/abhyasa-vairagya.md) · practices: [Practice (abhyāsa)](../practices/abhyasa.md)

### 1.14 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-14"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Practice followed for a long time, without a break, and with satkāra, accomplished through austerity (tapas), celibacy/studentship (brahmacarya), knowledge (vidyā) and faith (śraddhā), becomes firmly grounded (dṛḍha-bhūmi) when it has satkāra. That means its object is not suddenly overcome by the impression of emergence (vyutthāna-saṃskāra).

> dīrghakālāsevito nirantarāsevitaḥ satkārāsevitaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [abhyāsa](../terms/abhyasa.md), [dṛḍha-bhūmi](../terms/drdha-bhumi.md), [satkāra](../terms/satkara.md), [tapas](../terms/tapas.md), [brahmacarya](../terms/brahmacarya.md), [śraddhā](../terms/sraddha.md), [vyutthāna](../terms/vyutthana.md) · concepts: [Practice and dispassion (abhyāsa and vairāgya)](../concepts/abhyasa-vairagya.md) · practices: [Practice (abhyāsa)](../practices/abhyasa.md), [Austerity (tapas)](../practices/tapas.md) · obstacles: [Impressions of emergence (vyutthāna-saṃskāra)](../obstacles/vyutthana-samskara.md)

### 1.15 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-15"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Seen objects are, for example, women, food and drink, and lordship (aiśvarya). One is free of thirst for those; and free of thirst for heard-of objects — the attainment of heaven, of the bodiless state (vaidehya) and of merger in prakṛti (prakṛtilayatva). Even when divine or non-divine objects come into contact with him, the mind sees the fault in objects and, by the power of discerning insight (prasaṃkhyāna), has a consciousness of mastery that is without engagement (anābhoga-ātmikā) and empty of rejecting and taking (heya-upādeya-śūnyā). That is dispassion (vairāgya).

> striyo 'nnapānam aiśvaryam iti dṛṣṭaviṣaye vitṛṣṇasya svargavaidehyaprakṛtilayatvaprāptāv ānuśravikaviṣaye vitṛṣṇasya divyādivyaviṣayasaṃprayoge 'pi cittasya viṣayadoṣadarśinaḥ prasaṃkhyānabalād anābhogātmikā heyopādeyaśūnyā vaśīkārasaṃjñā vairāgyam

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [vairāgya](../terms/vairagya.md), [vaśīkāra-saṃjñā](../terms/vasikara-samjna.md), [prasaṃkhyāna](../terms/prasankhyana.md), [aiśvarya](../terms/aisvarya.md), [videha](../terms/videha.md), [prakṛtilaya](../terms/prakrtilaya.md) · concepts: [Dispassion, lower and higher](../concepts/vairagya.md), [The bodiless and those merged in prakṛti](../concepts/videha-prakrtilaya.md) · practices: [Dispassion (vairāgya)](../practices/vairagya.md)

### 1.16 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-16"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who has seen the fault in seen and scripture-taught objects is dispassionate; by the practice of the vision of the puruṣa, his understanding (buddhi) is refreshed by the discernment of the puruṣa's purity, and he is dispassionate toward the guṇas, whose properties are manifest and unmanifest. So dispassion is twofold. Of the two, the latter is only the clearness (prasāda) of knowledge. When it arises, the yogin in whom discernment has risen thinks: 'What was to be attained is attained; the afflictions to be destroyed are destroyed; the linked chain of becoming (bhava-saṃkrama), by whose unbroken continuance one is born and dies and after dying is born again, is cut.' The highest limit (parā kāṣṭhā) of knowledge is dispassion; kaivalya is inseparably bound to it (nāntarīyaka).

> dṛṣṭānuśravikaviṣayadoṣadarśī viraktaḥ puruṣadarśanābhyāsāt tacchuddhipravivekāpyāyitabuddhir guṇebhyo vyaktāvyaktadharmakebhyo virakta iti /
tad dvayaṃ vairāgyam /
tatra yad uttaraṃ taj jñānaprasādamātram /
yasyodaye sati yogī pratyuditakhyātir evaṃ manyate --- prāptaṃ prāpaṇīyaṃ, kṣīṇāḥ kṣetavyāḥ kleśāḥ, chinnaḥ śliṣṭaparvā bhavasaṃkramaḥ, yasyāvicchedāj janitvā mriyate mṛtvā ca jāyata iti /
jñānasyaiva parā kāṣṭhā vairāgyam /
etasyaiva hi nāntarīyakaṃ kaivalyam

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: practice, consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

terms: [para-vairāgya](../terms/para-vairagya.md), [guṇa-vaitṛṣṇya](../terms/guna-vaitrsnya.md), [puruṣa-khyāti](../terms/purusa-khyati.md), [kaivalya](../terms/kaivalya.md), [guṇa](../terms/guna.md) · concepts: [Dispassion, lower and higher](../concepts/vairagya.md), [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md) · practices: [Dispassion (vairāgya)](../practices/vairagya.md)

### 1.17 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-17"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Reasoning (vitarka) is the mind's gross engagement (ābhoga) with its support (ālambana); reflection (vicāra) is the subtle one; joy (ānanda) is delight (hlāda); I-am-ness (asmitā) is awareness (saṃvid) whose nature is single-selved (ekātmikā). Of these, the first samādhi, accompanied by all four, is 'with reasoning' (savitarka); the second, lacking reasoning, is 'with reflection' (savicāra); the third, lacking reflection, is 'with joy' (sānanda); the fourth, lacking that too, is 'I-am-ness alone' (asmitāmātra). All these samādhis are with a support (sālambana).

> vitarkaś cittasyālambane sthūla ābhogaḥ /
sūkṣmo vicāraḥ /
ānando hlādaḥ /
ekātmikā saṃvid asmitā /
tatra prathamaś catuṣṭayānugataḥ samādhiḥ savitarkaḥ /
dvitīyo vitarkavikalaḥ savicāraḥ /
tṛtīyo vicāravikalaḥ sānandaḥ /
caturthas tadvikalo 'smitāmātra iti /
sarva ete sālambanāḥ samādhayaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: advanced (samprajñāta samādhi) · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [vitarka](../terms/vitarka.md), [vicāra](../terms/vicara.md), [ānanda](../terms/ananda.md), [asmitā](../terms/asmita.md), [savitarkā](../terms/savitarka.md), [savicārā](../terms/savicara.md), [sānanda](../terms/sananda.md), [asmitāmātra](../terms/asmitamatra.md), [sālambana](../terms/salambana.md), [samprajñāta](../terms/samprajnata.md) · concepts: [Samādhi with full awareness (samprajñāta)](../concepts/samprajnata-samadhi.md), [Samādhi with seed and seedless (sabīja, nirbīja)](../concepts/sabija-nirbija.md) · disputes: [What are the objects of the bliss (ānanda) and I-am-ness (asmitā) forms of samprajñāta samādhi, and who are the videhas and prakṛtilayas?](../debates/objects-of-samprajnata.md)

### 1.18 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-18"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

When all activities have set (pratyastamaya), the stilling of the mind with only impressions remaining (saṃskāra-śeṣa) is samādhi called asamprajñāta. Its means is the higher dispassion (para vairāgya). Practice that rests on a support (sālambana) is not fit to bring this about; so the cognition of cessation (virāma-pratyaya), which has no real thing behind it (nirvastuka) and is empty of an object, is taken as the support. The mind that has practised that becomes without support (nirālambana), as if it had reached non-being; this is seedless (nirbīja) samādhi, asamprajñāta. The bhāṣya then says it is of two kinds, from means (upāya-pratyaya) and from birth (bhava-pratyaya), and that the first belongs to yogins.

> sarvavṛttipratyastamaye saṃskāraśeṣo nirodhaś cittasya samādhir asaṃprajñātaḥ /
tasya paraṃ vairāgyam upāyaḥ /
sālambano hy abhyāsas tatsādhanāya na kalpata iti virāmapratyayo nirvastuka ālambanīkriyate /
sa cārthaśūnyaḥ /
tadabhyāsapūrvakaṃ hi cittaṃ nirālambanam abhāvaprāptam iva bhavatīty eṣa nirbījaḥ samādhir asaṃprajñātaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: advanced (asamprajñāta samādhi) · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

terms: [asamprajñāta](../terms/asamprajnata.md), [nirbīja](../terms/nirbija.md), [virāma-pratyaya](../terms/virama-pratyaya.md), [para-vairāgya](../terms/para-vairagya.md), [nirvastuka](../terms/nirvastuka.md), [nirālambana](../terms/niralambana.md) · concepts: [Samādhi beyond object-awareness (asamprajñāta)](../concepts/asamprajnata-samadhi.md), [Samādhi with seed and seedless (sabīja, nirbīja)](../concepts/sabija-nirbija.md), [Dispassion, lower and higher](../concepts/vairagya.md), [Samādhi from means and from birth (upāya-pratyaya, bhava-pratyaya)](../concepts/upaya-bhava-pratyaya.md) · practices: [Dispassion (vairāgya)](../practices/vairagya.md)

### 1.19 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-19"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

For the bodiless (videha), who are gods, samādhi is from birth (bhava-pratyaya). With a mind serving only their own impressions, they experience as it were the state of kaivalya, and pass their span, the ripening of their own impressions of that kind. Likewise those merged in prakṛti (prakṛti-laya), whose mind, still holding an office (sādhikāra), is merged in prakṛti, experience as it were the state of kaivalya, until the mind returns again through the force of that office.

> videhānāṃ devānāṃ bhavapratyayaḥ /
te hi svasaṃskāramātropayogena cittena kaivalyapadam ivānubhavantaḥ svasaṃskāravipākaṃ tathājātīyakam ativāhayanti /
tathā prakṛtilayāḥ sādhikāre cetasi prakṛtilīne kaivalyapadam ivānubhavanti, yāvan na punar āvartate 'dhikāravaśāc cittam iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: cosmic · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, death-dying, karma-liberation_

terms: [bhava-pratyaya](../terms/bhava-pratyaya.md), [videha](../terms/videha.md), [prakṛtilaya](../terms/prakrtilaya.md), [kaivalya](../terms/kaivalya.md) · concepts: [The bodiless and those merged in prakṛti](../concepts/videha-prakrtilaya.md), [Samādhi from means and from birth (upāya-pratyaya, bhava-pratyaya)](../concepts/upaya-bhava-pratyaya.md), [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md) · obstacles: [Absorption in prakṛti or the bodiless state short of kaivalya](../obstacles/prakrtilaya-short-of-kaivalya.md)

### 1.20 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-20"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Samādhi from means (upāya-pratyaya) belongs to yogins. Faith (śraddhā) is serenity (saṃprasāda) of the mind; like a kind mother it protects the yogin. In one who has faith and seeks discernment (viveka), energy (vīrya) arises; in one in whom energy has arisen, mindfulness (smṛti) stands present; when mindfulness is present the mind, unagitated, is concentrated (samādhīyate); in one whose mind is concentrated, the discernment of insight (prajñā-viveka) comes about, by which he knows the thing as it is. From the practice of that, and from dispassion toward each of its objects, asamprajñāta samādhi comes about. The bhāṣya then says these yogins are of nine kinds by their means (mild, middling, intense) and, within each, by ardor (saṃvega: mild, middling, intense).

> upāyapratyayo yogināṃ bhavati /
śraddhā cetasaḥ saṃprasādaḥ /
sā hi jananīva kalyāṇī yoginaṃ pāti /
tasya hi śraddadhānasya vivekārthino vīryam upajāyate /
samupajātavīryasya smṛtir upatiṣṭhate /
smṛtyupasthāne ca cittam anākulaṃ samādhīyate /
samāhitacittasya prajñāviveka upāvartate /
yena yathārthaṃ vastu jānāti /
tadabhyāsāt tattadviṣayāc ca vairāgyād asaṃprajñātaḥ samādhir bhavati

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [śraddhā](../terms/sraddha.md), [vīrya](../terms/virya.md), [smṛti](../terms/smrti.md), [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md), [upāya-pratyaya](../terms/upaya-pratyaya.md), [saṃvega](../terms/samvega.md), [mṛdu](../terms/mrdu.md), [adhimātra](../terms/adhimatra.md), [Prājña](../terms/prajna.md) · concepts: [The five means: faith, energy, mindfulness, samādhi, insight](../concepts/sraddha-virya-smrti-samadhi-prajna.md), [Samādhi from means and from birth (upāya-pratyaya, bhava-pratyaya)](../concepts/upaya-bhava-pratyaya.md), [Grades of practitioners (mṛdu, madhya, adhimātra)](../concepts/grades-of-practitioners.md) · practices: [Dispassion (vairāgya)](../practices/vairagya.md), [Practice (abhyāsa)](../practices/abhyasa.md)

### 1.21 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-21"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

For those of intense ardor, the attainment of samādhi and the fruit of samādhi come about [near].

> samādhilābhaḥ samādhiphalaṃ ca bhavatīti

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [saṃvega](../terms/samvega.md) · concepts: [Grades of practitioners (mṛdu, madhya, adhimātra)](../concepts/grades-of-practitioners.md)

### 1.22 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-22"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The intense (tīvra) are themselves mildly, middlingly or supremely intense. From that distinction too: samādhi and its fruit are near to the one of mildly intense ardor, nearer to the one of middling intense ardor, and nearest to the one of supremely intense ardor whose means is also supreme. The closing lines ask whether samādhi is nearest only from this, or whether some other means exists.

> mṛdutīvro madhyatīvro 'dhimātratīvra iti /
tato 'pi viśeṣaḥ /
tadviśeṣād api mṛdutīvrasaṃvegasyāsannaḥ tato madhyatīvrasaṃvegasyāsannataraḥ, tasmād adhimātratīvrasaṃvegasyādhimātropāyasyāpy āsannatamaḥ samādhilābhaḥ samādhiphalaṃ ceti

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice_

terms: [saṃvega](../terms/samvega.md) · concepts: [Grades of practitioners (mṛdu, madhya, adhimātra)](../concepts/grades-of-practitioners.md)

### 1.23 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-23"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Through devotion (praṇidhāna), a special kind of bhakti, Īśvara, inclined [toward the yogin], favours him by mere intent thought (abhidhyāna-mātra). By that mere intent thought too, the attainment and fruit of samādhi are nearest for the yogin. The closing line asks who this Īśvara is, distinct from prakṛti and puruṣa.

> praṇidhānād bhaktiviśeṣād āvarjita īśvaras tam anugṛhṇāty abhidhyānamātreṇa /
tadabhidhyānamātrād api yogina āsannatamaḥ samādhilābhaḥ samādhiphalaṃ ca bhavatīti

_level: conventional · standpoint: devotional · path: devotion, meditation · stage: all · types: practice, ultimate_

### 1.24 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-24"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The afflictions (kleśa) are ignorance and the rest; actions (karma) are skilful and unskilful; ripening (vipāka) is their fruit; the residues (āśaya) are the tendencies (vāsanā) suited to them. These abide in the mind but are attributed to the puruṣa, since he is the enjoyer of their fruit, as victory or defeat abiding in the warriors is attributed to the master. The one untouched by this enjoyment is the special puruṣa, Īśvara. Objection: there are many liberated ones (kevalin) who have cut three bonds. But Īśvara's connection with those bonds (tat-saṃbandha) neither has been nor will be. The liberated one's earlier limit of bondage is known; not so Īśvara's. The one merged in prakṛti may have a later limit of bondage; not so Īśvara. He is ever free, ever Īśvara. His eternal excellence rests on the taking up of supreme sattva; its cause is scripture (śāstra), and scripture's cause is supreme sattva; the connection of these two is beginningless. His lordship is free from equal and greater: two equals cannot each have an object wished at once (e.g. 'let this be new' and 'let this be old'), since the objects conflict, so one's success would obstruct the other's free will. Whoever's lordship is free from equal and surpassing is Īśvara, and he is a special puruṣa.

> avidyādayaḥ kleśāḥ /
kuśalākuśalāni karmāṇi /
tatphalaṃ vipākaḥ /
tadanuguṇā vāsanā āśayāḥ /
te ca manasi vartamānāḥ puruṣe vyapadiśyante, sa hi tatphalasya bhokteti /
yathā jayaḥ parājayo vā yoddhṛṣu vartamānaḥ svāmini vyapadiśyate /
yo hy anena bhogenāparāmṛṣṭaḥ sa puruṣaviśeṣa īśvaraḥ /
kaivalyaṃ prāptās tarhi santi ca bahavaḥ kevalinaḥ /
te hi trīṇi bandhanāni cchittvā kaivalyaṃ prāptā īśvarasya ca tatsaṃbandho na bhūto na bhāvī /
yathā muktasya pūrvā bandhakoṭiḥ prajñāyate naivam īśvarasya /
yathā vā prakṛtilīnasyottarā bandhakoṭiḥ saṃbhāvyate naivam īśvarasya /
sa tu sadaiva muktaḥ sadaiveśvara iti /
yo 'sau prakṛṣṭasattvopādānād īśvarasya śāśvatika utkarṣaḥ sa kiṃ sanimitta āhosvin nirnimitta iti /
tasya śāstraṃ nimittam /
śāstraṃ punaḥ kiṃnimittam, prakṛṣṭasattvanimittam.
etayoḥ śāstrotkarṣayor īśvarasattve vartamānayor anādiḥ saṃbandhaḥ /
etasmād etad bhavati sadaiveśvaraḥ sadaiva mukta iti /
tac ca tasyaiśvaryaṃ sāmyātiśayavinirmuktam /
na tāvad aiśvaryāntareṇa tad atiśayyate /
yad evātiśayi syāt tad eva tat syāt /
tasmād yatra kāṣṭhāprāptir aiśvaryasya sa īśvara iti /
na ca tatsamānam aiśvaryam asti /
kasmāt, dvayos tulyayor ekasmin yugapatkāmite 'rthe navam idam astu purāṇam idam astv ity ekasya siddhāv itarasya prākāmyavighātād ūnatvaṃ prasaktam /
dvayoś ca tulyayor yugapatkāmitārthaprāptir nāsti /
arthasya viruddhatvāt /
tasmād yasya sāmyātiśayair vinirmuktam aiśvaryaṃ sa eveśvaraḥ /
sa ca puruṣaviśeṣa iti

_level: ultimate · standpoint: divine · path: devotion, knowledge · stage: all · types: ultimate, consciousness-mind_

### 1.25 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-25"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

This supersensible grasp (atīndriya-grahaṇa) of past, future and present things, singly and together, small or great, is the seed of omniscience; where, growing, it is unsurpassed, that one is omniscient. Since the seed of omniscience shows degrees, like measure it must reach a limit; where knowledge reaches its limit is the omniscient one, and he is a special puruṣa. Inference, once it has concluded only the general, is spent and cannot establish a particular; his name and particular features are to be sought from scripture (āgama). He has no benefit for himself, but his purpose is the benefit of beings: 'by teaching knowledge and dharma I will raise saṃsāra-bound puruṣas in the dissolutions of the aeon and the great dissolution.' And it is said: the first knower (ādividvān), the blessed supreme seer, taking up a created mind (nirmāṇa-citta) out of compassion, taught the tantra to Āsuri who wished to know.

> yad idam atītānāgatapratyutpannapratyekasamuccayātīndriyagrahaṇam alpaṃ bahv iti sarvajñabījam etad vivardhamānaṃ yatra niratiśayaṃ sa sarvajñaḥ /
asti kāṣṭhāprāptiḥ sarvajñabījasya sātiśayatvāt parimāṇavad iti /
yatra kāṣṭhāprāptir jñānasya sa sarvajñaḥ /
sa ca puruṣaviśeṣa iti /
sāmānyamātropasaṃhāre ca kṛtopakṣayam anumānaṃ na viśeṣapratipattau samartham iti /
tasya saṃjñādiviśeṣapratipattir āgamataḥ paryanveṣyā /
tasyātmānugrahābhāve 'pi bhūtānugrahaḥ prayojanam /
jñānadharmopadeśena kalpapralayamahāpralayeṣu saṃsāriṇaḥ puruṣān uddhariṣyāmīti /
tathā coktam --- ādividvān nirmāṇacittam adhiṣṭhāya kāruṇyād bhagavān paramarṣir āsuraye jijñāsamānāya tantraṃ provāceti

### 1.26 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-26"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The earlier teachers are limited by time. He, to whom time does not apply as a limit, is the teacher even of the ancients. As he is established, having reached the highest, at the beginning of this creation (sarga), so should he be understood in the creations that have passed.

> pūrve hi guravaḥ kālenāvacchidyante /
yatrāvacchedārthena kālo nopāvartate sa eṣa pūrveṣām api guruḥ /
yathāsya sargasyādau prakarṣagatyā siddhas tathātikrāntasargādiṣv api pratyetavyaḥ

_level: ultimate · standpoint: divine · path: devotion · stage: all · types: ultimate, teacher-transmission_

### 1.27 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-27"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Īśvara is what the praṇava designates (vācya). Is the relation of designated and designator made by convention (saṃketa), or does it stand like the light of a lamp? The relation stands; convention only points out an already established meaning, as the standing relation of father and son is shown by 'this is his father, this is his son'. In other creations too the convention is made in the same way, according to the power of designated and designator. The traditionalists (āgamin) hold that the relation of word and meaning is eternal, being eternal by the continuity of agreement (saṃpratipatti).

> vācya īśvaraḥ praṇavasya /
kim asya saṃketakṛtaṃ vācyavācakatvam atha pradīpaprakāśavad avasthitam iti /
sthito 'sya vācyasya vācakena saha saṃbandhaḥ /
saṃketas tv īśravasya sthitam evārtham abhinayati /
yathāvasthitaḥ pitṛputrayoḥ saṃbandhaḥ saṃketenāvadyotyate, ayam asya pitā, ayam asya putra iti /
sargāntareṣv api vācyavācakaśaktyapekṣas tathaiva saṃketaḥ kriyate /
saṃpratipattinityatayā nityaḥ śabdārthasaṃbandha ity āgaminaḥ pratijānate

_level: conventional · standpoint: divine · path: sound, devotion · stage: all · types: sound-language, ultimate_

### 1.28 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-28"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The repetition (japa) of the praṇava and the contemplation (bhāvana) of Īśvara, whom the praṇava denotes. For the yogin who repeats the praṇava and contemplates its meaning, the mind becomes one-pointed (ekāgra). And it is said [in a quoted verse]: 'From self-study (svādhyāya) let one take up yoga; from yoga let one bring self-study to mind; by the perfection of self-study and yoga the supreme self (paramātman) shines forth.'

> praṇavasya japaḥ praṇavābhidheyasya ceśvarasya bhāvanam /
tad asya yoginaḥ praṇavaṃ japataḥ praṇavārthaṃ ca bhāvayataś cittam ekāgraṃ saṃpadyate /
tathā coktam ---
"svādhyāyād yogam āsīta yogāt svādhyāyam āmanet /
svādhyāyayogasaṃpattyā paramātmā prakāśate

### 1.29 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-29"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The obstacles (antarāya), illness and the rest, do not arise through devotion to Īśvara. For him also there is sight of his own form (svarūpa-darśana): as Īśvara is a puruṣa pure, serene, alone (kevala), untouched by affliction, so this yogin also reaches the puruṣa who is the witness of the buddhi (buddheḥ pratisaṃvedī).

> ye tāvad antarāyā vyādhiprabhṛtayas te tāvad īśvarapraṇidhānān na bhavanti /
svarūpadarśanam apy asya bhavati /
yathaiveśvaraḥ puruṣaḥ śuddhaḥ prasannaḥ kevalo 'nupasargas tathāyam api buddheḥ pratisaṃvedī yaḥ puruṣas tam adhigacchati

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: devotion, meditation · stage: all · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [antarāya](../terms/antaraya.md), [vyādhi](../terms/vyadhi.md), [svarūpa-darśana](../terms/svarupa-darsana.md), [pratisaṃvedin](../terms/pratisamvedin.md), [puruṣa](../terms/purusa.md), [īśvara-praṇidhāna](../terms/isvara-pranidhana.md) · concepts: [Īśvara (the Lord)](../concepts/isvara.md), [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md), [The witness (the seer as pratisaṃvedin)](../concepts/witness.md) · practices: [Devotion to Īśvara (īśvara-praṇidhāna)](../practices/isvara-pranidhana.md) · obstacles: [The nine obstacles (antarāya), distractions of the mind](../obstacles/nine-antarayas.md)

### 1.30 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-30"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The nine obstacles are distractions (vikṣepa) of the mind. They occur together with the mind's activities (citta-vṛtti); when they are absent, the activities spoken of before do not occur. Illness (vyādhi) is disorder (vaiṣamya) of the bodily constituents (dhātu), the humours (rasa) and the organs (karaṇa). Dullness (styāna) is unfitness of the mind for work (akarmaṇyatā). Doubt (saṃśaya) is cognition touching both alternatives: 'this may be so, it may not be so'. Heedlessness (pramāda) is not cultivating the means of samādhi. Laziness (ālasya) is non-engagement (apravṛtti) due to heaviness (gurutva) of the body and of the mind. Non-abstention (avirati) is greed (gardha) of the mind, which consists in contact with sense-objects. Erroneous vision (bhrānti-darśana) is knowledge that is error (viparyaya). Failure to gain a stage (alabdha-bhūmikatva) is non-attainment of a ground (bhūmi) of samādhi. Instability (anavasthitatva) is the mind's not being established on a ground that has been gained; for when samādhi has been attained it would remain established. These distractions of the mind are called the nine taints of yoga (yoga-mala), enemies of yoga (yoga-pratipakṣa) and obstacles to yoga (yogāntarāya).

> navāntarāyāś cittasya vikṣepāḥ /
sahaite cittavṛttibhir bhavanti /
eteṣām abhāve na bhavanti pūrvoktāś cittavṛttayaḥ /
vyādhir dhāturasakaraṇavaiṣamyam /
styānam akarmaṇyatā cittasya /
saṃśaya ubhayakoṭispṛg vijñānaṃ syād idam evaṃ naivaṃ syād iti /
pramādaḥ samādhisādhanānām abhāvanam /
ālasyaṃ kāyasya cittasya ca gurutvād apravṛttiḥ /
aviratiś cittasya viṣayasaṃprayogātmā gardhaḥ /
bhrāntidarśanaṃ viparyayajñānam /
alabdhabhūmikatvaṃ samādhibhūmer alābhaḥ /
anavasthitatvaṃ yal labdhāyāṃ bhūmau cittasyāpratiṣṭhā /
samādhipratilambhe hi sati tadavasthitaṃ syād iti /
ete cittavikṣepā nava yogamalā yogapratipakṣā yogāntarāyā ity abhidhīyante

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [antarāya](../terms/antaraya.md), [citta-vikṣepa](../terms/citta-viksepa.md), [yoga-mala](../terms/yoga-mala.md), [vyādhi](../terms/vyadhi.md), [styāna](../terms/styana.md), [saṃśaya](../terms/samsaya.md), [pramāda](../terms/pramada.md), [ālasya](../terms/alasya.md), [avirati](../terms/avirati.md), [bhrānti-darśana](../terms/bhranti-darsana.md), [alabdha-bhūmikatva](../terms/alabdha-bhumikatva.md), [anavasthitatva](../terms/anavasthitatva.md) · concepts: [The five activities of the mind (pañca vṛttayaḥ)](../concepts/five-vrttis.md) · obstacles: [The nine obstacles (antarāya), distractions of the mind](../obstacles/nine-antarayas.md), [Illness (vyādhi) as a distraction](../obstacles/vyadhi.md), [Dullness (styāna)](../obstacles/styana.md), [Doubt (saṃśaya)](../obstacles/samsaya.md), [Heedlessness (pramāda)](../obstacles/pramada.md), [Laziness (ālasya)](../obstacles/alasya.md), [Non-abstention (avirati)](../obstacles/avirati.md), [Erroneous vision (bhrānti-darśana)](../obstacles/bhranti-darsana.md), [Failure to gain a stage (alabdha-bhūmikatva)](../obstacles/alabdha-bhumikatva.md), [Instability (anavasthitatva)](../obstacles/anavasthitatva.md)

### 1.31 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-31"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Pain (duḥkha) is of three kinds: relating to oneself (ādhyātmika), to other beings (ādhibhautika), and to the divine (ādhidaivika); it is that by which beings are struck and strive to strike it down. Dejection (daurmanasya) is a disturbance (kṣobha) of the mind through the frustration of a wish. Trembling of the limbs (aṅgamejayatva) is that which shakes the limbs. Inhalation (śvāsa) is when the vital breath (prāṇa) takes in outer air; exhalation (praśvāsa) is when it expels air from the belly. These are companions of distraction: they occur in one whose mind is distracted (vikṣipta); they do not occur in one whose mind is concentrated (samāhita).

> duḥkham ādhyātmikam ādhibhautikam ādhidaivikaṃ ca /
yenābhihatāḥ prāṇinas tadapaghātāya prayatante tad duḥkham /
daurmanasyam icchāvighātāc cetasaḥ kṣobhaḥ /
yad aṅgāny ejayati kampayati tad aṅgamejayatvam /
prāṇo yad bāhyaṃ vāyum ācāmati sa śvāsaḥ /
yat kauṣṭhyaṃ vāyuṃ niḥsārayati sa praśvāsaḥ /
ete vikṣepasahabhuvo vikṣiptacittasyaite bhavanti /
samāhitacittasyaite na bhavanti

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice, consciousness-mind, powers-experiences, body-layers_

terms: [duḥkha](../terms/duhkha.md), [ādhyātmika](../terms/adhyatmika.md), [ādhibhautika](../terms/adhibhautika.md), [ādhidaivika](../terms/adhidaivika.md), [daurmanasya](../terms/daurmanasya.md), [aṅgamejayatva](../terms/angamejayatva.md), [śvāsa](../terms/svasa.md), [praśvāsa](../terms/prasvasa.md), [vikṣipta](../terms/viksipta.md), [samāhita](../terms/samahita.md), [śvāsa-praśvāsa](../terms/svasa-prasvasa.md) · concepts: [The three kinds of pain (duḥkha) named in YBh 1.31](../concepts/three-kinds-of-duhkha.md) · obstacles: [The five accompaniments of distraction (vikṣepa-sahabhuva)](../obstacles/viksepa-sahabhuva.md)

### 1.32 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-32"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

To remove distraction, one should practise with the mind resting on a single principle (eka-tattva). The bhāṣya then argues against the view that the mind is only a cognition fixed to one object each and momentary (pratyartha-niyata, kṣaṇika): for such a view every mind would be one-pointed and none distracted. If cognitions were different by nature and unconnected by one mind, how could one remember what another saw, or one be the enjoyer of karma-store built by another? The cognition 'I' (aham) — 'what I saw I now touch; what I touched I now see' — stands as one across the different cognitions, is grasped by one's own experience, and the power of perception is not overcome by another means of knowing. Hence the mind is one and has many objects, and it persists.

> vikṣepapratiṣedhārtham ekatattvāvalambanaṃ cittam abhyaset /
yasya tu pratyarthaniyataṃ pratyayamātraṃ kṣaṇikaṃ ca cittaṃ tasya sarvam eva cittam ekāgraṃ nāsty eva vikṣiptam /
yadi punar idaṃ sarvataḥ pratyāhṛtyaikasminn arthe samādhīyate tadā bhavaty ekāgram ity ato na pratyarthaniyatam /
yo 'pi sadṛśapratyayapravāhena cittam ekāgraṃ manyate tasyaikāgratā yadi pravāhacittasya dharmas tadaikaṃ nāsti pravāhacittaṃ kṣaṇikatvāt /
atha pravāhāṃśasyaiva pratyayasya dharmaḥ, sa sarvaḥ sadṛśapratyayapravāhī vā visadṛśapratyayapravāhī vā pratyarthaniyatatvād ekāgra eveti vikṣiptacittānupapattiḥ /
tasmād ekam anekārtham avasthitaṃ cittam iti /
yadi ca cittenaikenānanvitāḥ svabhāvabhinnāḥ pratyayā jāyerann atha katham anyapratyayadṛṣṭasyānyaḥ smartā bhavet /
anyapratyayopacitasya ca karmāśayasyānyaḥ pratyaya upabhoktā bhavet /
kathaṃcit samādhīyamānam apy etad gomayapāyasīyanyāyam ākṣipati /
kiṃ ca svātmānubhavāpahnavaś cittasyānyatve prāpnoti /
katham, yad aham adrākṣaṃ tat spṛśāmi yac cāsprākṣaṃ tat paśyāmīty aham iti pratyayaḥ sarvasya pratyayasya bhede sati pratyayiny abhedenopasthitaḥ /
ekapratyayaviṣayo 'yam abhedātmāham iti pratyayaḥ katham atyantabhinneṣu citteṣu vartamānaḥ sāmānyam ekaṃ pratyayinam āśrayet /
svānubhavagrāhyaś cāyam abhedātmāham iti pratyayaḥ /
na ca pratyakṣasya māhātmyaṃ pramāṇāntareṇābhibhūyate /
pramāṇāntaraṃ ca pratyakṣabalenaiva vyavahāraṃ labhate /
tasmād ekam anekārtham avasthitaṃ ca cittam

### 1.33 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-33"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Toward all beings who have come to enjoy happiness one should cultivate friendliness (maitrī); toward the suffering, compassion (karuṇā); toward the virtuous, gladness (muditā); toward those of non-virtuous conduct, equanimity (upekṣā). In one who cultivates thus, a pure (śukla) dharma arises; from that the mind becomes clear (prasīdati); the clear mind attains a footing of stability (sthiti-pada) as one-pointed.

> tatra sarvaprāṇiṣu sukhasaṃbhogāpanneṣu maitrīṃ bhāvayet /
duḥkhiteṣu karuṇām /
puṇyātmakeṣu muditām /
apuṇyaśīleṣūpekṣām /
evam asya bhāvayataḥ śuklo dharma upajāyate /
tataś ca cittaṃ prasīdati /
prasannam ekāgraṃ sthitipadaṃ labhate

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: all · types: practice, ethics, consciousness-mind_

### 1.34 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-34"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

Pracchardana is the expulsion (vamana) of the air in the belly (kauṣṭhya) through the two nostrils by a special effort; vidhāraṇa is prāṇāyāma. By these two one may bring about the steadiness (sthiti) of the mind.

> kauṣṭhyasya vāyor nāsikāpuṭābhyāṃ prayatnaviśeṣād vamanaṃ pracchardanam, vidhāraṇaṃ prāṇāyāmas tābhyāṃ vā manasaḥ sthitiṃ saṃpādayet

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: all · types: practice, body-layers_

### 1.35 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-35"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

For one who holds [the mind] at the tip of the nose, the awareness of divine smell is 'the activity of smell' (gandha-pravṛtti); at the tip of the tongue, awareness of taste; at the palate, awareness of form (colour); in the middle of the tongue, awareness of touch; at the root of the tongue, awareness of sound. These activities, when they arise, bind the mind to steadiness, dispel doubt (saṃśaya), and become a doorway (dvāra) to the insight of samādhi. The same is to be understood of activities that arise about the moon, sun, planets, jewels, lamps and rays, which are also concerned with an object. What is learned from scripture, inference and a teacher's instruction is true, yet as long as even one part is not known by one's own experience, all remains as if beyond sight and does not give firm conviction about subtle matters such as liberation (apavarga). So, to reinforce scripture, inference and teacher, some particular thing must certainly be made directly perceived; when one part of what was taught is directly known, all the subtle matters, up to liberation, are trusted. This mind-preparation (citta-parikarma) is taught for this. With the consciousness of mastery (vaśīkāra-saṃjñā) arisen in the varied activities, the mind becomes able to make each thing directly perceived; and then faith, energy, mindfulness and samādhi will arise unobstructed.

> nāsikāgre dhārayato 'sya yā divyagandhasaṃvit sā gandhapravṛttiḥ /
jihvāgre rasasaṃvit /
tāluni rūpasaṃvit /
jihvāmadhye sparśasaṃvit /
jihvāmūle śabdasaṃvid ity etā vṛttaya utpannāś cittaṃ sthitau nibadhnanti, saṃśayaṃ vidhamanti, samādhiprajñāyāṃ ca dvārībhavantīti /
etena candrādityagrahamaṇipradīparaśmyādiṣu pravṛttir utpannā viṣayavaty eva veditavyā yady api hi tattacchāstrānumānācāryopadeśair avagatam arthatattvaṃ sadbhūtam eva bhavati /
eteṣāṃ yathābhūtārthapratipādanasāmarthyāt, tathāpi yāvad ekadeśo 'pi kaścin na svakaraṇasaṃvedyo bhavati tāvat sarvaṃ parokṣam ivāpavargādiṣu sūkṣmeṣv artheṣu na dṛḍhāṃ buddhim utpādayati /
tasmāc chāstrānumānācāryopadeśopodbalanārtham evāvaśyaṃ kaścid arthaviśeṣaḥ pratyakṣīkartavyaḥ /
tatra tadupadiṣṭārthaikadeśapratyakṣatve sati sarvaṃ sūkṣmaviṣayam api āpavargāc chraddhīyate /
etadartham evedaṃ cittaparikarma nirdiśyate /
aniyatāsu vṛttiṣu tadviṣayāyāṃ vaśīkārasaṃjñāyām upajātāyāṃ samarthaṃ syāt tasya tasyārthasya pratyakṣīkaraṇāyeti /
tathā ca sati śraddhāvīryasmṛtisamādhayo 'syāpratibandhena bhaviṣyantīti

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice, powers-experiences_

terms: [viṣayavatī pravṛtti](../terms/visayavati-pravrtti.md), [sthiti](../terms/sthiti.md), [saṃśaya](../terms/samsaya.md), [vaśīkāra-saṃjñā](../terms/vasikara-samjna.md), [parikarma](../terms/parikarma.md), [apavarga](../terms/apavarga.md) · concepts: [Coalescence (samāpatti)](../concepts/samapatti.md), [The five means: faith, energy, mindfulness, samādhi, insight](../concepts/sraddha-virya-smrti-samadhi-prajna.md), [Bodily places named for saṃyama and concentration](../concepts/bodily-loci-of-samyama.md) · practices: [Concentration giving rise to subtle sense-perception (YS 1.35)](../practices/visayavati-pravrtti.md)

### 1.36 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-36"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

For one who holds [the mind] in the lotus of the heart, the awareness of the buddhi arises. The sattva of the buddhi is shining (bhāsvara) and akin to space (ākāśa); through skill (vaiśāradya) in steadiness there, the activity is shaped (vikalpate) in the form of the light of the sun, moon, planets or a jewel. And the mind that has entered coalescence (samāpanna) in I-am-ness (asmitā) becomes like a great ocean without waves: tranquil (śānta), endless (ananta), I-am-ness only (asmitā-mātra). Of this it is said: 'having known that self of the size of an atom (aṇu-mātra), one knows fully "I am".' This twofold sorrowless (viśokā) activity, the one concerned with an object (viṣayavatī) and the one that is I-am-ness only (asmitā-mātrā), is called luminous (jyotiṣmatī). By it the yogin's mind gains a footing of steadiness.

> hṛdayapuṇḍarīke dhārayato yā buddhisaṃvit, buddhisattvaṃ hi bhāsvaram ākāśakalpaṃ, tatra sthitivaiśāradyāt pravṛttiḥ sūryendugrahamaṇiprabhārūpākāreṇa vikalpate /
tathāsmitāyāṃ samāpannaṃ cittaṃ nistaraṅgamahodadhikalpaṃ śāntam anantam asmitāmātraṃ bhavati /
yatredam uktam --- "tam aṇumātram ātmānam anuvidyāsmīty evaṃ tāvat saṃprajānīte" iti /
eṣā dvayī viśokā viṣayavatī, asmitāmātrā ca pravṛttir jyotiṣmatīty ucyate /
yayā yoginaś cittaṃ sthitipadaṃ labhata iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice, consciousness-mind, powers-experiences_

### 1.40 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-40"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

For one entering the subtle, the mind's footing extends up to the atom; for one entering the gross, up to the greatest magnitude. The unobstructedness (apratīghāta) of one who runs to both limits is the supreme mastery (para vaśīkāra). By that mastery the yogin's mind is complete (paripūrṇa) and no longer needs further preparation (parikarma) made by practice. The closing lines lead to the next question: what is the nature and object of coalescence (samāpatti) for a mind that has gained steadiness.

> sūkṣme niviśamānasya paramāṇvantaṃ sthitipadaṃ labhata iti /
sthūle niviśamānasya paramamahattvāntaṃ sthitipadaṃ cittasya /
evaṃ tām ubhayīṃ koṭim anudhāvato yo 'syāpratīghātaḥ sa paro vaśīkāraḥ /
tadvaśīkārāt paripūrṇaṃ yoginaś cittaṃ na punar abhyāsakṛtaṃ parikarmāpekṣata iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced (para-vaśīkāra) · types: practice, powers-experiences_

terms: [vaśīkāra](../terms/vasikara.md), [paramāṇu](../terms/paramanu.md), [parama-mahattva](../terms/parama-mahattva.md), [apratighāta](../terms/apratighata.md), [parikarma](../terms/parikarma.md), [samāpatti](../terms/samapatti.md) · concepts: [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md), [Coalescence (samāpatti)](../concepts/samapatti.md)

### 1.41 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-41"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

'Of one whose activities have dwindled' means one whose cognitions have set (pratyastamita-pratyaya). 'Like a flawless crystal' is an example: as a crystal, according to what it rests on, shines with the form of that support, so the mind coloured by a graspable support appears in the form of that graspable thing. Coloured by the subtle elements, it appears as the form of the subtle elements; by a gross support, in gross form; by the varieties of the world, in the form of the world. The same is to be seen with the instruments of grasping (the sense faculties): coloured by them, it appears in their form. Likewise coloured by the puruṣa as knower it appears in his form; and coloured by the liberated puruṣa (mukta-puruṣa) it appears in the form of the liberated puruṣa. So the mind, like a flawless crystal, in respect of knower, instrument and known, has the taking on of the form of what it stands in; this is called coalescence (samāpatti).

> kṣīṇavṛtter iti pratyastamitapratyayasyety arthaḥ /
abhijātasyeva maṇer iti dṛṣṭāntopādānam /
yathā sphaṭika upāśrayabhedāt tattadrūpoparakta upāśrayarūpākāreṇa nirbhāsate tathā grāhyālambanoparaktaṃ cittaṃ grāhyasamāpannaṃ grāhyasvarūpākāreṇa nirbhāsate /
bhūtasūkṣmoparaktaṃ bhūtasūkṣmasamāpannaṃ bhūtasūkṣmasvarūpābhāsaṃ bhavati /
tathā sthūlālambanoparaktaṃ sthūlarūpasamāpannaṃ sthūlarūpābhāsaṃ bhavati /
tathā viśvabhedoparaktaṃ viśvabhedasamāpannaṃ viśvarūpābhāsaṃ bhavati /
tathā grahaṇeṣv apīndriyeṣv api draṣṭavyam /
grahaṇālambanoparaktaṃ grahaṇasamāpannaṃ grahaṇasvarūpākāreṇa nirbhāsate /
tathā grahītṛpuruṣālambanoparaktaṃ grahītṛpuruṣasamāpannaṃ grahītṛpuruṣasvarūpākāreṇa nirbhāsate /
tathā muktapuruṣālambanoparaktaṃ muktapuruṣasamāpannaṃ muktapuruṣasvarūpākāreṇa nirbhāsata iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [samāpatti](../terms/samapatti.md), [kṣīṇa-vṛtti](../terms/ksina-vrtti.md), [abhijāta-maṇi](../terms/abhijata-mani.md), [uparakta](../terms/uparakta.md), [grahītṛ](../terms/grahitr.md), [grahaṇa](../terms/grahana.md), [grāhya](../terms/grahya.md), [bhūta-sūkṣma](../terms/bhuta-suksma.md) · concepts: [Coalescence (samāpatti)](../concepts/samapatti.md), [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md), [The seer and the seen (draṣṭṛ and dṛśya)](../concepts/seer-and-seen.md) · disputes: [What are the objects of the bliss (ānanda) and I-am-ness (asmitā) forms of samprajñāta samādhi, and who are the videhas and prakṛtilayas?](../debates/objects-of-samprajnata.md)

### 1.42 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-42"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Example: 'cow' as word, 'cow' as object, 'cow' as cognition — though distinct, they are seen taken together without division; and when divided, the properties of the word are one thing, of the object another, of the cognition another: their paths are separate. When the object (a cow, etc.) has risen into the samādhi-insight (samādhi-prajñā) of a yogin in coalescence, if it stands mixed with the conceptual constructions of word, object and cognition, that is the mixed (saṃkīrṇa) coalescence called savitarkā. But when, on the purification of the memory of word-convention, the samādhi-insight is empty of the constructions of scriptural and inferential knowledge, and the object stands only in its own form, defined only by the form of that form, that is nirvitarkā coalescence. It is the supreme perception (pratyakṣa), and the seed of testimony and inference; from it testimony and inference arise; and that seeing does not go along with knowledge from testimony and inference. So the seeing born of nirvitarka samādhi in the yogin is unmixed (asaṃkīrṇa) with any other means of knowing.

> tadyathā gaur iti śabdo gaur ity artho gaur iti jñānam ity avibhāgena vibhaktānām api grahaṇaṃ dṛṣṭam /
vibhajyamānāś cānye śabdadharmā anye 'rthadharmā anye vijñānadharmā ity eteṣāṃ vibhaktaḥ panthāḥ /
tatra samāpannasya yogino yo gavādyarthaḥ samādhiprajñāyāṃ samārūḍhaḥ sa cec chabdārthajñānavikalpānuviddha upāvartate sā saṃkīrṇā samāpattiḥ savitarkety ucyate. 1.42
yadā punaḥ śabdasaṃketasmṛtipariśuddhau śrutānumānajñānavikalpaśūnyāyāṃ samādhiprajñāyāṃ svarūpamātreṇāvasthito 'rthas tatsvarūpākāramātratayaivāvacchidyate /
sā ca nirvitarkā samāpattiḥ /
tat paraṃ pratyakṣam /
tac ca śrutānumānayor bījam /
tataḥ śrutānumāne prabhavataḥ /
na ca śrutānumānajñānasahabhūtaṃ tad darśanam /
tasmād asaṃkīrṇaṃ praṃmāṇāntareṇa yogino nirvitarkasamādhijaṃ darśanam iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, sound-language_

terms: [savitarkā](../terms/savitarka.md), [nirvitarkā](../terms/nirvitarka.md), [samāpatti](../terms/samapatti.md), [śabda-saṃketa](../terms/sabda-sanketa.md), [samādhi-prajñā](../terms/samadhi-prajna.md), [pratyakṣa](../terms/pratyaksa.md), [śruta](../terms/sruta.md), [anumāna](../terms/anumana.md), [vikalpa](../terms/vikalpa.md) · concepts: [Coalescence (samāpatti)](../concepts/samapatti.md), [Samādhi with full awareness (samprajñāta)](../concepts/samprajnata-samadhi.md), [Word, meaning and cognition](../concepts/word-meaning-cognition.md), [Means of valid knowledge (pramāṇa)](../concepts/pramana.md)

### 1.43 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-43"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence low

When the memory of word-convention, of scriptural and of inferential knowledge and of constructions is purified, the insight (prajñā) that was coloured by the form of the grasped, leaving aside as it were its own grasping form, becomes as if it had the form of the thing-meaning alone; it is then nirvitarkā coalescence. As was explained: its object is a whole (avayavin) — the 'world', a cow or a pot, of the form of a particular aggregation of atoms, grasped in one act of understanding; it appears as a shared property of the subtle elements, inferred from its manifest fruit, coloured by what manifests it, and disappears when another property (e.g. potsherds) arises. This property is called the whole. It is one, great or very small, touchable, active and impermanent; ordinary dealings are carried out with it. If the aggregation were a non-thing, then, the subtle cause not being perceived and there being no whole, almost everything would be false knowledge (mithyā-jñāna), 'established in a form not its own'; then even right knowledge would have no object. Whatever is perceived is taught as whole; so the whole exists, which has come to ordinary dealings about greatness and the like and is the object of nirvitarkā coalescence.

> yā śabdasaṃketaśrutānumānajñānavikalpasmṛtipariśuddhau grāhyasvarūpa.uparaktā prajñā svam iva prajñāsvarūpaṃ grahaṇātmakaṃ tyaktvā padārthamātrasvarūpā grāhyasvarūpāpanneva bhavati sā tadā nirvitarkā samāpattiḥ /
tathā ca vyākyātaṃ tasyā ekabuddhyupakramo hy arthātmāṇupracayaviśeṣātmā gavādir ghaṭādir vā lokaḥ /
sa ca saṃsthānaviśeṣo bhūtasūkṣmāṇāṃ sādhāraṇo dharma ātmabhūtaḥ phalena vyaktenānumitaḥ svavyañjakāñjanaḥ prādurbhavati /
dharmāntarasya kapālāder udaye ca tirobhavati /
sa eṣa dharmo 'vayavīty ucyate /
yo 'sāv ekaś ca mahāṃś cāṇīṃyaś ca sparśavāṃś ca kriyādharmakaś cānityaś ca tenāvayavinā vyavahārāḥ kriyante /
yasya punar avastukaḥ sa pracayaviśeṣaḥ /
sūkṣmaṃ ca kāraṇam anupalabhyam avikalpasya tasyāvayavyabhāvād atadrūpapratiṣṭhaṃ mithyājñānam iti prāyeṇa sarvam eva prāptaṃ mithyājñānam iti /
tadā ca samyagjñānam api kiṃ syād viṣayābhāvāt /
yad yad upalabhyate tat tad avayavitvenāmnātam /
tasmād asty avayavī yo mahattvādivyavahārāpannaḥ samāpatter nirvitarkāyā viṣayī bhavati

_level: conventional · standpoint: polemical · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, dispute_

(YBh 1.43)](../debates/the-whole-and-its-parts.md)

### 1.44 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-44"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Coalescence on the subtle elements (bhūta-sūkṣma) whose properties are manifest, limited by place, time and cause-experience, is called savicārā. Even there, the subtle element endowed with a risen property, graspable by one understanding, stands as support in the samādhi-insight. Coalescence on those [subtle elements] that are in every way and on every side not limited by quiescent, risen or unspecifiable properties (śānta-udita-avyapadeśya-dharma-anavacchinna), which follow all properties and have all properties as their nature, is called nirvicārā: the subtle element stands as support just in its own form, colouring the samādhi-insight; and when the insight is as if empty of its own form, with the object alone, it is called nirvicārā. Thus, savitarkā and nirvitarkā have a gross object; savicārā and nirvicārā a subtle object. In this way, the giving up of construction (vikalpa) in both is explained by the nirvitarkā.

> tatra bhūtasūkṣmakeṣv abhivyaktadharmakeṣu deśakālanimittānubhavāvacchinneṣu yā samāpattiḥ sā savicārety ucyate /
tatrāpy ekabuddhinirgrāhyam evoditadharmaviśiṣṭaṃ bhūtasūkṣmam ālambanībhūtaṃ samādhiprajñāyām upatiṣṭhate /
yā punaḥ sarvathā sarvataḥ śāntoditāvyapadeśyadharmānavacchinneṣu sarvadharmānupātiṣu sarvadharmātmakeṣu samāpattiḥ sā nirvicārety ucyate /
evaṃsvarūpaṃ hi tadbhūtasūkṣmam etenaiva svarūpeṇālambanībhūtam eva samādhiprajñāsvarūpam uparañjayati /
prajñā ca svarūpaśūnyevārthamātrā yadā bhavati tadā nirvicārety ucyate /
tatra mahadvastuviṣayā savitarkā nirvitarkā ca, sūkṣmavastuviṣayā savicārā nirvicārā ca /
evam ubhayor etayaiva nirvitarkayā vikalpahānir vyākhyāteti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [savicārā](../terms/savicara.md), [nirvicārā](../terms/nirvicara.md), [bhūta-sūkṣma](../terms/bhuta-suksma.md), [samādhi-prajñā](../terms/samadhi-prajna.md) · concepts: [Coalescence (samāpatti)](../concepts/samapatti.md), [Samādhi with full awareness (samprajñāta)](../concepts/samprajnata-samadhi.md)

### 1.45 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-45"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The subtle object of an atom of earth is the smell-tanmātra; of water, the taste-tanmātra; of fire, the form-tanmātra; of air, the touch-tanmātra; of ether, the sound-tanmātra. Of these, the subtle object is the ego-sense (ahaṃkāra); of that, the mere mark (liṅga-mātra); of the mere mark, the unmarked (aliṅga). There is nothing subtler beyond the unmarked. Objection: the puruṣa is subtle. Granted; but the puruṣa is not subtler than the marked in the way the unmarked is subtler than the mark: the puruṣa is not the material cause (anvayi-kāraṇa) of the mark, but only a cause (hetu). So the subtlety in the primal (pradhāna) is explained as unsurpassed.

> pārthivasyāṇor gandhatanmātraṃ sūkṣmo viṣayaḥ /
āpyasya rasatanmātram /
taijasasya rūpatanmātram /
vāyavīyasya sparśatanmātram /
ākāśasya śabdatanmātram iti /
teṣām ahaṃkāraḥ /
asyāpi liṅgamātraṃ sūkṣmo viṣayaḥ /
liṅgamātrasyāpy aliṅgaṃ sūkṣmo viṣayaḥ /
na cāliṅgāt paraṃ sūkṣmam asti /
nanv asti puruṣaḥ sūkṣma iti satyam /
yathā liṅgāt param aliṅgasya saukṣmyaṃ na caivaṃ puruṣasya /
kiṃtu, liṅgasyānvayikāraṇaṃ puruṣo na bhavati, hetus tu bhavatīti /
ataḥ pradhāne saukṣmyaṃ niratiśayaṃ vyākhyātam

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate, world-fate_

terms: [sūkṣma-viṣayatva](../terms/suksma-visayatva.md), [aliṅga](../terms/alinga.md), [liṅga-mātra](../terms/linga-matra.md), [ahaṃkāra](../terms/ahamkara.md), [tanmātra](../terms/tanmatra.md), [pradhāna](../terms/pradhana.md), [anvayi-kāraṇa](../terms/anvayi-karana.md), [hetu](../terms/hetu.md), [asmitā](../terms/asmita.md) · concepts: [Prakṛti (primordial nature)](../concepts/prakrti.md), [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md), [Coalescence (samāpatti)](../concepts/samapatti.md), [The levels of the guṇas (guṇa-parvan)](../concepts/guna-parvan.md)

### 1.46 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-46"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Those four coalescences have an outer thing as seed (bahir-vastu-bīja), so the samādhi too is with seed. There, on a gross object, it is savitarka or nirvitarka; on a subtle object, savicāra or nirvicāra: samādhi is thus counted as fourfold.

> tāś catasraḥ samāpattayo bahirvastubījā iti samādhir api sabījaḥ /
tatra sthūle 'rthe savitarko nirvitarkaḥ, sūkṣme 'rthe savicāro nirvicāra iti caturdhopasaṃkhyātaḥ samādhir iti

### 1.47 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-47"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Lucidity (vaiśāradya) is the clear, steady flow (sthiti-pravāha) of the luminous buddhi-sattva, freed from the taint of impurity and covering, not overcome by rajas and tamas. When this lucidity of nirvicāra samādhi arises, the yogin has inner clarity (adhyātma-prasāda): the light of insight (prajñāloka), concerned with things as they are, not following sequence, distinct. And it is said: 'Having climbed the terrace of insight, the wise one, free from sorrow, looks on the sorrowing people, as one on a mountain looks on those standing on the ground.'

> aśuddhyāvaraṇamalāpetasya prakāśātmano buddhisattvasya rajastamobhyām anabhibhūtaḥ svacchaḥ sthitipravāho vaiśāradyam /
yadā nirvicārasya samādher vaiśāradyam idaṃ jāyate tadā yogino bhavaty adhyātmaprasādo bhūtārthaviṣayaḥ kramānanurodhī sphuṭaḥ prajñālokaḥ /
tathā coktam --- "prajñāprasādam āruhya aśocyaḥ śocato janān / bhūmiṣṭhān iva śailasthaḥ sarvān prājño 'nupaśyati

### 1.48 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-48"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The insight that arises in one whose mind is concentrated there is named 'truth-bearing' (ṛtambharā). The name fits its meaning: it bears only truth (satyam eva bibharti), and in it there is not even a whiff (gandha) of erroneous knowledge (viparyāsa-jñāna). And it is said: 'By scripture, by inference, and by the relish of the practice of meditation — forming insight in these three ways one obtains the highest yoga.'

> tasmin samāhitacittasya yā prajñā jāyate tasyā ṛtaṃbhareti saṃjñā bhavati /
anvarthā ca sā, satyam eva bibharti na ca tatra viparyāsajñānagandho 'py astīti /
tathā coktam --- "āgamenānumānena dhyānābhyāsarasena ca / tridhā prakalpayan prajñāṃ labhate yogam uttamam

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind_
