### 1.49 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-49"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Śruta is knowledge from scripture (āgama), and its object is the general (sāmānya): a particular cannot be stated by scripture, since a word has no convention laid down for a particular. Inference too has only the general as object: it is said that where there is arrival, there is going; and inference concludes in a general way. So no particular is the object of testimony and inference. This particular, subtle, hidden or far away, is not grasped by ordinary perception either; nor is its non-existence established, since there is no proof of that. So that particular is grasped only by the samādhi-insight, whether in the subtle elements or in the puruṣa. Hence this insight has an object other than that of testimony and inference, because its purport is a particular.

> śrutam āgamavijñānaṃ tat sāmānyaviṣayam /
na hy āgamena śakyo viśeṣo 'bhidhātum, kasmāt, na hi viśeṣeṇa kṛtasaṃketaḥ śabda iti /
tathānumānaṃ sāmānyaviṣayam eva /
yatra prāptis tatra gatir yatrāprāptis tatra na bhavati gatir ity uktam /
anumānena ca sāmānyenopasaṃhāraḥ /
tasmāc chrutānumānaviṣayo na viśeṣaḥ kaścid astīti /
na cāsya sūkṣmavyavahitaviprakṛṣṭasya vastuno lokapratyakṣeṇa grahaṇam asti /
na cāsya viśeṣasyāpramāṇakasyābhāvo 'stīti samādhiprajñānirgrāhya eva sa viśeṣo bhavati bhūtasūkṣmagato vā puruṣagato vā /
tasmāc chrutānumānaprajñābhyām anyaviṣayā sā prajñā viśeṣārthatvād iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [śruta](../terms/sruta.md), [āgama](../terms/agama.md), [anumāna](../terms/anumana.md), [sāmānya](../terms/samanya.md), [viśeṣa](../terms/visesa.md), [samādhi-prajñā](../terms/samadhi-prajna.md) · concepts: [Truth-bearing insight (ṛtambharā prajñā)](../concepts/rtambhara-prajna.md), [Means of valid knowledge (pramāṇa)](../concepts/pramana.md)

### 1.50 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-50"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The impression (saṃskāra) born of samādhi-insight obstructs the store of impressions of emergence (vyutthāna-saṃskāra-āśaya). With the overcoming of the impressions of emergence, the cognitions born of them do not arise. With the stilling of cognitions, samādhi is present; from it, insight (prajñā); from that, impressions made by insight: so a new store of impressions arises again and again, and from it insight, and from that impressions. How will this excess of impressions not give the mind an office (adhikāra) again? Those impressions made by insight do not give the mind a further office, because they cause the wearing away of the afflictions (kleśa-kṣaya); for they make the mind sink from its own work, since the mind's whole striving ends in discernment (khyāti-paryavasāna).

> samādhiprajñāprabhavaḥ saṃskāro vyutthānasaṃskārāśayaṃ bādhate /
vyutthānasaṃskārābhibhavāt tatprabhavāḥ pratyayā na bhavanti /
pratyayanirodhe samādhir upatiṣṭhate /
tataḥ samādhijā prajñā, tataḥ prajñākṛtāḥ saṃskārā iti navo navaḥ saṃskārāśayo jāyate /
tataś ca prajñā, tataś ca saṃskārā iti /
katham asau saṃskārātiśayaś cittaṃ sādhikāraṃ na kariṣyatīti /
na te prajñākṛtāḥ saṃskārāḥ kleśakṣayahetutvāc cittam adhikāraviśiṣṭaṃ kurvanti /
cittaṃ hi te svakāryād avasādayanti /
khyātiparyavasānaṃ hi cittaceṣṭitam iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

terms: [saṃskāra](../terms/samskara.md), [samādhi-prajñā](../terms/samadhi-prajna.md), [vyutthāna-saṃskāra](../terms/vyutthana-samskara.md), [kleśa-kṣaya](../terms/klesa-ksaya.md) · concepts: [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md), [The wearing away of the afflictions (kleśa-kṣaya)](../concepts/klesa-ksaya.md), [Truth-bearing insight (ṛtambharā prajñā)](../concepts/rtambhara-prajna.md) · obstacles: [Impressions of emergence (vyutthāna-saṃskāra)](../obstacles/vyutthana-samskara.md)

### 1.51 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-1-51"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

This [stilling] not only opposes samādhi-insight but obstructs even the impressions made by insight, because the impression born of stilling (nirodha) obstructs the impressions born of samādhi. The existence of impressions made by the stilled mind is to be inferred from experience of the sequence of the time of remaining in stilling. The mind, with the impressions of emergence, stilling and samādhi and along with those that conduce to kaivalya (kaivalya-bhāgīya), dissolves (pravilīyate) into its own source (prakṛti) as that stands. Those impressions, opposing the mind's office, are not causes of its staying. Since the mind, its office finished, turns back along with the kaivalya-conducing impressions, on that turning back the puruṣa, established in his own form alone, is called pure (śuddha), alone (kevala), free (mukta).

> sa na kevalaṃ samādhiprajñāvirodhī prajñākṛtānām api saṃskārāṇāṃ pratibandhī bhavati /
kasmāt, nirodhajaḥ saṃskāraḥ samādhijān saṃskārān bādhata iti /
nirodhasthitikālakramānubhavena nirodhacittakṛtasaṃskārāstitvam anumeyam /
vyutthānanirodhasamādhiprabhavaiḥ saha kaivalyabhāgīyaiḥ saṃskāraiś cittaṃ svasyāṃ prakṛtāv avasthitāyāṃ pravilīyate /
tasmāt te saṃskārāś cittasyādhikāravirodhino na sthitihetavo bhavantīti /
yasmād avasitādhikāraṃ saha kaivalyabhāgīyaiḥ saṃskāraiś cittaṃ nivartate, tasmin nivṛtte puruṣaḥ svarūpamātrapratiṣṭho 'taḥ śuddhaḥ kevalo mukta ity ucyata iti

_level: ultimate · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced (nirbīja samādhi) · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, ultimate_

terms: [nirbīja](../terms/nirbija.md), [nirodha-saṃskāra](../terms/nirodha-samskara.md), [kaivalya-bhāgīya](../terms/kaivalya-bhagiya.md), [pravilaya](../terms/pravilaya.md), [svarūpa-pratiṣṭhā](../terms/svarupa-pratistha.md), [kevala](../terms/kevala.md), [mukta](../terms/mukta.md), [śuddha](../terms/suddha.md), [saṃskāra](../terms/samskara.md), [kaivalya](../terms/kaivalya.md) · concepts: [Samādhi with seed and seedless (sabīja, nirbīja)](../concepts/sabija-nirbija.md), [Samādhi beyond object-awareness (asamprajñāta)](../concepts/asamprajnata-samadhi.md), [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md)

### 2.1 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Yoga does not succeed for one without austerity (atapasvin). The impurity (aśuddhi), variegated by beginningless karma, afflictions and latent tendencies (vāsanā), and with a net of objects standing ready, cannot be broken up without austerity; hence austerity is taken up. And that austerity, it is held, should be practised by him in a way that does not harm the clarity of the mind (citta-prasādana). Self-study (svādhyāya) is the repetition (japa) of the purifying [syllables] beginning with the praṇava, or the study of the scriptures of liberation (mokṣa-śāstra). Devotion to Īśvara (īśvara-praṇidhāna) is the offering of all actions to the supreme teacher (parama-guru), or the renouncing of their fruit.

> nātapasvino yogaḥ sidhyati /
anādikarmakleśavāsanācitrā pratyupasthitaviṣayajālā cāśuddhir nāntareṇa tapaḥ saṃbhedam āpadyata iti tapasa upādānam /
tac ca cittaprasādanam abādhamānam anenāsevyam iti manyate /
svādhyāyaḥ praṇavādipavitrāṇāṃ japo mokṣaśāstrādhyayanaṃ vā /
īśvarapraṇidhānaṃ sarvakriyāṇāṃ paramagurāv arpaṇaṃ tatphalasaṃnyāso vā

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: action, meditation, devotion, sound · stage: beginner (vyutthita-citta (the unconcentrated mind)) · types: practice, ethics_

terms: [kriyāyoga](../terms/kriya-yoga.md), [tapas](../terms/tapas.md), [svādhyāya](../terms/svadhyaya.md), [īśvara-praṇidhāna](../terms/isvara-pranidhana.md), [aśuddhi](../terms/asuddhi.md), [vāsanā](../terms/vasana.md), [citta-prasādana](../terms/citta-prasadana.md), [japa](../terms/japa.md), [mokṣa-śāstra](../terms/moksa-sastra.md), [parama-guru](../terms/parama-guru.md), [phala-saṃnyāsa](../terms/phala-sannyasa.md), [vyutthāna](../terms/vyutthana.md) · concepts: [Kriyā-yoga, the yoga of action](../concepts/kriya-yoga.md) · practices: [Kriyā-yoga (austerity, self-study, devotion to Īśvara)](../practices/kriya-yoga.md), [Austerity (tapas)](../practices/tapas.md), [Self-study (svādhyāya)](../practices/svadhyaya.md), [Devotion to Īśvara (īśvara-praṇidhāna)](../practices/isvara-pranidhana.md), [Mantra repetition (japa)](../practices/japa.md) · obstacles: [Impurity (aśuddhi)](../obstacles/asuddhi.md)

### 2.2 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Practised, it brings samādhi into being and thins the afflictions (kleśa). The thinned afflictions it will make like burnt seeds (dagdha-bīja-kalpa) by the fire of discerning insight (prasaṃkhyāna), unable to sprout. By their thinning, the subtle insight (sūkṣmā prajñā) that discerns only the difference between sattva and puruṣa (sattva-puruṣa-anyatā-mātra-khyāti), untouched by afflictions, its office complete, becomes fit for reabsorption (pratiprasava). The closing line asks what the afflictions are and how many.

> sa hy āsevyamānaḥ samādhiṃ bhāvayati kleśāṃś ca pratanūkaroti /
pratanūkṛtān kleśān prasaṃkhyānāgninā dagdhabījakalpān aprasavadharmiṇaḥ kariṣyatīti /
teṣāṃ tanūkaraṇāt punaḥ kleśair aparāmṛṣṭā sattvapuruṣānyatāmātrakhyātiḥ sūkṣmā prajñā samāptādhikārā pratiprasavāya kalpiṣyata iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: action, meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice, consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

terms: [samādhi-bhāvanā](../terms/samadhi-bhavana.md), [kleśa-tanūkaraṇa](../terms/klesa-tanukarana.md), [prasaṃkhyāna](../terms/prasankhyana.md), [dagdha-bīja](../terms/dagdha-bija.md), [pratiprasava](../terms/pratiprasava.md), [sattva-puruṣa-anyatā-khyāti](../terms/sattva-purusa-anyata-khyati.md) · concepts: [Kriyā-yoga, the yoga of action](../concepts/kriya-yoga.md), [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../concepts/five-klesas.md), [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md) · practices: [Kriyā-yoga (austerity, self-study, devotion to Īśvara)](../practices/kriya-yoga.md) · obstacles: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../obstacles/five-klesas.md)

### 2.3 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

'Afflictions' means the five errors (viparyaya). Active (spandamāna), they make firm the office of the guṇas (guṇādhikāra), establish transformation (pariṇāma), raise the current of effect and cause (kārya-kāraṇa-srotas), and, becoming interdependent, they bring about the ripening of karma (karma-vipāka).

> kleśā iti pañca viparyayā ity arthaḥ /
te spandamānā guṇādhikāraṃ draḍhayanti, pariṇāmam avasthāpayanti, kāryakāraṇasrota unnamayanti, parasparānugrahatantrībhūtvā karmavipākaṃ cābhinirharantīti

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: general, meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

terms: [kleśa](../terms/klesa.md), [viparyaya](../terms/viparyaya.md), [guṇa-adhikāra](../terms/guna-adhikara.md), [pariṇāma](../terms/parinama.md), [karma-vipāka](../terms/karma-vipaka.md) · concepts: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../concepts/five-klesas.md), [The three guṇas](../concepts/three-gunas.md) · obstacles: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../obstacles/five-klesas.md)

### 2.4 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Here ignorance is the field (kṣetra), the ground of origin, of the later ones — I-am-ness and the rest — which have four varieties: dormant, attenuated, interrupted, fully active. Dormancy (prasupti) is the afflictions' having reached a seed-state (bīja-bhāva), resting in the mind as bare potency (śakti-mātra); their awakening (prabodha) is their coming face to face with a support (ālambana). For one with discerning insight (prasaṃkhyāna) whose seeds of affliction are burnt, even when the support is present the affliction does not arise again; how could a burnt seed sprout? Hence one 'whose afflictions are worn away, skilful, in his last body (carama-deha)' is spoken of; in him alone is that fifth state of the afflictions, the burnt-seed state. Attenuation (tanutva): afflictions struck by the cultivation of their opposite (pratipakṣa-bhāvanā) become thin. Interrupted (vicchinna): they act again and again in this or that form, cut off; e.g. at a time of passion anger is not seen, though passion, seen in one place, is not absent in another — Caitra, enamoured of one woman, is not thereby disenchanted with the others; there passion has gained activity, elsewhere it will gain it; so at that time it is dormant, attenuated and interrupted. Fully active (udāra): the one that has gained activity in regard to an object. All these do not go beyond being of the sphere of the afflictions. The bhāṣya notes that these four belong to particular afflictions in particular circumstances: as one is turned back by the cultivation of the opposite, so it becomes manifest through its own manifester's coloration. All the afflictions are varieties of ignorance, because ignorance pervades in all: what ignorance makes appear as a thing, that the afflictions lie along with; they are found at the time of a cognition of error, and are worn away as ignorance is worn away.

> atrāvidyā kṣetraṃ prasavabhūmir uttarareṣām asmitādīnāṃ caturvidhavikalpānāṃ prasuptatanuvicchinnodārāṇām /
tatra kā prasuptiḥ /
cetasi śaktimātrapratiṣṭhānāṃ bījabhāvopagamaḥ /
tasya prabodha ālambane saṃmukhībhāvaḥ /
prasaṃkhyānavato dagdhakleśabījasya saṃmukhībhūte 'py ālambane nāsau punar asti, dagdhabījasya kutaḥ praroha iti /
ataḥ kṣīṇakleśaḥ kuśalaś caramadeha ity ucyate /
tatraiva sā dagdhabījabhāvā pañcamī kleśāvasthā nānyatreti /
satāṃ kleśānāṃ tadā bījasāmarthyaṃ dagdham iti viṣayasya saṃmukhībhāve 'pi sati na bhavaty eṣāṃ prabodha ity uktā prasuptir dagdhabījānām aprarohaś ca /
tanutvam ucyate --- pratipakṣabhāvanopahatāḥ kleśās tanavo bhavanti /
tathā vicchidya vicchidya tena tenātmanā punaḥ punaḥ samudācarantīti vicchinnāḥ /
kathaṃ, rāgakāle krodhasyādarśanāt /
na hi rāgakāle krodhaḥ samudācarati /
rāgaś ca kvacid dṛśyamāno na viṣayāntare nāsti /
naikasyāṃ striyāṃ caitro rakta ity anyāsu strīṣu viraktaḥ, kiṃtu tatra rāgo labdhavṛttir anyatra tu bhaviṣyadvṛttir iti /
sa hi tadā prasuptatanuvicchinno bhavati /
viṣaye yo labdhavṛttiḥ sa udāraḥ /
sarva evaite kleśaviṣayatvaṃ nātikrāmanti /
kas tarhi vicchinnaḥ prasuptas tanur udāro vā kleśa iti, ucyate --- satyam evaitat, kiṃtu viśiṣṭānām evaiteṣāṃ vicchinnāditvam /
yathaiva pratipakṣabhāvanāto nivṛttas tathaiva svavyañjakāñjanenābhivyakta iti /
sarva evāmī kleśā avidyābhedāḥ /
kasmāt, sarveṣv avidyaivābhiplavate /
yad avidyayā vastv ākāryate tad evānuśerate kleśā viparyāsapratyayakāla upalabhyante kṣīyamāṇāṃ cāvidyām anu kṣīyanta iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: general, meditation · stage: all (prasupta tanu vicchinna udāra (+ dagdhabīja as the fifth)) · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

terms: [avidyā](../terms/avidya.md), [prasupta](../terms/prasupta.md), [tanu](../terms/tanu.md), [vicchinna](../terms/vicchinna.md), [udāra](../terms/udara.md), [dagdha-bīja](../terms/dagdha-bija.md), [bīja-bhāva](../terms/bija-bhava.md), [ālambana](../terms/alambana.md), [prabodha](../terms/prabodha.md), [pratipakṣa-bhāvanā](../terms/pratipaksa-bhavana.md), [carama-deha](../terms/carama-deha.md), [kṣīṇa-kleśa](../terms/ksina-klesa.md) · concepts: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../concepts/five-klesas.md), [The states of the afflictions (dormant, attenuated, interrupted, fully active)](../concepts/four-states-of-klesas.md), [Cultivating the opposite (pratipakṣa-bhāvanā)](../concepts/pratipaksa-bhavana.md) · practices: [Cultivating the opposite (pratipakṣa-bhāvanā)](../practices/pratipaksa-bhavana.md) · obstacles: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../obstacles/five-klesas.md), [Ignorance (avidyā)](../obstacles/avidya.md)

### 2.5 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Ignorance is the appearing of permanence in an impermanent effect: e.g. 'the earth is fixed; the sky with moon and stars is fixed; the dwellers in heaven are immortal.' Likewise the appearing of purity in the impure, the utterly disgusting body — 'the wise know the body to be impure because of its place, its seed, its support, its outflow, its end, and because its purity has to be supplied' [quoted verse]. The text illustrates this with a young woman who is perceived (jñāyate) as lovely, 'like a new crescent of the moon, as if made of honey and nectar', and asks what is connected with what (kasya kena abhisaṃbandhaḥ); so arises the inverted cognition (viparyāsa-pratyaya) of the pure in the impure. By this the cognition of merit in demerit and of the useful in the useless is also explained. Likewise the appearing of pleasure in pain; the sūtra will say later [YS 2.15]: 'everything is suffering for the discerning, because of the sufferings of transformation, torment and impression, and because of the conflict of the functions of the guṇas'. And the appearing of self in the not-self: in external instruments, sentient or insentient (cetana-acetana), in the body as the seat of enjoyment, in the puruṣa's instrument, the mind (manas). It is said here: 'One who takes sattva, manifest or unmanifest, to be self rejoices at its prosperity, thinking it his own prosperity, and grieves at its calamity, thinking it his own calamity; he is wholly unawakened (apratibuddha).' This fourfold ignorance is the root of the whole chain of afflictions and of the store of karma with its ripening. Its true nature is to be understood like 'non-friend' (amitra) and 'cow-track' (goṣpada): as a non-friend is neither the absence of a friend nor merely a friend but a rival opposed to him, and a cow-track is neither the absence of a cow's track nor the mere track but a place different from both, so ignorance is neither valid cognition nor the absence of valid cognition, but another cognition contrary to knowledge (vidyā).

> anitye kārye nityakhyātiḥ /
tadyathā --- dhruvā pṛthivī, dhruvā sacandratārakā dyauḥ, amṛtā divaukasa iti /
tathāśucau paramabībhatse kāye, ---
"sthānād bījād upaṣṭambhān niḥsyandān nidhanād api /
kāyam ādheyaśaucatvāt paṇḍitā hy aśuciṃ viduḥ" //
iti aśucau śucikhyātir dṛśyate /
naveva śaśāṅkalekhā kamanīyeyaṃ kanyā madhvamṛtāvayavanirmiteva candraṃ bhittvā niḥsṛteva jñāyate, nīlotpalapatrāyatākṣī hāvagarbhābhyāṃ locanābhyāṃ jīvalokam āśvāsayantīveti kasya kenābhisaṃbandhaḥ /
bhavati caivam aśucau śuciviparyāsapratyaya iti /
etenāpuṇye puṇyapratyayas tathaivānarthe cārthapratyayo vyākhyātaḥ /
tathā duḥkhe sukhakhyātiṃ vakṣyati --- "pariṇāmatāpasaṃskāraduḥkhair guṇavṛttivirodhāc ca duḥkham eva sarvaṃ vivekinaḥ" iti /
tatra sukhakhyātir avidyā /
tathānātmany ātmakhyātir bāhyopakaraṇeṣu cetanācetaneṣu bhogādhiṣṭhāne vā śarīre puruṣopakaraṇe vā manasy anātmany ātmakhyātir iti /
tathaitad atroktam --- "vyaktam avyaktaṃ vā sattvam ātmatvenābhipratītya tasya saṃpadam anu nandaty ātmasaṃpadaṃ manvānas tasya vyāpadam anu śocaty ātmavyāpadaṃ manvānaḥ sa sarvo 'pratibuddhaḥ" iti /
eṣā catuṣpadā bhavaty avidyā mūlam asya kleśasaṃtānasya karmāśayasya ca savipākasyeti /
tasyāś cāmitrāgoṣpadavadvastusatattvaṃ vijñeyam /
yathā nāmitro mitrābhāvo na mitramātraṃ kiṃtu tadviruddhaḥ sapatnaḥ /
yathā vāgoṣpadaṃ na goṣpadābhāvo na goṣpadamātraṃ kiṃtu deśa eva tābhyām anyad vastvantaram /
evam avidyā na pramāṇaṃ na pramāṇābhāvaḥ kiṃtu vidyāviparītaṃ jñānāntaram avidyeti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: general, knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

### 2.6 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-6"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The puruṣa is the power of the seer (dṛk-śakti); the buddhi is the power of seeing (darśana-śakti). The seeming coming-together of the two into a single form is called the affliction I-am-ness. When the powers of the enjoyer and the enjoyed, utterly distinct and utterly unmixed, are as if reached in non-separation, then enjoyment (bhoga) is possible. But when they attain their own forms, only kaivalya comes about; where would enjoyment be? It is also said: 'One who does not see the puruṣa beyond the buddhi as distinct by form, conduct, knowledge and the like will make a self-notion (ātma-buddhi) there through delusion (moha).'

> puruṣo dṛkśaktir buddhir darśanaśaktir ity etayor ekasvarūpāpattir ivāsmitā kleśa ucyate /
bhoktṛbhogyaśaktyor atyantavibhaktayor atyantāsaṃkīrṇayor avibhāgaprāptāv iva satyāṃ bhogaḥ kalpate /
svarūpapratilambhe tu tayoḥ kaivalyam eva bhavati kuto bhoga iti /
tathā coktam --- "buddhitaḥ paraṃ puruṣam ākāraśīlavidyādibhir vibhaktam apaśyan kuryāt tatrātmabuddhiṃ mohena" iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: general, knowledge · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

### 2.7 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-7"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

For one who has known pleasure, attachment (rāga) is the greed (gardha), thirst (tṛṣṇā) and covetousness (lobha), preceded by the memory of pleasure, toward the pleasure or its means.

> sukhābhijñasya sukhānusmṛtipūrvaḥ sukhe tatsādhane vā yo gardhas tṛṣṇā lobhaḥ sa rāga iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

### 2.8 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-8"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

For one who has known pain, aversion (dveṣa) is the recoil (pratigha), resentment (manyu), wish to strike (jighāṃsā) and anger (krodha), preceded by the memory of pain, toward the pain or its means.

> duḥkhābhijñasya duḥkhānusmṛtipūrvo duḥkhe tatsādhane vā yaḥ pratigho manyur jighāṃsā krodhaḥ sa dveṣaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: general · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind_

### 2.9 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-9"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

In every living being this wish for oneself (ātmāśīḥ) is constant: 'may I not cease to be; may I be.' It does not arise in one who has never experienced the nature of death; by it an experience in a previous birth is inferred. This affliction, clinging (abhiniveśa), flowing by its own momentum, is found even in a worm just born: its fear of death, not accounted for by perception, inference or scripture, having the form of the view of annihilation (uccheda-dṛṣṭi), points to the pain of death experienced in a previous birth. As this affliction is seen in the utterly deluded, so it is rooted in the wise too, who know the before and after; for in both the skilled and the unskilled this latent tendency (vāsanā) is the same, arising from the experience of the pain of death.

> sarvasya prāṇina iyam ātmāśīr nityā bhavati mā na bhūvaṃ bhūyāsam iti /
na cānanubhūtamaraṇadharmakasyaiṣā bhavaty ātmāśīḥ /
etayā ca pūrvajanmānubhavaḥ pratīyate /
sa cāyam abhiniveśaḥ kleśaḥ svarasavāhī kṛmer api jātamātrasya pratyakṣānumānāgamair asaṃbhāvito maraṇatrāsa ucchedadṛṣṭyātmakaḥ pūrvajanmānubhūtaṃ maraṇaduḥkham anumāpayati /
yathā cāyam atyantamūḍheṣu dṛśyate kleśas tathā viduṣo 'pi vijñātapūrvāparāntasya rūḍhaḥ /
kasmāt samānā hi tayoḥ kuśalākuśalayor maraṇaduḥkhānubhavād iyaṃ vāsaneti

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: general, meditation · stage: all · types: consciousness-mind, death-dying, karma-liberation_

### 2.10 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-10"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Those five afflictions, like burnt seeds, when the yogin's mind whose office is done is dissolved (pralīna), go to their setting along with it. The closing line begins the next: of those that remain in the seed-state, [what is their way?].

> te pañca kleśā dagdhabījakalpā yoginaś caritādhikāre cetasi pralīne saha tenaivāstaṃ gacchanti. 2.10
sthitānāṃ tu bījabhāvopagatānām ---

### 2.11 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-11"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The gross activities of the afflictions, thinned by kriyā-yoga, are to be abandoned by meditation (dhyāna) that is discerning insight (prasaṃkhyāna), until they are made subtle, until they are like burnt seeds. As the gross dirt of clothes is first shaken off and the subtle dirt afterwards removed with effort and means, so the gross activities of the afflictions have little opposition, but the subtle ones have great opposition.

> kleśānāṃ yā vṛttayaḥ sthūlās tāḥ kriyāyogena tanūkṛtāḥ satyaḥ prasaṃkhyānena dhyānena hātavyā yāvat sūkṣmīkṛtā yāvad dagdhabījakalpā iti /
yathā vastrāṇāṃ sthūlo malaḥ pūrvaṃ nirdhūyate paścāt sūkṣmo yatnenopāyena cāpanīyate tathā svalpapratipakṣāḥ sthūlā vṛttayaḥ kleśānāṃ, sūkṣmās tu mahāpratipakṣā iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [kleśa-vṛtti](../terms/klesa-vrtti.md), [sthūla](../terms/sthula.md), [sūkṣma](../terms/suksma.md), [dhyāna](../terms/dhyana.md), [prasaṃkhyāna](../terms/prasankhyana.md), [kriyāyoga](../terms/kriya-yoga.md), [dagdha-bīja](../terms/dagdha-bija.md) · concepts: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../concepts/five-klesas.md), [The states of the afflictions (dormant, attenuated, interrupted, fully active)](../concepts/four-states-of-klesas.md), [Kriyā-yoga, the yoga of action](../concepts/kriya-yoga.md) · practices: [Meditation (dhyāna) as the means of abandoning the afflictions' activities](../practices/dhyana.md), [Kriyā-yoga (austerity, self-study, devotion to Īśvara)](../practices/kriya-yoga.md) · obstacles: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../obstacles/five-klesas.md)

### 2.12 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-12"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The store of meritorious and demeritorious karma arises from desire (kāma), greed (lobha), delusion (moha) and anger (krodha). It is to be experienced either in the seen birth (dṛṣṭa-janma-vedanīya) or in the unseen birth. A meritorious store made with intense ardor by mantra, austerity and samādhi, or achieved by propitiating Īśvara, deities, great seers or those of great power, ripens at once. Likewise a demeritorious store, done repeatedly out of intense affliction against the frightened, the ill, the wretched, those who trust one, or those of great power and the ascetic, ripens at once. Examples: the boy Nandīśvara left his human form and was changed into divinity; likewise Nahuṣa, lord of the gods, left his own form and was changed into an animal. For those in hells there is no karma-store to be experienced in a seen birth; and for those whose afflictions are worn away there is none to be experienced in an unseen birth.

> tatra puṇyāpuṇyakarmāśayaḥ kāmalobhamohakrodhabhavaḥ /
sa dṛṣṭajanmavedanīyaś cādṛṣṭajanmavedanīyaś ca /
tatra tīvrasaṃvegena mantratapaḥsamādhibhir nirvartita īśvaradevatāmaharṣimahānubhāvānām ārādhanād vā yaḥ pariniṣpannaḥ sa sadyaḥ paripacyate puṇyakarmāśaya iti /
tathā tīvrakleśena bhītavyādhitakṛpaṇeṣu viśvāsopagateṣu vā mahānubhāveṣu vā tapasviṣu kṛtaḥ punaḥ punar apakāraḥ sa cāpi pāpakarmāśayaḥ sadya eva paripacyate /
yathā nandīśvaraḥ kumāro manuṣyapariṇāmaṃ hitvā devatvena pariṇataḥ /
tathā nahuṣo 'pi devānām indraḥ svakaṃ pariṇāmaṃ hitvā tiryaktvena pariṇata iti /
tatra nārakāṇāṃ nāsti dṛṣṭajanmavedanīyaḥ karmāśayaḥ /
kṣīṇakleśānām api nāsty adṛṣṭajanmavedanīyaḥ karmāśaya iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: general, action · stage: all · types: karma-liberation_

terms: [karmāśaya](../terms/karmasaya.md), [kāma](../terms/kama.md), [lobha](../terms/lobha.md), [moha](../terms/moha.md), [krodha](../terms/krodha.md), [dṛṣṭajanma-vedanīya](../terms/drstajanma-vedaniya.md), [adṛṣṭajanma-vedanīya](../terms/adrstajanma-vedaniya.md), [dṛṣṭa-janma-vedanīya](../terms/drsta-janma-vedaniya.md), [adṛṣṭa-janma-vedanīya](../terms/adrsta-janma-vedaniya.md) · concepts: [The store of karma (karmāśaya)](../concepts/karmasaya.md), [The four kinds of karma (4.7)](../concepts/four-kinds-of-karma.md) · obstacles: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../obstacles/five-klesas.md) · teachers: [Nandīśvara (as named in the Yoga-bhāṣya on 4.3)](../teachers/nandisvara-bhasya-example.md), [Nahuṣa](../teachers/nahusa.md)

### 2.13 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-13"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

While the afflictions exist, the store of karma begins to ripen; not when the root of affliction is cut. As rice grains still bound in their husk, whose seed-state is not burnt, are able to sprout, but not when the husk is removed or the seed burnt, so a karma-store bound by afflictions sprouts into ripening, not when the afflictions are removed or their seeds burnt by discerning insight. The ripening is threefold: birth (jāti), life-span (āyus) and experience (bhoga). The bhāṣya asks whether one karma causes one birth or many, and whether many karmas cause one birth or many, and rejects the alternatives: if each karma made one birth, the order of fruits of the beginningless, uncountable stored karma and of present karma would be unsettled and beings would have no assurance; if one karma caused many births, no time would remain for the rest; many karmas cannot cause many births at once, and if in sequence the earlier faults return. Therefore the varied store of merit and demerit made between one death and the next, standing as principal or secondary, becomes manifest at death, and, gathered by a single push (eka-praghaṭṭaka), completes death and produces just one birth; that birth gets its life-span from the same karma, and in that life-span experience arises from the same karma. Because it is the cause of birth, life-span and experience, that store is called 'of threefold ripening' (trivipāka). The bhāṣya goes on: this store is therefore called 'of one life' (ekabhavika). One to be experienced in the seen birth starts a single ripening, as cause of experience, or a double one, as cause of life-span and experience, as with Nandīśvara or Nahuṣa. The mind, by contrast, has been variegated since beginningless time by latent tendencies (vāsanā) produced by the experience of afflictions, karma and ripening, spread out on all sides like a fishing net with its knots; these vāsanās go back over many lives, whereas the karma-store is of one life, and the impressions that cause memory are the vāsanās, which are beginningless. The one-life store is of fixed (niyata) or unfixed (aniyata) ripening, and the rule stated holds only for what is to be experienced in the seen birth with fixed ripening. Unfixed karma to be experienced in an unseen birth has three courses: what was done but has not ripened is destroyed (as the black is destroyed here by the arising of the white); or it merges (āvāpa-gamana) into a principal karma; or it remains long, overpowered by a principal karma of fixed ripening. Because the place, time and cause of its ripening cannot be ascertained, the course of karma is varied and hard to know; yet an exception does not cancel the general rule, so the one-life karma-store is accepted.

> satsu kleśeṣu karmāśayo vipākārambhī bhavati nocchinnakleśamūlaḥ /
yathā tuṣāvanaddhāḥ śālitaṇḍulā adagdhabījabhāvāḥ prarohasamarthā bhavanti, nāpanītatuṣā dagdhabījabhāvā vā tathā kleśāvanaddhaḥ karmāśayo vipākaprarohī bhavati, nāpanītakleśo na prasaṃkhyānadagdhakleśabījabhāvo veti /
sa ca vipākas trividho jātir āyur bhoga iti /
tatredaṃ vicāryate --- kim ekaṃ karmaikasya janmanaḥ kāraṇam athaikaṃ karmānekaṃ janmākṣipatīti /
dvitīyā vicāraṇā --- kim anekaṃ karmānekaṃ janma nirvartayati athānekaṃ karmaikaṃ janma nirvartayatīti /
na tāvad ekaṃ karmaikasya janmanaḥ kāraṇam /
kasmāt, anādikālapracitasyāsaṃkhyeyasyāvaśiṣṭasya karmaṇaḥ sāṃpratikasya ca phalakramāniyamād anāśvāso lokasya prasaktaḥ, sa cāniṣṭa iti /
na caikaṃ karmānekasya janmanaḥ kāraṇam /
kasmāt, anekeṣu karmasu ekaikam eva karmānekasya janmanaḥ kāraṇam ity avaśiṣṭasya vipākakālābhāvaḥ prasaktaḥ, sa cāpy aniṣṭa iti /
na cānekaṃ karmānekasya janmanaḥ kāraṇam /
kasmāt, tad anekaṃ janma yugapan na saṃbhavatīti krameṇaiva vācyam /
tathā ca pūrvadoṣānuṣaṅgaḥ /
tasmāj janmaprāyaṇāntare kṛtaḥ puṇyāpuṇyakarmāśayapracayo vicitraḥ pradhānopasarjanabhāvenāvasthitaḥ prāyaṇābhivyakta ekapraghaṭṭakena maraṇaṃ prasādhya saṃmūrchita ekam eva janma karoti /
tac ca janma tenaiva karmaṇā labdhāyuṣkaṃ bhavati /
tasminn āyuṣi tenaiva karmaṇā bhogaḥ saṃpadyata iti /
asau karmāśayo janmāyurbhogahetutvāt trivipāko 'bhidhīyata iti

terms: [karmāśaya](../terms/karmasaya.md), [vipāka](../terms/vipaka.md), [jāti](../terms/jati.md), [āyus](../terms/ayus.md), [bhoga](../terms/bhoga.md), [trivipāka](../terms/trivipaka.md), [eka-praghaṭṭaka](../terms/eka-praghattaka.md), [prāyaṇa](../terms/prayana.md), [dagdha-bīja](../terms/dagdha-bija.md), [kleśa-mūla](../terms/klesa-mula.md) · concepts: [The fruition of karma: birth, life-span, experience](../concepts/vipaka-jati-ayus-bhoga.md), [The store of karma (karmāśaya)](../concepts/karmasaya.md), [The four kinds of karma (4.7)](../concepts/four-kinds-of-karma.md) · obstacles: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../obstacles/five-klesas.md)

### 2.14 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-14"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Those birth, life-span and experience, with merit as cause, have pleasure as fruit; with demerit as cause, pain as fruit. And as this pain is of the nature of what is contrary (pratikūla), so for the yogin even at the time of a sense-pleasure there is only pain, of the nature of what is contrary. The closing line leads to how this is shown.

> te janmāyurbhogāḥ puṇyahetukāḥ sukhaphalā apuṇyahetukā duḥkhaphalā iti /
yathā cedaṃ duḥkhaṃ pratikūlātmakam evaṃ viṣayasukhakāle 'pi duḥkham asty eva pratikūlātmakaṃ yoginaḥ. 2.14
kathaṃ, tad upapādyate ---

terms: [hlāda](../terms/hlada.md), [paritāpa](../terms/paritapa.md), [puṇya](../terms/punya.md), [apuṇya](../terms/apunya.md), [pratikūla](../terms/pratikula.md), [duḥkha](../terms/duhkha.md) · concepts: [The fruition of karma: birth, life-span, experience](../concepts/vipaka-jati-ayus-bhoga.md), [Everything is suffering for the discerning (YS 2.15)](../concepts/duhkha-for-the-discerning.md)

### 2.15 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-15"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Every experience of pleasure is shot through with attachment (rāga) and depends on means, sentient or insentient (cetana-acetana); so there is a karma-store born of attachment. Likewise one hates the means of pain and is deluded, so there is a store born of aversion and delusion; and it is said that 'enjoyment does not arise without harming beings', so there is a bodily store made by harm (hiṃsā). Pleasure from objects is (as said) ignorance. Pleasure is the calm of the senses from satisfaction (tṛpti) in enjoyments; the failure to be calmed, from fickleness (laulya), is pain. The senses cannot be made free of thirst by the habit of enjoyment, because with the habit of enjoying, attachments and the skill of the senses grow; so the habit of enjoyment is not a means to pleasure. One who seeks pleasure, steeped in objects, is like one frightened of a scorpion's venom who is bitten by a snake: he sinks in a great mire of pain. This is called suffering of transformation (pariṇāma-duḥkhatā): being contrary (pratikūla) it torments only the yogin, even in the state of pleasure. Suffering of torment (tāpa-duḥkhatā): every experience of pain is shot through with aversion and depends on means; seeking the means of pleasure, one acts with body, speech and mind, and then favours or injures others, and by favour and injury heaps up dharma and adharma; that store arises from greed and delusion. Suffering of impression (saṃskāra-duḥkhatā): from the experience of pleasure there is a store of pleasure-impressions, and from the experience of pain a store of pain-impressions; so when the ripening of karma is experienced as pleasure or pain, a further store of karma is heaped up. Thus this beginningless stream of suffering, spread out, disturbs only the yogin, because it is by nature adverse (pratikūla); for the discerning person is like the eyeball (akṣi-pātra): a strand of wool laid on the eye pains by its touch but not on other limbs, so these sufferings afflict only the yogin, not the other person. The other takes up suffering brought by his own karma, drops what he has taken up and takes up again what he has dropped, as if pervaded by ignorance through a mind varied by beginningless tendencies; following the I-making and mine-making, he is followed in each birth by the threefold heats (tāpa) from external and inner causes. Seeing himself and the whole mass of beings being carried along by the beginningless stream of suffering, the yogin takes refuge in right seeing (samyag-darśana), the cause of the end of all suffering.

> sarvasyāyaṃ rāgānuviddhaś cetanācetanasādhanādhīnaḥ sukhānubhava iti tatrāsti rāgajaḥ karmāśayaḥ /
tathā ca dveṣṭi duḥkhasādhanāni muhyati ceti dveṣamohakṛto 'py asti karmāśayaḥ /
tathā coktam --- "nānupahatya bhūtāny upabhogaḥ saṃbhavatīti hiṃsākṛto 'py asti śarīraḥ karmāśayaḥ" iti /
viṣayasukhaṃ cāvidyety uktam /
yā bhogeṣv indriyāṇāṃ tṛpter upaśāntis tat sukham /
yā laulyād anupaśāntis tad duḥkham /
na cendriyāṇāṃ bhogābhyāsena vaitṛṣṇyaṃ kartuṃ śakyam /
kasmāt, yato bhogābhyāsam anu vivardhante rāgāḥ kauśalāni cendriyāṇām iti /
tasmād anupāyaḥ sukhasya bhogābhyāsa iti /
sa khalv ayaṃ vṛścikaviṣabhīta ivāśīviṣeṇa daṣṭo yaḥ sukhārthī viṣayānuvāsito mahati duḥkhapaṅke nimagna iti /
eṣā pariṇāmaduḥkhatā nāma pratikūlā sukhāvasthāyām api yoginam eva kliśnāti /
atha kā tāpaduḥkhatā, sarvasya dveṣānuviddhaś cetanācetanasādhanādhīnas tāpānubhava iti tatrāsti dveṣajaḥ karmāśayaḥ /
sukhasādhanāni ca prārthayamānaḥ kāyena vācā manasā ca parispandate tataḥ param anugṛhṇāty upahanti ceti parānugrahapīḍābhyāṃ dharmādharmāv upacinoti /
sa karmāśayo lobhān mohāc ca bhavatīty eṣā tāpaduḥkhatocyate /
kā punaḥ saṃskāraduḥkhatā, sukhānubhavāt sukhasaṃskārāśayo duḥkhānubhavād api duḥkhasaṃskārāśaya iti /
evaṃ karmabhyo vipāke 'nubhūyamāne sukhe duḥkhe vā punaḥ karmāśayapracaya iti /
evam idam anādi duḥkhasroto viprasṛtaṃ yoginam eva pratikūlātmakatvād udvejayati /
kasmāt, akṣipātrakalpo hi vidvān iti /
yathorṇātantur akṣipātre nyastaḥ sparśena duḥkhayati na cānyeṣu gātrāvayaveṣu, evam etāni duḥkhāny akṣipātrakalpaṃ yoginam eva kliśnanti netaraṃ pratipattāram /
itaraṃ tu svakarmopahṛtaṃ duḥkham upāttam upāttaṃ tyajantaṃ tyaktaṃ tyaktam upādadānam anādivāsanāvicitrayā cittavṛttyā samantato 'nuviddham ivāvidyayā hātavya evāhaṃkāramamakārānupātinaṃ jātaṃ jātaṃ bāhyādhyātmikobhayanimittās triparvāṇas tāpā anuplavante /
tad evam anādinā duḥkhasrotasā vyuhyamānam ātmānaṃ bhūtagrāmaṃ ca dṛṣṭvā yogī sarvaduḥkhakṣayakāraṇaṃ samyagdarśanaṃ śaraṇaṃ prapadyata iti

### 2.15 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-15-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Also for the discerning everything is suffering because of the conflict of the functions of the guṇas. The guṇas of the buddhi, of the nature of illumination (prakhyā), activity (pravṛtti) and inertia (sthiti), cooperate with one another and produce a cognition (pratyaya) that is calm (śānta), terrible (ghora) or deluded (mūḍha), but always threefold. The functioning of the guṇas is unsteady (cala), so the mind is said to change quickly. The stronger forms and stronger functions conflict with one another, while the general ones proceed along with the stronger. So the guṇas, by mutual support, produce cognitions of pleasure, pain and delusion, all being of all forms; the difference among them is made by which guṇa is principal. Therefore, for the discerning, everything is suffering.

> guṇavṛttivirodhāc ca duḥkham eva sarvaṃ vivekinaḥ /
prakhyāpravṛttisthitirūpā buddhiguṇāḥ parasparānugrahatantrī bhūtvā śāntaṃ ghoraṃ mūḍhaṃ vā pratyayaṃ triguṇam evārabhante /
calaṃ ca guṇavṛttam iti kṣiprapariṇāmi cittam uktam /
rūpātiśayā vṛttyatiśayāś ca paraspareṇa virudhyante, sāmānyāni tv atiśayaiḥ saha pravartante /
evam ete guṇā itaretarāśrayeṇopārjitasukhaduḥkhamohapratyayāḥ sarve sarvarūpā bhavantīti, guṇapradhānabhāvakṛtas tv eṣāṃ viśeṣa iti /
tasmād duḥkham eva sarvaṃ vivekina iti

### 2.15 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-15-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Ignorance (avidyā) is the seed of origin of this great mass of suffering; right seeing (samyag-darśana) is the cause of its absence. As the science of healing is fourfold — disease, cause of disease, health, medicine — so this śāstra is fourfold: saṃsāra, cause of saṃsāra, liberation (mokṣa), means of liberation. Of these, saṃsāra, full of suffering, is what is to be abandoned (heya); the conjunction (saṃyoga) of pradhāna and puruṣa is the cause of what is to be abandoned; the absolute ceasing of the conjunction is the abandonment (hāna); the means of abandonment is right seeing. Here the nature of the abandoner (hātṛ) can be neither to-be-taken nor to-be-abandoned: in abandoning it there would follow the doctrine of annihilation (uccheda-vāda); in taking it, the doctrine of causation (hetu-vāda); in rejecting both, the doctrine of eternity (śāśvata-vāda). This is right seeing.

> tad asya mahato duḥkhasamudāyasya prabhavabījam avidyā /
tasyāś ca samyagdarśanam abhāvahetuḥ /
yathā cikitsāśāstraṃ caturvyūham --- rogo rogahetur ārogyaṃ bhaiṣajyam iti /
evam idam api śāstram caturvyūham eva /
tadyathā --- saṃsāraḥ saṃsārahetur mokṣo mokṣopāya iti /
tatra duḥkhabahulaḥ saṃsāro heyaḥ /
pradhānapuruṣayoḥ saṃyogo heyahetuḥ /
saṃyogasyātyantikī nivṛttir hānam /
hānopāyaḥ samyagdarśanam /
tatra hātuḥ svarūpam upādeyaṃ vā heyaṃ vā na bhavitum arhatīti hāne tasyocchedavādaprasaṅga upādāne ca hetuvādaḥ /
ubhayapratyākhyāne śāśvatavāda ity etat samyagdarśanam

### 2.16 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-16"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Past suffering, passed through by enjoyment, does not fall on the side of what is to be abandoned. Present suffering, mounted on enjoyment in its own moment, does not become something to be abandoned in another moment. So only the suffering not yet come afflicts the yogin who is like the eyeball, not the other knower; and that alone is to be abandoned.

> duḥkham atītam upabhogenātivāhitaṃ na heyapakṣe vartate /
vartamānaṃ ca svakṣaṇe bhogārūḍham iti na tat kṣaṇāntare heyatām āpadyate /
tasmād yad evānāgataṃ duḥkhaṃ tad evākṣipātrakalpaṃ yoginaṃ kliśnāti netaraṃ pratipattāram /
tad eva heyatām āpadyate. 2.16
tasmād yad eva heyam ity ucyate tasyaiva kāraṇaṃ pratinirdiśyate ---

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general · stage: all · types: karma-liberation_

### 2.17 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-17"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The seer is the puruṣa, the witness (pratisaṃvedin) of the buddhi. The seen is all properties (dharma) resting on buddhi-sattva. This seen, like a magnet-jewel (ayaskānta-maṇi), helps by mere nearness; by being seen it becomes the possession of the puruṣa, whose form is seeing, its master, since it has become an object of experience. Though it obtains its own being through another's form and is independent, it is dependent because it is for another. The beginningless, purpose-made conjunction of those two powers, the power of seeing and the power of sight, is the cause of what is to be abandoned, that is, of suffering. And it is said: 'By avoiding the cause of that conjunction there would be an absolute counteraction of suffering.' Why? Because for a suffering-cause that can be avoided a counteraction is seen: the sole of the foot can be pierced, a thorn can pierce, and the avoidance is not standing on the thorn, or standing on it with a shoe between. One who knows these three in the world, beginning the counteraction, does not get the pain born of piercing, since he can perceive the three. Here too, of rajas the heater, only sattva is heated (tapya), because the action of heating is in the object (karmastha). The heating is in sattva as object, not in the unchanging, inactive field-knower (kṣetrajña), since objects have been shown to him; but when sattva is heated, the puruṣa, following its form, is also heated afterwards.

> draṣṭā buddheḥ pratisaṃvedī puruṣaḥ /
dṛśyā buddhisattvopārūḍhāḥ sarve dharmāḥ /
tad etad dṛśyam ayaskāntamaṇikalpaṃ saṃnidhimātropakāri dṛśyatvena svaṃ bhavati puruṣasya dṛśirūpasya svāminaḥ, anubhavakarmaviṣayatām āpannaṃ yataḥ /
anyasvarūpeṇa pratilabdhātmakaṃ svatantram api parārthatvāt paratantram /
tayor dṛgdarśanaśaktyor anādir arthakṛtaḥ saṃyogo heyahetur duḥkhasya kāraṇam ity arthaḥ /
tathā coktam --- tatsaṃyogahetuvivarjanāt syād ayam ātyantiko duḥkhapratīkāraḥ /
kasmāt, duḥkhahetoḥ parihāryasya pratīkāradarśanāt /
tadyathā --- pādatalasya bhedyatā, kaṇṭakasya bhettṛtvaṃ, parihāraḥ kaṇṭakasya pā(pa)dānadhiṣṭhānaṃ pādatrāṇavyavahitena vādhiṣṭhānam, etat trayaṃ yo veda loke sa tatra pratīkāram ārabhamāṇo bhedajaṃ duḥkhaṃ nāpnoti /
kasmāt, tritvopalabdhisāmarthyād iti /
atrāpi tāpakasya rajasaḥ sattvam eva tapyam /
kasmāt, tapikriyāyāḥ karmasthatvāt, sattve karmaṇi tapikriyā nāpariṇāmini niṣkriye kṣetrajñe, darśitaviṣayatvāt /
sattve tu tapyamāne tadākārānurodhī puruṣo 'py anutapyata iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: intermediate · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, ultimate_

terms: [draṣṭṛ](../terms/drastr.md), [dṛśya](../terms/drsya.md), [saṃyoga](../terms/samyoga.md), [heya-hetu](../terms/heya-hetu.md), [pratisaṃvedin](../terms/pratisamvedin.md), [ayaskānta-maṇi](../terms/ayaskanta-mani.md), [buddhi-sattva](../terms/buddhi-sattva.md), [kṣetrajña](../terms/ksetrajna.md) · concepts: [The seer and the seen (draṣṭṛ and dṛśya)](../concepts/seer-and-seen.md), [The conjunction of seer and seen (saṃyoga)](../concepts/samyoga.md), [The fourfold scheme of the śāstra (heya, heya-hetu, hāna, hānopāya)](../concepts/caturvyuha.md), [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md), [The witness (the seer as pratisaṃvedin)](../concepts/witness.md)

### 2.18 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-18"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Sattva has the character of illumination; rajas, of activity; tamas, of inertia. These guṇas are coloured by one another in their divisions, transform, have the nature of conjunction and disjunction, take shape by mutual support, and, though each is a limb of the others, have powers that do not blend; they follow the division of like and unlike powers. When [one of them is] predominant (pradhāna-velā) their presence is shown; even when subordinate (guṇatve), their existence is inferred from their mere functioning as included in the predominant one; they are employed for the puruṣa's purpose, help by mere nearness like magnets, and following the working of any one without reliance on cognition are called by the name pradhāna. This is 'the seen'. It transforms as elements (bhūta) — earth and so on, subtle and gross — and as sense-faculties (indriya) — ear and so on, subtle and gross. It does not act without purpose but with a purpose accepted: for the seen exists for the puruṣa's enjoyment and liberation. Enjoyment (bhoga) is the ascertaining of the natures of desired and undesired qualities without distinction [from the seer]; liberation (apavarga) is the ascertaining of the nature of the enjoyer. There is no other seeing besides these two. It is said: 'This one, seeing all states brought before the three guṇas, which act, and the fourth, the non-acting puruṣa, like and unlike them and witness of their action, as fitting, suspects some other non-seeing.' How are enjoyment and liberation, made by the buddhi and abiding in the buddhi, attributed to the puruṣa? As victory or defeat abiding in the warriors is attributed to their master, who is the enjoyer of the fruit, so bondage and liberation, abiding in the buddhi, are attributed to the puruṣa. The buddhi's non-completion of the puruṣa's purpose is bondage; its ascertaining that purpose is liberation. By this, grasping, holding, reasoning-out, rejecting, knowledge of the real, and clinging (abhiniveśa) abiding in the buddhi are superimposed as existing in the puruṣa.

> prakāśaśīlaṃ sattvam /
kriyāśīlaṃ rajaḥ sthitiśīlaṃ tama iti /
ete guṇāḥ parasparoparaktapravibhāgāḥ pariṇāminaḥ saṃyogaviyogadharmāṇa itaretaropāśrayeṇopārjitamūrtayaḥ parasparāṅgāṅgitve 'py asaṃbhinnaśaktipravibhāgās tulyajātīyātulyajātīyaśaktibhedānupātinaḥ pradhānavelāyām upadarśitasaṃnidhānā guṇatve 'pi ca vyāpāramātreṇa pradhānāntarṇītānumitāstitāḥ puruṣārthakartavyatayā prayuktasāmarthyāḥ saṃnidhimātropakāriṇo 'yaskāntamaṇikalpāḥ pratyayam antareṇaikatamasya vṛttim anuvartamānāḥ pradhānaśabdavācyā bhavanti /
etad dṛśyam ity ucyate /
tad etad bhūtendriyātmakaṃ bhūtabhāvena pṛthivyādinā sūkṣmasthūlena pariṇamate /
tathendriyabhāvena śrotrādinā sūkṣmasthūlena pariṇamata iti /
tat tu nāprayojanam api tu prayojanam urarīkṛtya pravartata iti bhogāpavargārthaṃ hi tad dṛśyaṃ puruṣasyeti /
tatreṣṭāniṣṭaguṇasvarūpāvadhāraṇam avibhāgāpannaṃ bhogo bhoktuḥ svarūpāvadhāraṇam apavarga iti /
dvayor atiriktam anyad darśanaṃ nāsti /
tathā coktam --- ayaṃ tu khalu triṣu guṇeṣu kartṛṣv akartari ca puruṣe tulyātulyajātīye caturthe tatkriyāsākṣiṇy upanīyamānān sarvabhāvān upapannān anupaśyann adarśanam anyac chaṅkata iti /
tāv etau bhogāpavargau buddhikṛtau buddhāv eva vartamānau kathaṃ puruṣe vyapadiśyete iti /
yathā vijayaḥ parājayo vā yoddhṛṣu vartamānaḥ svāmini vyapadiśyate, sa hi tatphalasya bhokteti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: intermediate · types: consciousness-mind, body-layers, karma-liberation, world-fate_

terms: [dṛśya](../terms/drsya.md), [sattva](../terms/sattva.md), [rajas](../terms/rajas.md), [tamas](../terms/tamas.md), [pradhāna](../terms/pradhana.md), [mahābhūta](../terms/mahabhuta.md), [indriya](../terms/indriya.md), [bhoga](../terms/bhoga.md), [apavarga](../terms/apavarga.md), [bandha](../terms/bandha.md), [mokṣa](../terms/moksa.md), [buddhi](../terms/buddhi.md) · concepts: [The three guṇas](../concepts/three-gunas.md), [The seer and the seen (draṣṭṛ and dṛśya)](../concepts/seer-and-seen.md), [The seen exists for the seer (bhoga and apavarga)](../concepts/purpose-of-prakrti.md), [Prakṛti (primordial nature)](../concepts/prakrti.md)

### 2.19 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-19"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The particularized (viśeṣa) are the elements (ākāśa, air, fire, water, earth), the five cognitive faculties (ear, skin, eye, tongue, nose), the five action faculties (speech, hand, foot, excretory organ, generative organ) and mind (manas) as the eleventh, serving every purpose: the sixteenfold particularized transformation of the guṇas. The six unparticularized (aviśeṣa) are the five tanmātras (sound, touch, form, taste, smell) and I-am-ness alone (asmitā-mātra). The mere mark (liṅga-mātra) is the great principle (mahat-tattva), being-alone (sattā-mātra), of which the six are transformations; the unmarked (aliṅga) is the unmanifest primal (pradhāna), 'neither being nor non-being', into which things dissolve on reabsorption. In the unmarked state, the purpose of the puruṣa is not a cause; in the three other stages it is, so they are called non-eternal. The guṇas follow all properties; they do not set or arise; they seem to arise and perish through their manifestations that come and go, like Devadatta 'becoming poor' when his cows die, from the death of the cows and not from any loss of himself. Each stage is mixed with the next nearer to the unmarked and is separated out from it in order; there is no further principle beyond the particularized, so the particularized undergo no further tattva-transformation; their transformations of property, mark and state will be explained later.

> tatrākāśavāyvagnyudakabhūmayo bhūtāni śabdasparśarūparasagandhatanmātrāṇām aviśeṣāṇāṃ viśeṣāḥ /
tathā śrotratvakcakṣurjihvāghrāṇāni buddhīndriyāṇi, vākpāṇipādapāyūpasthāḥ karmendriyāṇi, ekādaśaṃ manaḥ sarvārtham, ity etāny asmitālakṣaṇasyāviśeṣasya viśeṣāḥ /
guṇānām eṣa ṣoḍaśako viśeṣapariṇāmaḥ /
ṣaḍ aviśeṣāḥ /
tadyathā --- śabdatanmātraṃ sparśatanmātraṃ rūpatanmātraṃ rasatanmātraṃ gandhatanmātraṃ ceti ekadvitricatuḥpañcalakṣaṇāḥ śabdādayaḥ pañcāviśeṣāḥ, ṣaṣṭhaś cāviśeṣo 'smitāmātra iti /
ete sattāmātrasyātmano mahataḥ ṣaḍaviśeṣapariṇāmāḥ /
yat tat paramaviśeṣebhyo liṅgamātraṃ mahattattvaṃ tasminn ete sattāmātre mahaty ātmany avasthāya vivṛddhikāṣṭhām anubhavanti /
pratisaṃsṛjyamānāś ca tasminn eva sattāmātre mahaty ātmany avasthāya yat tan niḥsattāsattaṃ niḥsadasan nirasad avyaktam aliṅgaṃ pradhānaṃ tat pratiyanti /
eṣa teṣāṃ liṅgamātraḥ pariṇāmo niḥsattāsattaṃ cāliṅgapariṇāma iti /
aliṅgāvasthāyāṃ na puruṣārtho hetur nāliṅgāvasthāyām ādau puruṣārthatā kāraṇaṃ bhavatīti /
na tasyāḥ puruṣārthatā kāraṇaṃ bhavatīti /
nāsau puruṣārthakṛteti nityākhyāyate /
trayāṇāṃ tv avasthāviśeṣāṇām ādau puruṣārthatā kāraṇaṃ bhavati /
sa cārtho hetur nimittaṃ kāraṇaṃ bhavatīty anityākhyāyate /
guṇās tu sarvadharmānupātino na pratyastam ayante nopajāyante /
vyaktibhir evātītānāgatavyayāgamavatībhir guṇānvayinībhir upajananāpāyadharmakā iva pratyavabhāsante /
yathā devadatto daridrāti /
kasmāt yato 'sya mriyante gāva iti, gavām eva maraṇāt tasya daridrāṇaṃ na svarūpahānād iti samaḥ samādhiḥ /
liṅgamātram aliṅgasya pratyāsannaṃ, tatra tat saṃsṛṣṭaṃ vivicyate kramān ativṛttayaḥ /
tathā ṣaḍ aviśeṣā liṅgamātre saṃsṛṣṭā vivicyante pariṇāmakramaniyamāt /
tathā teṣv aviśeṣeṣu bhūtendriyāṇi saṃsṛṣṭāni vivicyante /
tathā coktaṃ purastāt /
na viśeṣebhyaḥ paraṃ tattvāntaram astīti viśeṣāṇāṃ nāsti tattvāntarapariṇāmaḥ /
teṣāṃ tu dharmalakṣaṇāvasthāpariṇāmā vyākhyāyiṣyante

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: intermediate · types: consciousness-mind, body-layers, world-fate_

terms: [guṇa-parvan](../terms/guna-parvan.md), [viśeṣa](../terms/visesa.md), [aviśeṣa](../terms/avisesa.md), [liṅga-mātra](../terms/linga-matra.md), [aliṅga](../terms/alinga.md), [mahat](../terms/mahat.md), [tanmātra](../terms/tanmatra.md), [asmitā-mātra](../terms/asmita-matra.md), [pradhāna](../terms/pradhana.md), [manas](../terms/manas.md), [mahābhūta](../terms/mahabhuta.md), [indriya](../terms/indriya.md) · concepts: [The levels of the guṇas (guṇa-parvan)](../concepts/guna-parvan.md), [The three guṇas](../concepts/three-gunas.md), [Prakṛti (primordial nature)](../concepts/prakrti.md)

### 2.20 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-20"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

'Seeing alone' means the power of the seer (dṛk-śakti) itself, untouched by any qualifier. That puruṣa is the witness (pratisaṃvedin) of the buddhi; he is neither of the same form as the buddhi nor utterly different. Not the same: the buddhi changes, since its objects are known or unknown; the puruṣa always has a known object, which shows that he is unchanging. The buddhi is for another (acts by combination); the puruṣa is for himself. The buddhi, since it determines every object, is threefold in guṇa and so non-conscious; the puruṣa is the overseer (upadraṣṭṛ) of the guṇas. Not utterly different: though pure, he looks on the cognitions (pratyaya) of the buddhi; looking at them, though not of their nature, he appears as if of their nature. It is said: 'The power of the enjoyer is unchanging and non-transferring (apratisaṃkramā); in the changing object it acts as if transferred and follows its activity; of that activity of the buddhi which has taken on the light of consciousness, by mere likeness the activity of knowing is called undistinguished from the buddhi's activity.'

> dṛśimātra iti dṛkśaktir eva viśeṣaṇāparāmṛṣṭety arthaḥ /
sa puruṣo bhuddheḥ pratisaṃvedī /
sa buddher na sarūpo nātyantaṃ virūpa iti /
na tāvat sarūpaḥ kasmāt /
jñātājñātaviṣayatvāt pariṇāminī hi buddhiḥ /
tasyāś ca viṣayo gavādir ghaṭādir vā jñātaś cājñātaś ceti pariṇāmitvaṃ darśayati /
sadājñātaviṣayatvaṃ tu puruṣasyāpariṇāmitvaṃ paridīpayati kasmāt /
na hi buddhiś ca nāma puruṣaviṣayaś ca syād agṛhītā gṛhītā ceti siddhaṃ puruṣasya sadājñātaviṣayatvaṃ tataś cāpariṇāmitvam iti /
kiṃ ca parārthā buddhiḥ saṃhatyakāritvāt, svārthaḥ puruṣa iti /
tathā sarvārthādhyavasāyakatvāt triguṇā buddhis triguṇatvād acetaneti /
guṇānāṃ tūpadraṣṭā puruṣa ity ato na sarūpaḥ /
astu tarhi virūpa iti /
nātyantaṃ virūpaḥ kasmāt /
śuddho 'py asau pratyayānupaśyo yataḥ /
pratyayaṃ bauddham anupaśyati, tam anupaśyann atadātmāpi tadātmaka iva pratyavabhāsate /
tathā coktam --- apariṇāminī hi bhoktṛśaktir apratisaṃkramā ca pariṇāminy arthe pratisaṃkrānteva tadvṛttim anupatati, tasyāś ca prāptacaitanyopagraharūpāyā buddhivṛtter anukāramātratayā buddhivṛttyaviśiṣṭā hi jñānavṛttir ity ākhyāyate

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: intermediate · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate_

terms: [draṣṭṛ](../terms/drastr.md), [dṛśi-mātra](../terms/drsi-matra.md), [śuddha](../terms/suddha.md), [pratisaṃvedin](../terms/pratisamvedin.md), [buddhi](../terms/buddhi.md), [apariṇāmin](../terms/aparinamin.md), [apratisaṃkramā](../terms/apratisamkrama.md), [bhoktṛ-śakti](../terms/bhoktr-sakti.md), [upadraṣṭṛ](../terms/upadrastr.md), [puruṣa](../terms/purusa.md) · concepts: [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md), [The seer and the seen (draṣṭṛ and dṛśya)](../concepts/seer-and-seen.md), [The witness (the seer as pratisaṃvedin)](../concepts/witness.md)

### 2.21 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-21"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The seen has become an object (karma) of the puruṣa whose form is seeing; so the very being of the seen is for that: it is its own form. Its own form, having its being through another's form, once made to serve enjoyment and liberation, is no longer seen by the puruṣa; by loss of its own form it would be destroyed, but it is not destroyed.

> dṛśirūpasya puruṣasya karmarūpatām āpannaṃ dṛśyam iti tadartha eva dṛśyasyātmā bhavati /
svarūpaṃ bhavatīty arthaḥ /
svarūpaṃ tu pararūpeṇa pratilabdhātmakaṃ bhogāpavargārthatāyāṃ kṛtāyāṃ puruṣeṇa na dṛśyata iti /
svarūpahānād asya nāśaḥ prāpto na tu vinaśyati

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: intermediate · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [dṛśya](../terms/drsya.md), [ātman](../terms/atman.md) · concepts: [The seen exists for the seer (bhoga and apavarga)](../concepts/purpose-of-prakrti.md), [The seer and the seen (draṣṭṛ and dṛśya)](../concepts/seer-and-seen.md)

### 2.22 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-22"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

For the one puruṣa whose purpose is done, the seen, though ceased, has not ceased, since it is common to other puruṣas. For the skilful puruṣa it has come to ceasing; but for the unskilful ones, whose purpose is not done, it obtains its own form through another's form, having become the object of their seeing. So, since the two powers, of seeing and of sight, are eternal, the conjunction has been explained as beginningless. And it is said: 'From the beginningless conjunction of the property-bearers, the conjunction of the mere properties too is beginningless.'

> kṛtārtham ekaṃ puruṣaṃ prati dṛśyaṃ naṣṭam api nāśaṃ prāptam apy anaṣṭaṃ tadanyapuruṣasādhāraṇatvāt /
kuśalaṃ puruṣaṃ prati nāśaṃ prāptam apy akuśalān puruṣān prati na kṛtārtham iti teṣāṃ dṛśeḥ karmaviṣayatām āpannaṃ labhata eva pararūpeṇātmarūpam iti /
ataś ca dṛgdarśanaśaktyor nityatvād anādiḥ saṃyogo vyākhyāta iti /
tathā coktam --- dharmiṇām anādisaṃyogād dharmamātrāṇām apy anādiḥ saṃyoga iti. 2.22
saṃyogasvarūpābhidhitsayedaṃ sūtraṃ pravavṛte ---

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, world-fate_

terms: [kṛtārtha](../terms/krtartha.md), [sādhāraṇa](../terms/sadharana.md), [saṃyoga](../terms/samyoga.md), [anādi](../terms/anadi.md), [dharmin](../terms/dharmin.md), [puruṣa](../terms/purusa.md) · concepts: [The seen exists for the seer (bhoga and apavarga)](../concepts/purpose-of-prakrti.md), [Plurality of puruṣas](../concepts/plurality-of-purusas.md), [The conjunction of seer and seen (saṃyoga)](../concepts/samyoga.md) · disputes: [Are the individual souls one with Brahman/God, eternally distinct from him and from one another, or both different and non-different?](../debates/souls-one-or-distinct.md)

### 2.23 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-23"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence low

The puruṣa, the master, is conjoined with his own seen for the sake of seeing. The apprehension of the seen from that conjunction is enjoyment (bhoga); the apprehension of the seer's own form is liberation (apavarga). The conjunction ends when the work of seeing ends; so seeing is said to be the cause of disjunction; and seeing is the opposite of non-seeing (adarśana), so non-seeing is said to be the occasion of the conjunction. Here seeing is not the cause of liberation as something produced: by the mere absence of non-seeing there is absence of bondage, and that is liberation. When seeing arises, non-seeing, the cause of bondage, is destroyed; so seeing, that is knowledge, is called the cause of kaivalya. The bhāṣya then asks what this non-seeing is, and sets out views found in the śāstra: whether it is the guṇas' office (adhikāra); or the non-arising of the principal mind (pradhāna-citta) of the master whose form is seeing and to whom objects have been shown, that is, the absence of seeing while his own seen is present; or the purposiveness (arthavattā) of the guṇas; or ignorance stilled together with its own mind, the seed of the mind's arising; or the manifestation of the impression of movement when the impression of stillness is exhausted (on which a quotation says the pradhāna, if only still, would not be pradhāna, and if only moving, would not be pradhāna; its functioning gets the name of pradhāna in both ways). Some say seeing-power itself is non-seeing, on the authority of a scripture that the pradhāna's activity is for the sake of making the self known; some say non-seeing is a property of both; some say knowledge itself is non-seeing. These are the alternatives found in the śāstra; the plurality of alternatives concerns the conjunction of all puruṣas with the guṇas in general.

> puruṣaḥ svāmī dṛśyena svena darśanārthaṃ saṃyuktaḥ /
tasmāt saṃyogād dṛśyasyopalabdhir yā sa bhogaḥ /
yā tu draṣṭuḥ svarūpopalabdhiḥ so 'pavargaḥ /
darśanakāryāvasānaḥ saṃyoga iti darśanaṃ viyogasya kāraṇam uktam /
darśanam adarśanasya pratidvandvīty adarśanaṃ saṃyoganimittam uktam /
nātra darśanaṃ mokṣakāraṇam adarśanābhāvād eva bandhābhāvaḥ sa mokṣa iti /
darśanasya bhāve bandhakāraṇasyādarśanasya nāśa ity ato darśanaṃ jñānaṃ kaivalyakāraṇam uktam /
kiṃcedam adarśanaṃ nāma, kiṃ guṇānām adhikāra āhosvid dṛśirūpasya svāmino darśitaviṣayasya pradhānacittasyānutpādaḥ /
svasmin dṛśye vidyamāne yo darśanābhāvaḥ /
kim arthavattā guṇānām /
athāvidyā svacittena saha niruddhā svacittasyotpattibījam /
kiṃ sthitisaṃskārakṣaye gatisaṃskārābhivyaktiḥ /
yatredam uktaṃ pradhānaṃ sthityaiva vartamānaṃ vikārākaraṇād apradhānaṃ syāt /
tathā gatyaiva vartamānaṃ vikāranityatvād apradhānaṃ syāt /
ubhayathā cāsya vṛttiḥ pradhānavyavahāraṃ labhate nānyathā /
kāraṇāntareṣv api kalpiteṣv eva samānaś carcaḥ /
darśanaśaktir evādarśanam ity eke, "pradhānasyātmakhyāpanārthā pravṛttiḥ" iti śruteḥ /
sarvabodhyabodhasamarthaḥ prākpravṛtteḥ puruṣo na paśyati sarvakāryakaraṇasamarthaṃ dṛśyaṃ tadā na dṛśyata iti /
ubhayasyāpy adarśanaṃ dharma ity eke /
tatredaṃ dṛśyasya svātmabhūtam api puruṣapratyayāpekṣaṃ darśanaṃ dṛśyadharmatvena bhavati /
tathā puruṣasyānātmabhūtam api dṛśyapratyayāpekṣaṃ puruṣadharmatvenevādarśanam avabhāsate /
darśanaṃ jñānam evādarśanam iti kecid abhidadhati /
ity ete śāstragatā vikalpāḥ /
tatra vikalpabahutvam etat sarvapuruṣāṇāṃ guṇānāṃ saṃyoge sādhāraṇaviṣayam

### 2.24 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-24"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence low

'Ignorance' means the latent tendency (vāsanā) of erroneous knowledge (viparyaya-jñāna). The buddhi, imbued with that tendency, does not reach the discernment of the puruṣa (puruṣa-khyāti), its completed work, and returns still holding its office. But when it does reach that discernment, its office finished and non-seeing ceased, it does not return, since the cause of bondage is absent. Someone [an opponent] makes the point with the tale of the impotent man: the foolish wife says, 'Impotent one, noble sir, my sister has children; why not I?' He replies, 'When I am dead I will beget a child on you.' So the knowledge that exists does not bring about the cessation of the mind; what hope that it will when it has been destroyed? One who is almost a teacher (ācāryadeśīya) replies: is not liberation just the cessation of the buddhi, since with the cause of non-seeing absent, the buddhi ceases? Non-seeing, the cause of bondage, is turned back by seeing. So the cessation of the mind is liberation; why this misplaced confusion of mind?

> viparyayajñānavāsanety arthaḥ /
viparyayajñānavāsanāvāsitā ca na kāryaniṣṭhāṃ puruṣakhyātiṃ buddhiḥ prāpnoti sādhikārā punar āvartate /
sā tu puruṣakhyātiparyavasānāṃ kāryaniṣṭhāṃ prāpnoti, caritādhikārā nivṛttādarśanā bandhakāraṇābhāvān na punar āvartate /
atra kaścit paṇḍakopākhyānenodghāṭayati --- mugdhayā bhāryayābhidhīyate --- paṇḍakāryaputra, apatyavatī me bhaginī kimarthaṃ nāma nāham iti, sa tām āha --- mṛtas te 'ham apatyam utpādayiṣyāmīti /
tathedaṃ vidyamānaṃ jñānaṃ cittanivṛttiṃ na karoti, vinaṣṭaṃ kariṣyatīti kā pratyāśā /
tatrācāryadeśīyo vakti --- nanu buddhinivṛttir eva mokṣo 'darśanakāraṇābhāvād buddhinivṛttiḥ /
tac cādarśanaṃ bandhakāraṇaṃ darśanān nivartate /
tatra cittanivṛttir eva mokṣaḥ, kimartham asthāna evāsya mativibhramaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: polemical · path: knowledge · stage: intermediate · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, dispute_

### 2.25 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-25"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

From the absence of that non-seeing there is the absence of the conjunction of buddhi and puruṣa: the absolute ending of bondage. This is abandonment (hāna). The seer's isolation (kaivalya) is the puruṣa's state of being unmixed, again not conjoined with the guṇas. When the cause of suffering has ceased, the ending of suffering is the abandonment; then, as was said, the puruṣa is established in his own form.

> tasyādarśanasyābhāvād buddhipuruṣasaṃyogābhāva ātyantiko bandhanoparama ity arthaḥ /
etad dhānam /
taddṛśeḥ kaivalyaṃ puruṣasyāmiśrībhāvaḥ punar asaṃyogo guṇair ity arthaḥ /
duḥkhakāraṇanivṛttau duḥkhoparamo hānam, tadā svarūpapratiṣṭhaḥ puruṣa ity uktam. 2.25
atha hānasya kaḥ prāptyupāya iti ---

### 2.26 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-26"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Discerning awareness (viveka-khyāti) is the cognition of the difference between sattva and puruṣa. But it 'floats' (plavate) while false knowledge (mithyā-jñāna) has not ceased. When false knowledge has come to the state of a burnt seed, barren of issue, then for the sattva whose dust of affliction is shaken off, abiding in supreme lucidity and in the supreme consciousness of mastery, the stream of discerning cognition becomes stainless. That unwavering discernment is the means of abandonment. From it, false knowledge attains the burnt-seed state and does not arise again: this is the path of liberation, the means of abandonment.

> sattvapuruṣānyatāpratyayo vivekakhyātiḥ /
sā tv anivṛttamithyājñānā plavate /
yadā mithyājñānaṃ dagdhabījabhāvaṃ vandhyaprasavaṃ saṃpadyate tadā vidhūtakleśarajasaḥ sattvasya pare vaiśāradye parasyāṃ vaśīkārasaṃjñāyāṃ vartamānasya vivekapratyayapravāho nirmalo bhavati /
sā vivekakhyātir aviplavā hānopāyaḥ /
tato mithyājñānasya dagdhabījabhāvopagamaḥ punaś cāprasava ity eṣa mokṣasya mārgo hānasyopāya iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice, consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

### 2.27 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-27"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

'For him' refers back to the one in whom discernment has risen. It is sevenfold when, impurity and covering being gone and no other cognition arising, the discerning person's insight has seven forms: (1) what is to be abandoned is fully known, nothing more to be known; (2) the causes of what is to be abandoned are worn away, nothing more to be worn away; (3) abandonment has been realised by the samādhi of stilling (nirodha-samādhi); (4) the means of abandonment, discernment, has been cultivated. This fourfold is the liberation of insight, accomplished (kāryā vimukti). The liberation of the mind (citta-vimukti) is threefold: (5) the buddhi's office is done; (6) the guṇas, like boulders fallen from a mountain peak, have no footing, turn toward dissolution into their cause, and go to their setting along with it; (7) once dissolved, there is no further arising of them, for lack of purpose. In this state the puruṣa is beyond connection with the guṇas, light of his own form only, stainless, alone (kevalin). The puruṣa who looks on this sevenfold insight of the final stage is called skilful (kuśala); even at the reabsorption of the mind he is liberated and skilful, being beyond the guṇas.

> tasyeti pratyuditakhyāteḥ pratyāmnāyaḥ /
saptadheti aśuddhyāvaraṇamalāpagamāc cittasya pratyayāntarānutpāde sati saptaprakāraiva prajñā vivekino bhavati /
tadyathā --- parijñātaṃ heyaṃ nāsya punaḥ parijñeyam asti /
kṣīṇā heyahetavo na punar eteṣāṃ kṣetavyam asti /
sākṣātkṛtaṃ nirodhasamādhinā hānam /
bhāvito vivekakhyātirūpo hānopāya iti /
eṣā catuṣṭayī kāryā vimuktiḥ prajñāyāḥ /
cittavimuktis tu trayī caritādhikārā buddhiḥ /
guṇā giriśikharataṭacyutā iva grāvāṇo niravasthānāḥ svakāraṇe pralayābhimukhāḥ saha tenāstaṃ gacchanti /
na caiṣāṃ pravilīnānāṃ punar asty utpādaḥ prayojanābhāvād iti /
etasyām avasthāyāṃ guṇasaṃbandhātītaḥ svarūpamātrajyotir amalaḥ kevalī puruṣa iti /
etāṃ saptavidhāṃ prāntabhūmiprajñām anupaśyan puruṣaḥ kuśala ity ākhyāyate /
pratiprasave 'pi cittasya muktaḥ kuśala ity eva bhavati guṇātītatvād iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: realized (saptadhā prānta-bhūmi prajñā) · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

### 2.28 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-28"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The eight limbs of yoga (yogāṅga) are to be taught. By practising them, the impurity (aśuddhi), which is fivefold error (viparyaya), is destroyed; when it is destroyed, right knowledge (samyag-jñāna) becomes manifest. As the means are practised, so the impurity thins; as it wears away, the light of knowledge grows in order with the wearing away; that growth reaches its highest in the discerning awareness (viveka-khyāti), that is, up to the knowledge of the nature of the guṇas and the puruṣa. The practice of the limbs is the cause of separation from impurity, as an axe is of what is cut; and the cause of attaining discerning awareness, as dharma is of pleasure. The śāstra has nine kinds of cause, and a verse gives them: origin (utpatti), support (sthiti), manifestation (abhivyakti), modification (vikāra), cognition (pratyaya), attainment (āpti), separation (viyoga), otherness (anyatva), sustaining (dhṛti). Examples: the mind is the cause of origin for cognition; the puruṣa's purpose is the cause of the mind's support, as food is of the body; light is the cause of manifestation of form, and so is the cognition of form; another object is the cause of modification of the mind, as fire of the cooked; smoke-knowledge is cause of cognition of fire; the practice of the limbs is cause of attainment of discerning awareness, and also of separation from impurity; a goldsmith is cause of otherness for gold; and, in one cognition of a woman, ignorance is cause to deludedness, aversion to pain, attachment to pleasure, knowledge of truth to impartiality; the body is the sustaining cause of the faculties, and so on. Practice of the limbs of yoga is cause in only two ways: separation (from impurity) and attainment (of discernment).

> yogāṅgāny aṣṭāv abhidhāyiṣyamāṇāni /
teṣām anuṣṭhānāt pañcaparvaṇo viparyayasyāśuddhirūpasya kṣayo nāśaḥ /
tatkṣaye samyagjñānasyābhivyaktiḥ /
yathā yathā ca sādhanāny anuṣṭhīyante tathā tathā tanutvam aśuddhir āpadyate /
yathā yathā ca kṣīyate tathā tathā kṣayakramānurodhinī jñānasyāpi dīptir vivardhate /
sā khalv eṣā vivṛddhiḥ prakarṣam anubhavatyā vivekakhyāteḥ, ā guṇapuruṣasvarūpavijñānād ity arthaḥ /
yogāṅgānuṣṭhānam aśuddher viyogakāraṇam /
yathā paraśuś chedyasya /
vivekakhyātes tu prāptikāraṇaṃ yathā dharmaḥ sukhasya nānyathā kāraṇam /
kati caitāni kāraṇāni śāstre bhavanti /
navaivety āha /
tadyathā ---
"utpattisthityabhivyaktivikārapratyayāptayaḥ /
viyogānyatvadhṛtayaḥ kāraṇaṃ navadhā smṛtam //" iti.
tatrotpattikāraṇaṃ mano bhavati vijñānasya, sthitikāraṇaṃ manasaḥ puruṣārthatā, śarīrasyevāhāra iti /
abhivyaktikāraṇaṃ yathā rūpasyālokas tathā rūpajñānam, vikārakāraṇaṃ manaso viṣayāntaram /
yathāgniḥ pākyasya /
pratyayakāraṇaṃ dhūmajñānam agnijñānasya /
prāptikāraṇaṃ yogāṅgānuṣṭhānaṃ vivekakhyāteḥ /
viyogakāraṇaṃ tad evāśuddheḥ /
anyatvakāraṇaṃ yathā suvarṇasya suvarṇakāraḥ /
evam ekasya strīpratyayasyāvidyā mūḍhatve dveṣo duḥkhatve rāgaḥ sukhatve tattvajñānaṃ mādhyasthye /
dhṛtikāraṇaṃ śarīram indriyāṇām /
tāni ca tasya /
mahābhūtāni śarīrāṇām, tāni ca parasparaṃ sarveṣāṃ tairyagyaunamānuṣadaivatāni ca parasparārthatvād ity evaṃ nava kāraṇāni /
tāni ca yathāsaṃbhavaṃ padārthāntareṣv api yojyāni /
yogāṅgānuṣṭhānaṃ tu dvidhaiva kāraṇatvaṃ labhata iti

### 2.30 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-30"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Non-harming (ahiṃsā) is the absence of hostility (anabhidroha) toward all beings, in every way and at all times. The later restraints and observances have it as their root; they are taught for its sake, are taken up to make it pure. It is said: 'As this Brāhmaṇa takes up more and more vows, so, turning away from what is done through heedlessness and has harm as its source, he makes that same non-harming pure of form.' Truthfulness (satya) is speech and mind that are in accord with the fact (yathārtha): as seen, as inferred, so speech and mind. Speech is uttered to pass one's understanding to another; if it is not deceived, mistaken or barren of understanding, it is truthful; it is spoken for the benefit of all beings, not to injure them. If, even when so spoken, it were only a harm to beings, it would not be truth but sin, and one would reach grievous darkness through that mere semblance of merit. So one should speak, after examining, the truth that is for the good of all beings. Stealing (steya) is taking others' goods contrary to the scripture; its prohibition, in the form of absence of longing (aspṛhā), is non-stealing (asteya). Continence (brahmacarya) is restraint of the generative organ in one whose senses are guarded. Non-possessiveness (aparigraha) is non-acceptance of objects, on seeing the faults of acquiring, keeping, losing, attachment and harming in them. These are the restraints.

> tatrāhiṃsā sarvathā sarvadā sarvabhūtānām anabhidrohaḥ /
uttare ca yamaniyamās tanmūlās tatsiddhiparatayaiva tatpratipādanāya pratipādyante /
tadavadātarūpakaraṇāyaivopādīyante /
tathā coktam --- sa khalv ayaṃ brāhmaṇo yathā yathā vratāni bahūni samāditsate tathā tathā pramādakṛtebhyo hiṃsānidānebhyo nivartamānas tām evāvadātarūpām ahiṃsāṃ karoti /
satyaṃ yathārthe vāṅmanase /
yathā dṛṣṭaṃ yathānumitaṃ tathā vāṅ manaś ceti /
paratra svabodhasaṃkrāntaye vāg uktā, sā yadi na vañcitā bhrāntā vā pratipattivandhyā vā bhaved iti /
eṣā sarvabhūtopakārārthaṃ pravṛttā na bhūtopaghātāya /
yadi caivam apy abhidhīyamānā bhūtopaghātaparaiva syān na satyaṃ bhavet pāpam eva bhavet tena puṇyābhāsena puṇyapratirūpakeṇa kaṣṭaṃ tamaḥ prāpnuyāt /
tasmāt parīkṣya sarvabhūtahitaṃ satyaṃ brūyāt /
steyam aśāstrapūrvakaṃ dravyāṇāṃ parataḥ svīkaraṇaṃ, tatpratiṣedhaḥ punar aspṛhārūpam asteyam iti /
brahmacaryaṃ guptendriyasyopasthasya saṃyamaḥ /
viṣayāṇām arjanarakṣaṇakṣayasaṅgahiṃsādoṣadarśanād asvīkaraṇam aparigraha ity ete yamāḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: ethical-social · path: general, meditation · stage: beginner · types: ethics, practice_

### 2.31 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-31"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Non-harming limited by class (jāti) is a fisherman's harming fish only, not elsewhere. Limited by place (deśa): 'I will not kill in a holy place.' Limited by time (kāla): 'I will not kill on the fourteenth day or on a holy day.' Limited by occasion (samaya): one who has stopped harming in those three, saying 'I will kill only for a god or a Brāhmaṇa, not otherwise'; and as with warriors, harm in battle only and not elsewhere. Non-harming and the rest, not limited by these class, place, time and occasion, are to be observed in every way. Being unbroken in all grounds (bhūmi) and all objects, in every way, they are called universal (sārvabhauma), the great vow (mahāvrata).

> tatrāhiṃsā jātyavacchinnā matsyavadhakasya matsyeṣv eva nānyatra hiṃsā /
saiva deśāvacchinnā na tīrthe haniṣyāmīti /
saiva kālāvacchinnā na caturdaśyāṃ na puṇye 'hani haniṣyāmīti /
saiva tribhir uparatasya samayāvacchinnā devabrāhmaṇārthe nānyathā haniṣyāmīti /
yathā ca kṣatriyāṇāṃ yuddha eva hiṃsā nānyatreti /
ebhir jātideśakālasamayair anavacchinnā ahiṃsādayaḥ sarvathaiva paripālanīyāḥ /
sarvabhūmiṣu sarvaviṣayeṣu sarvathaivāviditavyabhicārāḥ sārvabhaumā mahāvratam ity ucyante
