### 2.32 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-32"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

External purity (śauca) is that produced by earth, water and the like, and the eating of what is clean; inner purity is washing away the taints of the mind (citta-mala). Contentment (saṃtoṣa) is not wishing to take more than the means at hand. Austerity (tapas) is bearing the pairs of opposites (dvandva): hunger and thirst, cold and heat, standing and sitting, silence of wood (kāṣṭha-mauna) and silence of gesture (ākāra-mauna); its vows, as fits, are the kṛcchra, cāndrāyaṇa and sāṃtapana and the like. Self-study (svādhyāya) is study of the scriptures of liberation or repetition of the praṇava. Devotion to Īśvara (īśvara-praṇidhāna) is offering all actions to that supreme teacher. A verse is quoted: 'Whether lying, sitting or going on the road, being at ease, his web of harmful thoughts (vitarka) worn away, seeing the wearing away of the seed of saṃsāra, let him be ever united and a sharer in the enjoyment of the immortal.' It is said of this: 'from that comes realization of the inward consciousness and the absence of obstacles' (1.29).

> tatra śaucaṃ mṛjjalādijanitaṃ medhyābhyavaharaṇādi ca bāhyam /
ābhyantaraṃ cittamalānām ākṣālanam /
saṃtoṣaḥ saṃnihitasādhanād adhikasyānupāditsā /
tapo dvaṃdvasahanam /
dvaṃdvāś ca jighatsāpipāse śītoṣṇe sthānāsane kāṣṭhamaunākāramaune ca /
vratāni caiṣāṃ yathāyogaṃ kṛcchracāndrāyaṇasāṃtapanādīni /
svādhyāyo mokṣaśāstrāṇām adhyayanaṃ praṇavajapo vā /
īśvarapraṇidhānaṃ tasmin paramagurau sarvakarmārpaṇam.
śayyāsanastho 'tha pathi vrajan vā svasthaḥ parikṣīṇavitarkajālaḥ /
saṃsārabījakṣayam īkṣamāṇaḥ syān nityayukto 'mṛtabhogabhāgī //
yatredam uktaṃ tataḥ pratyakcetanādhigamo 'py antarāyābhāvaś ceti

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general, meditation, devotion · stage: beginner · types: practice, ethics_

terms: [niyama](../terms/niyama.md), [śauca](../terms/sauca.md), [saṃtoṣa](../terms/santosa.md), [tapas](../terms/tapas.md), [dvandva](../terms/dvandva.md), [svādhyāya](../terms/svadhyaya.md), [īśvara-praṇidhāna](../terms/isvara-pranidhana.md), [citta-mala](../terms/citta-mala.md), [kṛcchra](../terms/krcchra.md), [cāndrāyaṇa](../terms/candrayana.md), [sāṃtapana](../terms/santapana.md) · concepts: [The five observances (niyama)](../concepts/niyamas.md), [Kriyā-yoga, the yoga of action](../concepts/kriya-yoga.md) · practices: [The observances (niyama)](../practices/niyama.md), [Purity (śauca)](../practices/sauca.md), [Contentment (saṃtoṣa)](../practices/santosa.md), [Austerity (tapas)](../practices/tapas.md), [Self-study (svādhyāya)](../practices/svadhyaya.md), [Devotion to Īśvara (īśvara-praṇidhāna)](../practices/isvara-pranidhana.md)

### 2.33 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-33"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

When harmful thoughts (vitarka) such as violence arise in this Brāhmaṇa — 'I will kill the one who has wronged me; I will tell a lie; I will take his goods; I will lie with his wives; I will become owner of his possessions' — then, oppressed in this way by the fever of thoughts (vitarka-jvara) inclined to the wrong road and greatly inflamed, he should cultivate their opposites. He should think: 'I, being cooked in the terrible coals of saṃsāra, have come for refuge to the dharma of yoga, by giving safety (abhaya) to all beings; if, having abandoned the harmful thoughts, I take them up again, I am like a dog's behaviour: as a dog licks what it has vomited, so am I, taking up what I have given up.' The same is to be applied to the other sūtras.

> yadāsya brāhmaṇasya hiṃsādayo vitarkā jāyeran haniṣyāmy aham apakāriṇam anṛtam api vakṣyāmi dravyam apy asya svīkariṣyāmi dāreṣu cāsya vyavāyī bhaviṣyāmi parigraheṣu cāsya svāmī bhaviṣyāmīti /
evam unmārgapravaṇavitarkajvareṇātidīptena bādhyamānas tatpratipakṣān bhāvayet /
ghoreṣu saṃsārāṅgāreṣu pacyamānena mayā śaraṇam upāgataḥ sarvabhūtābhayapradānena yogadharmaḥ /
sa khalv ahaṃ tyaktvā vitarkān punas tān ādadānas tulyaḥ śvavṛtteneti bhāvayet /
yathā śvā vāntāvalehī tathā tyaktasya punar ādadāna iti /
evamādi sūtrāntareṣv api yojyam

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: general, meditation · stage: beginner · types: practice, ethics, consciousness-mind_

terms: [vitarka](../terms/vitarka.md), [pratipakṣa-bhāvanā](../terms/pratipaksa-bhavana.md), [vitarka-jvara](../terms/vitarka-jvara.md), [abhaya-dāna](../terms/abhaya-dana.md), [yogadharma](../terms/yogadharma.md), [śvavṛtti](../terms/svavrtti.md) · concepts: [Cultivating the opposite (pratipakṣa-bhāvanā)](../concepts/pratipaksa-bhavana.md), [The five restraints (yama)](../concepts/yamas.md) · practices: [Cultivating the opposite (pratipakṣa-bhāvanā)](../practices/pratipaksa-bhavana.md) · obstacles: [Harmful thoughts (vitarka): violence and the rest](../obstacles/vitarka-himsadi.md)

### 2.34 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-34"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Violence is threefold: done (kṛta), caused to be done (kārita), approved (anumodita). Each is again threefold, from greed (lobha, e.g. for meat or skin), from anger ('he wronged me'), or from delusion ('it will be dharma for me'); greed, anger and delusion are each mild, middling or intense. So violence has twenty-seven forms; the mild, middling and intense are again each threefold, making eighty-one; and with rules, options and combinations it is countless, since the kinds of living beings are countless. The same applies to untruth and the rest. 'Suffering and ignorance without end as fruit' is the cultivation of the opposite: the violent one first takes away the victim's strength, then torments him by the fall of a weapon, then deprives him of life. From robbing strength, his own animate and inanimate implements lose strength; from causing suffering he suffers in hells, animal births and ghost-states; and from robbing life, moment by moment at the end of his own life, wishing for death, he still breathes somehow, because the ripening of suffering is fixed. If somehow such violence goes with merit, then in gaining pleasure he will have a short life. The same is to be applied, as fits, to untruth and the rest. Thus contemplating the unwanted ripening that follows harmful thoughts, one should not set the mind on them.

> tatra hiṃsā tāvat --- kṛtā kāritānumoditeti tridhā /
ekaikā punas tridhā lobhena māṃsacarmārthena krodhenāpakṛtam aneneti mohena dharmo me bhaviṣyatīti /
lobhakrodhamohāḥ punas trividhā mṛdumadhyādhimātrā iti /
evaṃ saptaviṃśatir bhedā bhavanti hiṃsāyāḥ /
mṛdumadhyādhimātrāḥ punas tridhā --- mṛdumṛdur madhyamṛdus tīvramṛdur iti /
tathā mṛdumadhyo madhyamadhyas tīvramadhya iti /
tathā mṛdutīvro madhyatīvro 'dhimātratīvra iti /
evam ekāśītibhedā hiṃsā bhavati /
sā punar niyamavikalpasamuccayabhedād asaṃkhyeyā, prāṇabhṛdbhedasyāparisaṃkhyeyatvād iti /
evam anṛtādiṣv api yojyam /
te khalv amī vitarkā duḥkhājñānānantaphalā iti pratipakṣabhāvanam /
duḥkham ajñānaṃ cānantaṃ phalaṃ yeṣām iti pratipakṣabhāvanam /
tathā ca hiṃsakas tāvat prathamaṃ vadhyasya vīryam ākṣipati /
tataś ca śastrādinipātena duḥkhayati /
tato jīvitād api mocayati /
tato vīryākṣepād asya cetanācetanam upakaraṇaṃ kṣīṇavīryaṃ bhavati /
duḥkhotpādān narakatiryakpretādiṣu duḥkham anubhavati, jīvitavyaparopaṇāt pratikṣaṇaṃ ca jīvitātyaye vartamāno maraṇam icchann api duḥkhavipākasya niyatavipākavedanīyatvāt kathaṃcid evocchvasiti /
yadi ca kathaṃcit puṇyāvāpagatā hiṃsā bhavet tatra sukhaprāptau bhaved alpāyur iti /
evam anṛtādiṣv api yojyaṃ yathāsaṃbhavam /
evaṃ vitarkāṇāṃ cāmum evānugataṃ vipākam aniṣṭaṃ bhāvayan na vitarkeṣu manaḥ praṇidadhīta. 2.34
pratipakṣabhāvanād dhetor heyā vitarkā yadāsya syur aprasavadharmāṇas tadā tatkṛtam aiśvaryaṃ yoginaḥ siddhisūcakaṃ bhavati /
tadyathā ---

### 2.40 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-40"></a>

One who begins purity, with disgust for his own body, sees the faults of the body and does not cling to it (kāya-anabhiṣvaṅgin): he becomes a striver (yati). And non-contact with others: seeing the nature of the body, wishing to be rid even of his own body, and seeing no purity of the body even while washing it with earth, water and the like, how could he be in utter contact with the bodies of others, which are so unclean?

### 2.47 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-47"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

'It is accomplished' completes the sentence. Posture is accomplished by the cessation of effort (prayatna), so that there is no shaking of the limbs; or the mind, having coalesced with the endless, brings posture about.

> bhavatīti vākyaśeṣaḥ prayatnoparamāt sidhyaty āsanaṃ yena nāṅgamejayo bhavati /
anante vā samāpannaṃ cittam āsanaṃ nirvartayatīti

### 2.49 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-49"></a>

When mastery of posture is present: inhalation (śvāsa) is the drawing in of outer air; exhalation (praśvāsa) is the expelling of the air in the belly; the cutting off of the course of the two, the absence of both, is prāṇāyāma.

> saty āsanajaye bāhyasya vāyor ācamanaṃ śvāsaḥ, kauṣṭhyasya vāyor niḥsāraṇaṃ praśvāsaḥ, tayor gativiccheda ubhayābhāvaḥ prāṇāyāmaḥ. 2.49

### 2.49-51 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-49-51"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

When posture is mastered, inhalation is the drawing in of outer air and exhalation the expelling of inner air; the cutting off of their movement, the absence of both, is prāṇāyāma. It is external (absence of movement after exhalation), internal (after inhalation), or a third, stationary one, in which both cease at once through a single effort, as water on a hot stone contracts on all sides. Each is observed by place (its range), time (a measure of moments) and number (of breaths), as mild, middling or intense, and so practised becomes long and subtle. The fourth comes gradually through mastery of the stages, after the spheres of inhalation and exhalation have been ascertained and passed beyond.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: intermediate · types: practice, body-layers_

terms: [prāṇāyāma](../terms/pranayama.md) · practices: [Breath-regulation (prāṇāyāma) in the Yoga Sūtra](../practices/pranayama.md), [The fourth prāṇāyāma (YS 2.51)](../practices/caturtha-pranayama.md)

_Superseded by [2.49](yoga-bhasya.md#tea-yoga-bhasya-2-49)_

### 2.50 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-50"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high · _restricted: summary only_

The external (bāhya) is the absence of movement that follows exhalation; the internal (ābhyantara) is the absence of movement that follows inhalation; the third, the suspended operation (stambha-vṛtti), is where both are absent, brought about by a single effort — as water dropped on a hot stone shrinks on all sides, so movement of both stops at once. All three are said to be regulated (paridṛṣṭa) by place (deśa), time (kāla) and number (saṃkhyā); Vyāsa glosses each only in general terms (the extent of its field, a limit in moments, and a count of breaths in successive 'strokes' (udghāta), graded mild, middling and intense) and gives no figures. Practised so, it becomes long and subtle.

> yatra praśvāsapūrvako gatyabhāvaḥ sa bāhyaḥ /
yatra śvāsapūrvako gatyabhāvaḥ sa ābhyantaraḥ /
tṛtīyaḥ stambhavṛttir yatrobhayābhāvaḥ sakṛṭprayatnād bhavati /
yathā tapte nyastam upale jalaṃ sarvataḥ saṃkocam āpadyate tathā dvayor yugapad gatyabhāva iti /
trayo 'py ete deśena paridṛṣṭā iyān asya viṣayo deśa iti /
kālena paridṛṣṭāḥ kṣaṇānām iyattāvadhāraṇenāvacchinnā ity arthaḥ /
saṃkhyābhiḥ paridṛṣṭā etāvadbhiḥ śvāsapraśvāsaiḥ prathama udghātas tadvan nigṛhītasyaitāvadbhir dvitīya udghāta evaṃ tṛtīyaḥ /
evaṃ mṛdur evaṃ madhya evaṃ tīvra iti saṃkhyāparidṛṣṭaḥ /
sa khalv ayam evam abhyasto dīrghasūkṣmaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: intermediate · types: practice, body-layers_

terms: [bāhya](../terms/bahya.md), [ābhyantara](../terms/abhyantara.md), [stambha-vṛtti](../terms/stambha-vrtti.md), [kāla](../terms/kala.md), [saṃkhyā](../terms/sankhya.md), [udghāta](../terms/udghata.md), [dīrgha](../terms/dirgha.md), [sūkṣma](../terms/suksma.md), [prāṇāyāma](../terms/pranayama.md) · concepts: [The eight limbs of yoga (aṣṭāṅga)](../concepts/astanga-yoga.md), [The five vital airs in Yoga](../concepts/prana-vayus-yoga.md) · practices: [Breath-regulation (prāṇāyāma) in the Yoga Sūtra](../practices/pranayama.md)

### 2.51 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-51"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate · _restricted: summary only_

The external, fixed by place, time and number, is surpassed (ākṣipta); so too the internal, fixed by place, time and number, is surpassed; in both ways it is long and subtle. Preceded by that, by mastery of the ground (bhūmi-jaya), the gradual absence of movement of both is the fourth prāṇāyāma. The third differs: its stopping is begun once, without attention to an object, regulated by place, time and number, long and subtle. The fourth is the stopping preceded by the surpassing of both, gradually, by mastery of ground, after determining the object of inhalation and exhalation; this is the difference.

> deśakālasaṃkhyābhir bāhyaviṣayaparidṛṣṭa ākṣiptaḥ /
tathābhyantaraviṣayaparidṛṣṭa ākṣiptaḥ /
ubhayathā dīrghasūkṣmaḥ /
tatpūrvako bhūmijayāt krameṇobhayor gatyabhāvaś caturthaḥ prāṇāyāmaḥ /
tṛtīyas tu viṣayānālocito gatyabhāvaḥ sakṛdārabdha eva deśakālasaṃkhyābhiḥ paridṛṣṭo dīrghasūkṣmaḥ /
caturthas tu śvāsapraśvāsayor viṣayāvadhāraṇāt krameṇa bhūmijayād ubhayākṣepapūrvako gatyabhāvaś caturthaḥ prāṇāyāma ity ayaṃ viśeṣa iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: body-breath · stage: advanced · types: practice, body-layers_

terms: [caturtha-prāṇāyāma](../terms/caturtha-pranayama.md), [ākṣepa](../terms/aksepa.md), [bhūmi-jaya](../terms/bhumi-jaya.md), [gati-abhāva](../terms/gati-abhava.md), [kāla](../terms/kala.md), [saṃkhyā](../terms/sankhya.md) · concepts: [The eight limbs of yoga (aṣṭāṅga)](../concepts/astanga-yoga.md), [The five vital airs in Yoga](../concepts/prana-vayus-yoga.md) · practices: [The fourth prāṇāyāma (YS 2.51)](../practices/caturtha-pranayama.md), [Breath-regulation (prāṇāyāma) in the Yoga Sūtra](../practices/pranayama.md)

### 2.52 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-52"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

For this yogin who practises prāṇāyāma, the karma that covers the knowledge of discernment (viveka-jñāna) is worn away. It is said of it: 'Sattva, whose nature is light, is covered by a great magic net made of great delusion (mahāmoha-maya indrajāla), and is employed in what should not be done.' By the practice of prāṇāyāma this light-covering karma, which binds one to saṃsāra, becomes weak and is worn away moment by moment. It is also said: 'There is no austerity higher than prāṇāyāma; from it is the purification of taints and the shining of knowledge.'

> prāṇāyāmān abhyasyato 'sya yoginaḥ kṣīyate vivekajñānāvaraṇīyaṃ karma /
yat tad ācakṣate /
mahāmohamayenendrajālena prakāśaśīlaṃ sattvam āvṛtya tad evākārye niyuṅkta iti /
tad asya prakāśāvaraṇaṃ karma saṃsāranibandhanaṃ prāṇāyāmābhyāsād durbalaṃ bhavati pratikṣaṇaṃ ca kṣīyate /
tathā coktam /
"tapo na paraṃ prāṇāyāmāt tato viśuddhir malānāṃ dīptiś ca jñānasya" iti. 2.52
kiṃca ---

### 2.54 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-54"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

When there is no contact with their own objects, the senses, as if imitating the mind's own form: when the mind is stilled (citta-nirodha) the senses too are stilled, like the mind; they do not need another means, as the mastery of the other senses does. As bees fly up after the king of the bees (madhukara-rāja) when he flies up and settle when he settles, so the senses, when the mind is stilled, are stilled: this is sense-withdrawal.

> svaviṣayasaṃprayogābhāve cittasvarūpānukāra iveti, cittanirodhe cittavanniruddhānīndriyāṇi netarendriyajayavadupāyāntaram apekṣante /
yathā madhukararājaṃ makṣikā utpatantam anūtpatanti niviśamānam anu niviśante tathendriyāṇi cittanirodhe niruddhānīty eṣa pratyāhāraḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [pratyāhāra](../terms/pratyahara.md), [indriya](../terms/indriya.md), [citta-nirodha](../terms/citta-nirodha.md), [madhukara-rāja](../terms/madhukara-raja.md) · concepts: [The eight limbs of yoga (aṣṭāṅga)](../concepts/astanga-yoga.md) · practices: [Sense-withdrawal (pratyāhāra) in the Yoga Sūtra](../practices/pratyahara.md)

### 2.55 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-2-55"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Some say mastery of the senses is non-addiction (avyasana) to sound and the other objects: attachment (sakti) is 'addiction' (vyasana) since it throws (vyasyati) one off the better (śreyas); a course of action that does not conflict [with that] is right. Others say it is contact with sound and the rest at one's own wish. Some say it is knowledge of sound and the rest that is empty of pleasure and pain, when attachment and aversion are absent. Jaigīṣavya says it is simply non-engagement (apratipatti) through one-pointedness of the mind. From that, this mastery is supreme: for the yogin the senses, stilled when the mind is stilled, do not need another means made by effort, as mastery of the other senses does.

> śabdādiṣv avyasanam indriyajaya iti kecit /
saktir vyasanaṃ vyasyaty enaṃ śreyasa iti /
aviruddhā pratipattir nyāyyā /
śabdādisaṃprayogaḥ svecchayety anye /
rāgadveṣābhāve sukhaduḥkhaśūnyaṃ śabdādijñānam indriyajaya iti kecit /
cittaikāgryād apratipattir eveti jaigīṣavyaḥ /
tataś ca paramā tv iyaṃ vaśyatā yac cittanirodhe niruddhānīndriyāṇi netarendriyajayavat prayatnakṛtam upāyāntaram apekṣante yogina iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: polemical · path: meditation · stage: intermediate · types: practice, consciousness-mind, dispute_

(YBh 2.55)](../debates/what-is-mastery-of-senses.md), [What is mastery of the senses (indriya-jaya)?](../debates/mastery-of-the-senses.md)

### 3.1 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The bhāṣya says the binding is of the mind by its activity alone (vṛtti-mātra), either to places such as the navel wheel (nābhicakra), the lotus of the heart (hṛdaya-puṇḍarīka), the light in the head (mūrdhan jyotis), the tip of the nose or the tip of the tongue, or to an external object; this is concentration.

> nābhicakre hṛdayapuṇḍarīke mūrdhni jyotiṣi nāsikāgre jihvāgra ity evamādiṣu deśeṣu bāhye vā viṣaye cittasya vṛttimātreṇa bandha iti dhāraṇā.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: unmarked · types: practice, body-layers_

terms: [dhāraṇā](../terms/dharana.md), [citta](../terms/citta.md), [nābhi-cakra](../terms/nabhi-cakra.md), [mūrdha-jyotis](../terms/murdha-jyotis.md) · concepts: [Bodily places named for saṃyama and concentration](../concepts/bodily-loci-of-samyama.md) · practices: [Dhāraṇā (concentration)](../practices/dharana.md)

### 3.2 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

In that place, the one-pointedness of the cognition that has the object of meditation as its support, a like flow (sadṛśa pravāha) not touched by any other cognition, is meditation.

> tasmin deśe dhyeyālambanasya pratyayasyaikatānatā sadṛśaḥ pravāhaḥ pratyayāntareṇāparāmṛṣṭo dhyānam.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: unmarked · types: practice_

terms: [dhyāna](../terms/dhyana.md), [pratyaya](../terms/pratyaya.md) · concepts: [The eight limbs of yoga (aṣṭāṅga)](../concepts/astanga-yoga.md) · practices: [Meditation (dhyāna) as the means of abandoning the afflictions' activities](../practices/dhyana.md)

### 3.3 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

When meditation itself shines only as the form of the object meditated on, and is as if empty of its own nature, which consists of cognition (pratyaya), through entering into the nature of the object meditated on, it is then called samādhi.

> dhyānam eva dhyeyākārānirbhāsaṃ pratyayātmakena svarūpeṇa śūnyam iva yadā bhavati dhyeyasvabhāvāveśāt tadā samādhir ity ucyate.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: unmarked · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md), [dhyāna](../terms/dhyana.md) · concepts: [The eight limbs of yoga (aṣṭāṅga)](../concepts/astanga-yoga.md) · practices: [Samādhi (in the Vibhūti-pāda account)](../practices/samadhi.md)

### 3.4 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

This triad of concentration, meditation and samādhi on a single object is saṃyama: three means with one object are called saṃyama, and 'saṃyama' is the technical term (paribhāṣā) of this treatise for the three.

> tad etad dhāraṇādhyānasamādhitrayam ekatra saṃyamaḥ /
ekaviṣayāṇi trīṇi sādhanāni saṃyama ity ucyate /
tad asya trayasya tāntrikī paribhāṣā saṃyama iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: unmarked · types: practice_

terms: [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [dhāraṇā](../terms/dharana.md), [dhyāna](../terms/dhyana.md), [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md) · concepts: [Saṃyama](../concepts/samyama.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.5 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

From mastery of saṃyama comes the light of the insight born of samādhi (samādhi-prajñā); the more firmly saṃyama takes its footing (sthira-pada), the clearer (viśāradī) the samādhi-insight becomes.

> tasya saṃyamasya jayāt samādhiprajñāyā bhavaty āloko yathā yathā saṃyamaḥ sthirapado bhavati tathā tathā samādhiprajñā viśāradī bhavati.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [Prājña](../terms/prajna.md) · concepts: [Saṃyama](../concepts/samyama.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.6 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-6"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Saṃyama is applied to the stage that comes immediately next after the stage mastered. One who has not mastered the lower stage and, skipping the next, tries saṃyama on the far stages does not obtain it, and then how could he have the light of insight? Of one who through the grace of Īśvara (īśvara-prasāda) has already mastered a higher stage, saṃyama on lower stages, such as knowledge of another's mind, is not fitting, since that purpose is known in another way. That this stage is next to that one, in this yoga itself is the teacher (upādhyāya); as it is said: 'By yoga yoga is to be known; yoga advances out of yoga; he who is unheedful (apramatta) through yoga delights long in yoga.'

> tasya saṃyamasya jitabhūmer yānantarā bhūmis tatra viniyogaḥ /
na hy ajitādharabhūmir anantarabhūmiṃ vilaṅghya prāntabhūmiṣu saṃyamaṃ labhate /
tadabhāvāc ca kutas tasya prajñālokaḥ /
īśvaraprasādāj jitottarabhūmikasya ca nādharabhūmiṣu paracittajñānādiṣu saṃyamo yuktaḥ /
kasmāt, tadarthasyānyata evāvagatatvāt /
bhūmer asyā iyam anantarā bhūmir ity atra yoga evopādhyāyaḥ /
katham evaṃ hy uktam /
"yogena yogo jñātavyo yogo yogāt pravartate / yo 'pramattas tu yogena sa yoge ramate ciram" iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice, teacher-transmission_

terms: [bhūmi](../terms/bhumi.md), [yoga](../terms/yoga.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md) · concepts: [Saṃyama](../concepts/samyama.md), [Īśvara, Hiraṇyagarbha and the transmission of Yoga](../concepts/isvara-as-first-teacher.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.7 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-7"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

This triad of concentration, meditation and samādhi is inner to samprajñāta samādhi, in relation to the five earlier means beginning with the restraints (yama).

> tad etad dhāraṇādhyānasamādhitrayam antaraṅgaṃ saṃprajñātasya samādheḥ pūrvebhyo yamādibhyaḥ pañcabhyaḥ sādhanebhya iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: unmarked · types: practice_

terms: [antaraṅga](../terms/antaranga.md), [dhāraṇā](../terms/dharana.md), [dhyāna](../terms/dhyana.md), [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md), [yama](../terms/yama.md) · concepts: [Inner and outer limbs](../concepts/antaranga-bahiranga.md), [The eight limbs of yoga (aṣṭāṅga)](../concepts/astanga-yoga.md)

### 3.8 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-8"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Even that inner triad of means is outer to seedless yoga; why? Because [seedless yoga] occurs when they are absent.

> tad apy antaraṅgaṃ sādhanatrayaṃ nirbījasya yogasya bahiraṅgaṃ bhavati kasmāt, tadabhāve bhāvād iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice, consciousness-mind_

terms: [bahiraṅga](../terms/bahiranga.md), [nirbīja](../terms/nirbija.md), [samprajñāta](../terms/samprajnata.md) · concepts: [Inner and outer limbs](../concepts/antaranga-bahiranga.md), [Samādhi with seed and seedless (sabīja, nirbīja)](../concepts/sabija-nirbija.md)

### 3.9 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-9"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The impressions of emergence are properties of the mind, not of the nature of cognition (pratyaya); so when cognitions are restrained they are not restrained; the impressions of restraint are also properties of the mind. Between these two there is suppression and emergence: the impressions of emergence dwindle, the impressions of restraint are laid down. The mind follows into the moment of restraint; this changing (anyathātva) of the impressions in one mind moment by moment is the transformation of restraint. The mind then has only impressions left (saṃskāra-śeṣa), as explained in [the account of] restraint-samādhi.

> vyutthānasaṃskārāś cittadharmā na te pratyayātmakā iti pratyayanirodhe na niruddhā nirodhasaṃskārā api cittadharmās tayor abhibhavaprādurbhāvau vyutthānasaṃskārā hīyante nirodhasaṃskārā ādhīyante /
nirodhakṣaṇaṃ cittam anveti tad ekasya cittasya pratikṣaṇam idaṃ saṃskārānyathātvaṃ nirodhapariṇāmaḥ /
tadā saṃskāraśeṣaṃ cittam iti nirodhasamādhau vyākhyātam.

_level: unmarked · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [nirodha-pariṇāma](../terms/nirodha-parinama.md), [saṃskāra](../terms/samskara.md), [vyutthāna](../terms/vyutthana.md), [nirodha](../terms/nirodha.md) · concepts: [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md), [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md), [Samādhi beyond object-awareness (asamprajñāta)](../concepts/asamprajnata-samadhi.md)

### 3.10 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-10"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The mind's peaceful flow depends on skill gained through practice of the restraint-impressions; when that impression is weak, the impression whose property is restraint is overpowered by the impression whose property is emergence (vyutthāna-dharmin).

> nirodhasaṃskārābhyāsapāṭavāpekṣā praśāntavāhitā cittasya bhavati /
tatsaṃskāramāndye vyutthānadharmiṇā saṃskāreṇa nirodhadharmasaṃskāro 'bhibhūyata iti.

_level: unmarked · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [praśānta-vāhitā](../terms/prasanta-vahita.md), [saṃskāra](../terms/samskara.md) · concepts: [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md), [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md)

### 3.11 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-11"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

All-objectness is a property of the mind and one-pointedness is also a property of the mind; the dwindling of all-objectness means its concealment (tirobhāva), the arising of one-pointedness means its manifestation (āvirbhāva). The mind, as the property-bearer following both properties, which are its own nature, is brought into samādhi; this is the samādhi-transformation of the mind.

> sarvārthatā cittadharmaḥ /
ekāgratāpi cittadharmaḥ /
sarvārthatāyāḥ kṣayas tirobhāva ity arthaḥ /
ekāgratāyā udaya āvirbhāva ity arthaḥ /
tayor dharmitvenānugataṃ cittaṃ, tad idaṃ cittam apāyopajanayoḥ svātmabhūtayor dharmayor anugataṃ samādhīyate sa cittasya samādhipariṇāmaḥ.

_level: unmarked · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [samādhi-pariṇāma](../terms/samadhi-parinama.md), [ekāgratā](../terms/ekagrata.md) · concepts: [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md)

### 3.12 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-12"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

For one whose mind is concentrated, the former cognition is calm and the next, like it, has arisen; the samādhi-mind follows both, and so again and again until samādhi breaks. This is the one-pointedness transformation of the mind as property-bearer.

> samāhitacittasya pūrvapratyayaḥ śānta uttaras tatsadṛśa uditaḥ, samādhicittam ubhayor anugataṃ punas tathaivāsamādhibhreṣād iti /
sa khalv ayaṃ dharmiṇaś cittasyaikāgratāpariṇāmaḥ.

_level: unmarked · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [ekāgratā-pariṇāma](../terms/ekagrata-parinama.md), [pratyaya](../terms/pratyaya.md) · concepts: [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md)

### 3.13 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-13"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The bhāṣya applies to the elements and senses what was said of the mind: transformation of property (dharma-pariṇāma), of characteristic (lakṣaṇa-pariṇāma, the passage of a property through the three paths, future, present and past) and of condition (avasthā-pariṇāma, e.g. restraint-impressions becoming strong and emergence-impressions weak at moments of restraint). Not for a moment does the flow of the guṇas stand empty of these transformations, and that flow is unsteady (cala); the guṇas' own nature is the cause of their activity. In the highest sense (paramārthataḥ) there is only one transformation, since a property is only the own-form of the property-bearer; a change of state (bhāva) of the property in the bearer is not a change of the substance (dravya), as when a golden vessel is broken and remade. The bhāṣya answers objections (that the bearer would be unchanging, that the paths would be mixed, that the condition-transformation would make things unchanging) and illustrates with clay becoming lump and pot. It defines transformation as the arising of another property when the former property ceases, in a substance that abides.

> atha ko 'yaṃ pariṇāmaḥ.avasthitasya dravyasya pūrvadharmanivṛttau dharmāntarotpattiḥ pariṇāma iti.

_level: unmarked · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: unmarked · types: consciousness-mind, body-layers, world-fate_

terms: [dharma-pariṇāma](../terms/dharma-parinama.md), [lakṣaṇa-pariṇāma](../terms/laksana-parinama.md), [avasthā-pariṇāma](../terms/avastha-parinama.md), [pariṇāma](../terms/parinama.md), [dharmin](../terms/dharmin.md), [mahābhūta](../terms/mahabhuta.md), [indriya](../terms/indriya.md), [adhvan](../terms/adhvan.md) · concepts: [Transformation of property, time-mark and state](../concepts/dharma-laksana-avastha-parinama.md), [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md)

### 3.14 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-14"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

A property is the capacity (śakti) of the property-bearer as delimited by its fitness (yogyatā), inferred from the differences in what it brings forth. The bearer is distinguished from its calmed and undesignable properties while it is experiencing its own function in the present; when it is joined with them only in general form, nothing distinguishes it. The calmed properties are those that have acted and ceased; the arisen are active; the undesignable are those in the state 'everything is of the nature of everything' (sarvaṃ sarvātmakam). The bhāṣya argues that the bearer abides through manifest and unmanifest properties, and that for one for whom this is only properties without a continuing bearer (niranvaya) there would be no enjoyment (bhoga), since an act done by one cognition could not be enjoyed by another; there would be no memory, since what one has seen another cannot remember; and recognition (pratyabhijñāna) of a thing shows that the abiding bearer stands.

> yasya tu dharmamātram evedaṃ niranvayaṃ tasya bhogābhāvaḥ /
kasmāt, anyena vijñānena kṛtasya karmaṇo 'nyat kathaṃ bhoktṛtvenādhikriyate /
tatsmṛtyabhāvaś ca nānyadṛṣṭasya smaraṇam anyasyāstīti /
vastupratyabhijñānāc ca sthito 'nvayī dharmī yo dharmānyathātvam abhyupagataḥ pratyabhijñāyate tasmān nedaṃ dharmamātraṃ niranvayam iti.

_level: unmarked · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: unmarked · types: consciousness-mind, world-fate, dispute_

terms: [dharmin](../terms/dharmin.md), [dharma](../terms/dharma.md) · concepts: [Property and property-bearer (dharma, dharmin)](../concepts/dharma-dharmin.md), [Transformation of property, time-mark and state](../concepts/dharma-laksana-avastha-parinama.md) · disputes: [Is the mind a series of momentary cognitions, or one enduring mind with many objects?](../debates/citta-momentary-or-enduring.md)

### 3.15 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-15"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Where a single bearer would seem to have a single transformation, difference of sequence is the cause of difference of transformations. Sequence is defined as: the property immediately next after a property is its sequence (e.g. powder, lump, pot, shard, grain of clay). Illustrated for the property-transformation (lump ceases, pot arises), the characteristic-transformation (pot from future to present, lump from present to past; the past has no sequence since there is no 'before and after' in it) and the condition-transformation (a new pot's oldness, shown by a succession of moments); the last is a third transformation, distinct from those of property and characteristic. The mind's properties are of two kinds, seen (of the nature of cognition) and unseen (of the nature of mere thing); the unseen are seven, established by inference, listed in a quoted verse: restraint (nirodha), dharma, impressions (saṃskāra), transformation (pariṇāma), life (jīvana), effort (ceṣṭā) and power (śakti).

> tadyathā cūrṇamṛtpiṇḍamṛdghaṭamṛtkapālamṛtkaṇamṛd iti ca kramaḥ /
yo yasya dharmasya samanantaro dharmaḥ sa tasya kramaḥ /

_level: unmarked · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: unmarked · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [krama](../terms/krama.md), [pariṇāma](../terms/parinama.md), [dharma-pariṇāma](../terms/dharma-parinama.md), [lakṣaṇa-pariṇāma](../terms/laksana-parinama.md), [avasthā-pariṇāma](../terms/avastha-parinama.md) · concepts: [Transformation of property, time-mark and state](../concepts/dharma-laksana-avastha-parinama.md), [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md), [Moment and sequence (time)](../concepts/ksana-krama.md), [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md)

### 3.16 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-16"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

From saṃyama on the transformations of property, characteristic and condition, yogins have knowledge of the past and the future. Saṃyama has been said to be the three [concentration, meditation, samādhi] on one object; by it the three transformations are directly perceived, and this brings about the knowledge of past and future in them.

> dharmalakṣaṇāvasthāpariṇāmeṣu saṃyamād yogināṃ bhavaty atītānāgatajñānam /
dhāraṇādhyānasamādhitrayam ekatra saṃyama uktaḥ /
tena pariṇāmatrayaṃ sākṣātkriyamāṇam atītānāgatajñānaṃ teṣu saṃpādayati.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [pariṇāma](../terms/parinama.md), [atīta](../terms/atita.md), [anāgata](../terms/anagata.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [Saṃyama](../concepts/samyama.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md), [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.17 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-17"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The bhāṣya holds that the organ of speech (vāc) functions only in producing the sounds (varṇa); the ear has only the modification of sound as its object; a word (pada) is grasped by the intellect that gathers up the sounds in order. Since sounds cannot occur together, each taken singly is not a word; the word is a single mental presentation, without parts, order or sounds, brought forward by the last sound and understood through worldly usage from beginningless speech-impressions (vāgvyavahāra-vāsanā). The convention (saṃketa) linking word and meaning is a mutual superimposition, remembered as 'this word is this object, this object is this word'. So word, object and cognition ('cow' as sound, as thing, as cognition) are confused through mutual superimposition. Whoever knows their distinction knows everything (sarvavit). The bhāṣya gives examples showing that the word, the object and the cognition do not go together ('the palace whitens' as action, 'the white palace' as factor), the white object being the support of both word and cognition. So by saṃyama on their distinction the yogin gains knowledge of the cries of all beings (sarva-bhūta-ruta-jñāna).

> evam ete śabdārthapratyayā itaretarādhyāsāt saṃkīrṇā gaur iti śabdo gaur ity artho gaur iti jñānam /
ya eṣāṃ pravibhāgajñaḥ sa sarvavit /

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation, knowledge, sound · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, sound-language_

terms: [śabda](../terms/sabda.md), [pratyaya](../terms/pratyaya.md), [adhyāsa](../terms/adhyasa.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [Word, meaning and cognition](../concepts/word-meaning-cognition.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.18 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-18"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The bhāṣya says these impressions (saṃskāra) are of two kinds: those that are causes of memory and of the afflictions (smṛti-kleśa-hetu), of the nature of latent tendencies (vāsanā), and those that are causes of the fruition of action (vipāka-hetu), of the nature of merit and demerit (dharma, adharma). Formed in earlier births, they are unseen properties of the mind (like transformation, effort, restraint, power, life). Saṃyama on them is able to make them directly perceived, which is never without experience of place, time and cause; thus knowledge of former births arises in the yogin, and likewise, in the case of others, awareness of their births.

> dvaye khalv amī saṃskārāḥ smṛtikleśahetavo vāsanārūpā vipākahetavo dharmādharmarūpāḥ /

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, karma-liberation_

terms: [saṃskāra](../terms/samskara.md), [vāsanā](../terms/vasana.md), [smṛti](../terms/smrti.md), [kleśa](../terms/klesa.md), [vipāka](../terms/vipaka.md), [dharma](../terms/dharma.md), [adharma](../terms/adharma.md), [saṃtoṣa](../terms/santosa.md) · concepts: [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md), [Latent tendencies (vāsanā)](../concepts/vasana.md), [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../concepts/five-klesas.md), [The fruition of karma: birth, life-span, experience](../concepts/vipaka-jati-ayus-bhoga.md), [Everything is suffering for the discerning (YS 2.15)](../concepts/duhkha-for-the-discerning.md), [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md) · teachers: [Jaigīṣavya](../teachers/jaigisavya.md), [Āvaṭya](../teachers/avatya.md)

### 3.18 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-18-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

In the story told here, the blessed Jaigīṣavya, seeing through direct perception of impressions the succession of his births over ten great creations, was asked by Āvaṭya whether pleasure or pain had been more. He says that all he experienced was suffering only; asked whether even his mastery over the primal source (pradhāna-vaśitva) and the unsurpassed happiness of contentment (santoṣa-sukha) are on the side of suffering, he answers that this contentment is called unsurpassed only relative to the happiness of objects, and relative to the happiness of kaivalya it is suffering; that this contentment is a property of the buddhi-sattva; it is made of the three guṇas, as is the cognition of it, and it is put on the side of what is to be abandoned (heya).

> kaivalyasukhāpekṣayā duḥkham eva /
buddhisattvasyāyaṃ dharmas triguṇas triguṇaś ca pratyayo heyapakṣe nyasta iti /

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, powers-experiences_

terms: [duḥkha](../terms/duhkha.md), [saṃtoṣa](../terms/santosa.md), [kaivalya](../terms/kaivalya.md), [heya](../terms/heya.md), [guṇa](../terms/guna.md), [buddhi](../terms/buddhi.md), [sattva](../terms/sattva.md) · concepts: [Everything is suffering for the discerning (YS 2.15)](../concepts/duhkha-for-the-discerning.md), [The three guṇas](../concepts/three-gunas.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md) · teachers: [Jaigīṣavya](../teachers/jaigisavya.md), [Āvaṭya](../teachers/avatya.md)

### 3.19 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-19"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

By saṃyama on the cognition, through direct perception of the cognition, there follows from that the knowledge of another's mind.

> pratyaye saṃyamāt pratyayasya sākṣātkaraṇāt tataḥ paracittajñānam.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, consciousness-mind_

terms: [pratyaya](../terms/pratyaya.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.20 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-20"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The yogin knows that a cognition is coloured (rakta, i.e. affected by passion) but does not know that it is coloured 'in this support (ālambana)'. The support of another's cognition is not made the support of the yogin's mind; only the other's cognition itself has become the support of the yogin's mind.

> raktaṃ pratyayaṃ jānāty amuṣminn ālambane raktam iti na jānāti /
parapratyayasya yad ālambanaṃ tad yogicittena nālambanīkṛtaṃ parapratyayamātraṃ tu yogicittasyālambanībhūtam iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, consciousness-mind_

terms: [ālambana](../terms/alambana.md), [pratyaya](../terms/pratyaya.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.21 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-21"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

By saṃyama on the form of the body, the yogin suspends the power of that form to be grasped; with that power suspended, there is no contact with the light of the eye, and disappearance arises for the yogin. By this, the disappearance of sound and the like is to be understood as explained.

> kāyasya rūpe saṃyamād rūpasya yā grāhyā śaktis tāṃ pratiṣṭabhnāti /
grāhyaśaktistambhe sati cakṣuṣprakāśāsaṃprayoge 'ntardhānam utpadyate yoginaḥ /
etena śabdādyantardhānam uktaṃ veditavyam.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, body-layers_

terms: [antardhāna](../terms/antardhana.md), [rūpa](../terms/rupa.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.22 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-22"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Karma that ripens as life-span is of two kinds, with acceleration and without; the bhāṣya illustrates this with a wet cloth spread out, which dries quickly, against the same cloth bundled up, which dries slowly, and with a fire set in dry grass and driven by wind, against the same fire laid on a heap of grass, which burns slowly. By saṃyama on this karma there is knowledge of the end (aparānta), that is, of departure (prāyaṇa), or [knowledge] from omens (ariṣṭa).

> āyurvipākaṃ karma dvividhaṃ sopakramaṃ nirupakramaṃ ca /
tatra yathārdraṃ vastraṃ vitānitaṃ laghīyasā kālena śuṣyet tathā sopakramam /
yathā ca tad eva saṃpiṇḍitaṃ cireṇa saṃśuṣyed evaṃ nirupakramam /
yathā vāgniḥ śuṣke kakṣe mukto vātena samantato yuktaḥ kṣepīyasā kālena dahet tathā sopakramam /
yathā vā sa evāgnis tṛṇarāśau kramaśo 'vayaveṣu nyastaś cireṇa dahet tathā nirupakramam /
tadaikabhavikam āyuṣkaraṃ karma dvividhaṃ sopakramaṃ nirupakramaṃ ca /
tatsaṃyamād aparāntasya prāyaṇasya jñānam /

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, death-dying, karma-liberation_

terms: [karma](../terms/karma.md), [sopakrama](../terms/sopakrama.md), [nirupakrama](../terms/nirupakrama.md), [aparānta](../terms/aparanta.md), [ariṣṭa](../terms/arista.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The four kinds of karma (4.7)](../concepts/four-kinds-of-karma.md), [Death in Yoga: clinging, omens, departure](../concepts/death-in-yoga.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.22 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-22-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Omens are of three kinds: relating to oneself (ādhyātmika: with ears stopped one does not hear the sound in one's own body, or with the eyes pressed one does not see the light), relating to beings (ādhibhautika: one sees Yama's men, or suddenly sees departed fathers) and relating to the gods (ādhidaivika: one suddenly sees heaven or the siddhas), or else everything reversed; by these he knows that the end has arrived.

> ariṣṭebhyo veti trividham ariṣṭam ādhyātmikam ādhibhautikam ādhidaivikaṃ ceti /
tatrādhyātmikaṃ ghoṣaṃ svadehe pihitakarṇo na śṛṇoti, jyotir vā netre 'vaṣṭabdhe na paśyati /
tathādhibhautikaṃ yamapuruṣān paśyati, pitṝn atītān akasmāt paśyati /
tathādhidaivikaṃ svargam akasmāt siddhān vā paśyati /
viparītaṃ vā sarvam iti anena vā jānāty aparāntam upasthitam iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: death-dying, powers-experiences_

terms: [karma](../terms/karma.md), [sopakrama](../terms/sopakrama.md), [nirupakrama](../terms/nirupakrama.md), [aparānta](../terms/aparanta.md), [ariṣṭa](../terms/arista.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The four kinds of karma (4.7)](../concepts/four-kinds-of-karma.md), [Death in Yoga: clinging, omens, departure](../concepts/death-in-yoga.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.23 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-23"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Friendliness, compassion and gladness are three cultivations (bhāvanā). Cultivating friendliness toward beings who are happy, one obtains the power of friendliness; cultivating compassion toward those in suffering, the power of compassion; cultivating gladness toward the virtuous, the power of gladness. The samādhi that arises from cultivation is saṃyama, and from it powers of unfailing potency arise. Toward the wicked there is equanimity (upekṣā), but this is not a cultivation; so there is no samādhi in it, hence no power from equanimity, because there is no saṃyama there.

> maitrī karuṇā muditeti tisro bhāvanās tatra bhūteṣu sukhiteṣu maitrīṃ bhāvayitvā maitrībalaṃ labhate /
duḥkhiteṣu karuṇāṃ bhāvayitvā karuṇābalaṃ labhate /
puṇyaśīleṣu muditāṃ bhāvayitvā muditābalaṃ labhate /
bhāvanātaḥ samādhir yaḥ sa saṃyamas tato balāny avandhyavīryāṇi jāyante /
pāpaśīleṣūpekṣā na tu bhāvanā /
tataś ca tasyāṃ nāsti samādhir ity ato na balam upekṣātas tatra saṃyamābhāvād iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, ethics, practice_

terms: [maitrī](../terms/maitri.md), [kāruṇya](../terms/karuna.md), [muditā](../terms/mudita.md), [upekṣā](../terms/upeksa.md), [bala](../terms/bala.md), [bhāvanā](../terms/bhavana.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md) · concepts: [The four attitudes (maitrī, karuṇā, muditā, upekṣā)](../concepts/four-attitudes.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Cultivating the four attitudes (maitrī, karuṇā, muditā, upekṣā)](../practices/four-attitudes.md)

### 3.24 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-24"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

By saṃyama on the strength of an elephant he becomes possessed of an elephant's strength; by saṃyama on the strength of Garuḍa (vainateya), of Garuḍa's strength; on the strength of the wind, of the wind's strength; and so on.

> hastibale saṃyamād dhastibalo bhavati.vainateyabale saṃyamād vainateyabalo bhavati /
vāyubale saṃyamād vāyubalo bhavatīty evamādi.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [bala](../terms/bala.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.25 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-25"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The luminous activity (jyotiṣmatī pravṛtti) of the mind has been spoken of; the yogin casts the light of it on an object that is subtle, hidden or distant, and attains that object.

> jyotiṣmatī pravṛttir uktā manasas tasyā ya ālokas taṃ yogī sūkṣme vā vyavahite vā viprakṛṣṭe vārthe vinyasya tam artham adhigacchati.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, consciousness-mind_

terms: [jyotiṣmatī](../terms/jyotismati.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [The sorrowless, luminous activity (YS 1.36)](../practices/visoka-jyotismati.md)

### 3.26 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-26"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The bhāṣya first sets out the seven worlds and their beings at length: the earth-world from the Avīci hell up to the back of Meru, with the six great hells, the seven nether worlds and the seven-continent earth around Meru; the intermediate space of planets and stars; heaven (svar), the Mahendra world with six classes of gods; the great world (mahar) of Prajāpati; and the three Brahma-worlds (Jana, Tapas, Satya), whose gods live on meditation and, in the Satya world, are marked by savitarka, savicāra, ānanda-only and asmitā-only meditation-happiness. In the extract: all these seven worlds are Brahma-worlds; but those who are disembodied (videha) and merged in prakṛti (prakṛtilaya) stand in the place of liberation and are not placed within the worlds. This is to be directly realised by the yogin, by doing saṃyama at the door of the sun and then elsewhere, practising thus until all this has been seen.

> ta ete sapta lokāḥ sarva eva brahmalokāḥ /
videhaprakṛtilayās tu mokṣapade vartanta iti na lokamadhye nyastā iti /
etad yoginā sākṣātkaraṇīyaṃ sūryadvāre saṃyamaṃ kṛtvā, tato 'nyatrāpi evaṃ tāvad abhyased yāvad idaṃ sarvaṃ dṛṣṭam iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, world-fate_

terms: [videha](../terms/videha.md), [prakṛtilaya](../terms/prakrtilaya.md), [savitarkā](../terms/savitarka.md), [savicārā](../terms/savicara.md), [ānanda](../terms/ananda.md), [asmitā](../terms/asmita.md) · concepts: [Vyāsa's cosmology (the seven worlds)](../concepts/yoga-cosmology-vyasa.md), [The bodiless and those merged in prakṛti](../concepts/videha-prakrtilaya.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.27 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-27"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Having done saṃyama on the moon, one would know the arrangement of the stars.

> candre saṃyamaṃ kṛtvā tārāṇāṃ vyūhaṃ vijānīyāt.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, world-fate_

terms: [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.28 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-28"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Having done saṃyama on the pole star, one would know the motion of the stars; one who has done saṃyama on the celestial vehicles (ūrdhva-vimāna) would know them.

> tato dhruve saṃyamaṃ kṛtvā tārāṇāṃ gatiṃ vijānīyāt ūrdhvavimāneṣu kṛtasaṃyamas tāni vijānīyāt.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, world-fate_

### 3.29 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-29"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Having done saṃyama on the navel wheel, one would know the arrangement of the body: the three humours (doṣa) of wind, bile and phlegm; and the seven constituents (dhātu), namely skin, blood, flesh, sinew, bone, marrow and semen, each earlier one being outer to the next. This is the arrangement.

> nābhicakre saṃyamaṃ kṛtvā kāyavyūhaṃ vijānīyāt vātapittaśleṣmāṇas trayo doṣāḥ /
dhātavaḥ sapta tvaglohitamāṃsasnāyvasthimajjāśukrāṇi pūrvaṃ pūrvam eṣāṃ bāhyam ity eṣa vinyāsaḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, body-layers_

### 3.30 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-30"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Below the tongue is the cord (tantu), below the cord the throat (kaṇṭha), below that the pit (kūpa); from saṃyama there, hunger and thirst do not trouble.

> jihvāyā adhastāt tantus tantor adhastāt kaṇṭhas tato 'dhastāt kūpas tatra saṃyamāt kṣutpipāse na bādhete.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, body-layers_

terms: [kaṇṭha-kūpa](../terms/kantha-kupa.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [Bodily places named for saṃyama and concentration](../concepts/bodily-loci-of-samyama.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.30-32 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-30-32"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Below the tongue is the 'thread', below that the throat, below that the well: saṃyama there stops hunger and thirst. Below the well, in the chest, is the tortoise-shaped channel: saṃyama there gives steadiness, like that of a snake or an iguana. Inside the skull is a luminous opening, the light in the head: saṃyama there gives sight of the perfected beings who move between heaven and earth.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: body-layers, powers-experiences_

terms: [kaṇṭha-kūpa](../terms/kantha-kupa.md), [kūrma-nāḍī](../terms/kurma-nadi.md), [mūrdha-jyotis](../terms/murdha-jyotis.md) · concepts: [Bodily places named for saṃyama and concentration](../concepts/bodily-loci-of-samyama.md)

### 3.31 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-31"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Below the pit, in the chest, is a channel (nāḍī) of tortoise shape; one who has done saṃyama on it obtains a steady footing, like a snake or a lizard.

> kūpād adha urasi kūrmākārā nāḍī, tasyāṃ kṛtasaṃyamaḥ sthirapadaṃ labhate yathā sarpo godhā veti.

### 3.32 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-32"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Within the skull is a radiant light in an opening; having done saṃyama there, one sees the siddhas who move between heaven and earth.

> śiraḥkapāle 'ntaś chidraṃ prabhāsvaraṃ jyotis tatra saṃyamaṃ kṛtvā siddhānāṃ dyāvāpṛthivyor antarālacāriṇāṃ darśanam.

### 3.33 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-33"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Prātibha is called the saving one (tāraka), the foreshadowing (pūrva-rūpa) of knowledge born of discrimination (viveka-ja jñāna), as the glow of the sun before sunrise. By it the yogin knows everything, when prātibha knowledge arises.

> prātibhaṃ nāma tārakaṃ tadvivekajasya jñānasya pūrvarūpam /
yathodaye prabhā bhāskarasya /
tena vā sarvam eva jānāti yogī prātibhasya jñānasyotpattāv iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, consciousness-mind_

terms: [prātibha](../terms/pratibha.md), [tāraka](../terms/taraka.md), [viveka-ja jñāna](../terms/vivekaja-jnana.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md), [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md), [Omniscience in Yoga](../concepts/omniscience-in-yoga.md)

### 3.34 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-34"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

In this city of Brahman (brahma-pura) is a small lotus, a dwelling; the knowing (vijñāna) is there; from saṃyama on it, awareness of the mind.

> yad idam asmin brahmapure daharaṃ puṇḍarīkaṃ veśma tatra vijñānaṃ tasmin saṃyamāc cittasaṃvit.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, consciousness-mind, body-layers_

terms: [hṛdaya](../terms/hrdaya.md), [citta-saṃvit](../terms/citta-samvit.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [Bodily places named for saṃyama and concentration](../concepts/bodily-loci-of-samyama.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.35 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-35"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The bhāṣya says that the intellect-sattva (buddhi-sattva), having subdued rajas and tamas, is transformed into the cognition of the distinctness of sattva and puruṣa; puruṣa is utterly of a different nature from the changing sattva, pure, of the nature of consciousness only. Experience is the non-distinguishing cognition of the two; it belongs to sattva, which is for another (parārthatva), and so is an object of sight. From saṃyama on the puruṣa-cognition that is distinct from it, knowledge concerning puruṣa arises. Puruṣa is not seen by a cognition in the form of buddhi-sattva; puruṣa itself sees that cognition, resting on itself, as in the quoted words 'by what would one know the knower?'.

> yas tu tasmād viśiṣṭaś citimātrarūpo 'nyaḥ pauruṣeyaḥ pratyayas tatra saṃyamāt puruṣaviṣayā prajñā jāyate /
na ca puruṣapratyayena buddhisattvātmanā puruṣo dṛśyate puruṣa eva taṃ pratyayaṃ svātmāvalambanaṃ paśyati

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, powers-experiences, ultimate_

terms: [bhoga](../terms/bhoga.md), [sattva](../terms/sattva.md), [puruṣa](../terms/purusa.md), [pratyaya](../terms/pratyaya.md), [svārtha](../terms/svartha.md), [parārtha](../terms/parartha.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md) · concepts: [Saṃyama](../concepts/samyama.md), [Reflection of consciousness in buddhi](../concepts/reflection-of-purusa-in-buddhi.md), [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md), [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.36 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-36"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

From prātibha, knowledge of the subtle, the hidden, the distant, the past and the future; from śrāvaṇa, hearing of divine sound; from vedanā, attainment of divine touch; from ādarśa, awareness of divine forms; from āsvāda, awareness of divine tastes; from vārtā, knowledge of divine smells. These arise constantly (nityam).

> prātibhāt sūkṣmavyavahitaviprakṛṣṭātītānāgatajñānam /
śrāvaṇād divyaśabdaśravaṇam /
vedanād divyasparśādhigamaḥ /
ādarśād divyarūpasaṃvit āsvādād divyarasasaṃvit vārtāto divyagandhavijñānam ity etāni nityaṃ jāyante.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [prātibha](../terms/pratibha.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md)

### 3.37 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-37"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Those, prātibha and the rest, arising in one whose mind is in samādhi, are obstacles (upasarga), because they are opposed (pratyanīka) to the seeing of it; arising in one whose mind is outgoing (vyutthita), they are powers (siddhi).

> te prātibhādayaḥ samāhitacittasyotpadyamānā upasargās taddarśanapratyanīkatvāt vyutthitacittasyotpadyamānāḥ siddhayaḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice, powers-experiences_

terms: [upasarga](../terms/upasarga.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md), [vyutthāna](../terms/vyutthana.md), [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md) · concepts: [The powers as obstacles in samādhi (3.37)](../concepts/siddhis-as-obstacles.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Samādhi (in the Vibhūti-pāda account)](../practices/samadhi.md) · obstacles: [Powers as obstacles in samādhi (upasarga)](../obstacles/siddhis-as-upasarga.md) · disputes: [Are the powers (siddhi) arising in yoga signs of attainment or obstacles?](../debates/siddhis-sign-or-obstacle.md)

### 3.38 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-38"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Bondage is the mind's fixed lodging in the body through the force of the store of karma (karmāśaya); by the strength of samādhi this cause of bondage slackens, and awareness of the mind's movement is likewise born of samādhi. With the exhaustion of karma-bondage and knowledge of his own mind's movement, the yogin draws the mind out of his own body and places it in other bodies; the senses follow the mind placed there, as bees follow the king bee when it flies up or settles.

> lolībhūtasya manaso 'pratiṣṭhasya śarīre karmāśayavaśād bandhaḥ pratiṣṭhety arthaḥ tasya karmaṇo bandhakāraṇasya śaithilyaṃ samādhibalād bhavati /
pracārasaṃvedanaṃ ca cittasya samādhijam eva karmabandhakṣayāt svacittasya pracārasaṃvedanāc ca yogī cittaṃ svaśarīrān niṣkṛṣya śarīrāntareṣu nikṣipati /
nikṣiptaṃ cittaṃ cendriyāṇy anu patanti /
yathā madhukararājānaṃ makṣikā utpatantam anūtpatanti niviśamānam anu niviśante tathendriyāṇi paraśarīrāveśe cittam anu vidhīyanta iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, karma-liberation_

terms: [para-śarīra-āveśa](../terms/para-sarira-avesa.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.39 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-39"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The whole activity of the senses, marked by breath (prāṇa) and the rest, is life (jīvana); its action is fivefold. Prāṇa moves through mouth and nose and works as far as the heart; samāna leads evenly and works as far as the navel; apāna leads downward and works as far as the soles; udāna leads upward and works as far as the head; vyāna pervades. Of these prāṇa is chief. From mastery of udāna come non-contact with water, mud and thorns, and, at the time of departure (prāyaṇa), the rising up, which he obtains by his mastery.

> samastendriyavṛttiḥ prāṇādilakṣaṇā jīvanaṃ, tasya kriyā pañcatayī prāṇo mukhanāsikāgatir āhṛdayavṛttiḥ /
samaṃ nayanāt samānaś cānābhivṛttiḥ /
apanayanād apāna āpādatalavṛttiḥ unnayanād udāna āśirovṛttiḥ vyāpī vyāna iti /
eṣāṃ pradhānaṃ prāṇaḥ udānajayāj jalapaṅkakaṇṭakādiṣv asaṅga utkrāntiś ca prāyaṇakāle bhavati tāṃ vaśitvena pratipadyate.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation, body-breath · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, body-layers, death-dying_

terms: [udāna](../terms/udana.md), [utkrānti](../terms/utkranti.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md), [prāṇa](../terms/prana.md), [samāna](../terms/samana.md) · concepts: [The five vital airs in Yoga](../concepts/prana-vayus-yoga.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md), [Death in Yoga: clinging, omens, departure](../concepts/death-in-yoga.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.40 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-40"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

One who has mastered samāna makes a blowing up (upadhmāna) of the fire-element (tejas) and causes it to blaze.

> jitasamānas tejasa upadhmānaṃ kṛtvā jvalayati.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, body-layers_

terms: [samāna](../terms/samana.md), [jvalana](../terms/jvalana.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The five vital airs in Yoga](../concepts/prana-vayus-yoga.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md)

### 3.41 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-41"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Ākāśa is the support (pratiṣṭhā) of all ears and of all sounds, as stated: those hearing in the same place all hear in one place, and this is the mark (liṅga) of ākāśa; its non-obstruction (anāvaraṇa) and, from the seeing of the formless as unobstructing, its all-pervasiveness are also declared. The ear is inferred from the grasping of sound: of a deaf and a non-deaf person, one grasps sound and the other does not, so the ear alone has sound as its object. For the yogin who has done saṃyama on the relation of ear and ākāśa, divine hearing comes into operation.

> sarvaśrotrāṇām ākāśaṃ pratiṣṭhā sarvaśabdānāṃ ca yathoktam --- tulyadeśaśravaṇānām ekadeśaśrutitvaṃ sarveṣāṃ bhavatīti tac caitad ākāśasya liṅgam /
anāvaraṇaṃ coktam /
tathāmūrtasyānāvaraṇadarśanād vibhutvam api prakhyātam ākāśasya /
śabdagrahaṇānumitaṃ śrotram /
badhirābadhirayor ekaḥ śabdaṃ gṛhṇāty aparo na gṛhṇātīti tasmāc chrotram eva śabdaviṣayam śrotrākāśayoḥ saṃbandhe kṛtasaṃyamasya yogino divyaṃ śrotraṃ pravartate.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [ākāśa](../terms/akasa.md), [divya śrotra](../terms/divya-srotra.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.42 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-42"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Wherever the body is, there is ākāśa, which gives it room; the body's relation to it is its attainment. Having done saṃyama there and mastered that relation, or having attained union with light things such as cotton down, down to atoms, the yogin, with the relation mastered, becomes light. By lightness he goes over water on his feet, then over a spider's thread, then over rays, and then has movement in the sky as he wishes.

> yatra kāyas tatrākāśaṃ tasyāvakāśadānāt kāyasya tena saṃbandhaḥ prāptis tatra kṛtasaṃyamo jitvā tatsaṃbandhaṃ laghuṣu vā tūlādiṣv ā paramāṇubhyaḥ samāpattiṃ labdhvā jitasaṃbandho laghur bhavati /
laghutvāc ca jale pādābhyāṃ viharati tatas tūrṇanābhitantumātre vihṛtya raśmiṣu viharati tato yatheṣṭam ākāśagatir asya bhavatīti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [ākāśa](../terms/akasa.md), [ākāśa-gamana](../terms/akasa-gamana.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.43 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-43"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The mind's obtaining a functioning outside the body is a concentration (dhāraṇā) called videhā. If it occurs for a mind established in the body by mere outward functioning, it is called imagined (kalpita); but the outward functioning of a mind that has actually gone out, independent of the body, is non-imagined (akalpita). By the imagined one they accomplish the non-imagined Great Bodiless, by which yogins enter other bodies. From this concentration there is destruction of the covering of the intellect-sattva (buddhi-sattva), whose nature is light: the covering being the triad of affliction, karma and ripening (kleśa-karma-vipāka), rooted in rajas and tamas.

> śarīrād bahir manaso vṛttilābho videhā nāma dhāraṇā /
sā yadi śarīrapratiṣṭhasya manaso bahirvṛttimātreṇa bhavati sā kalpitety ucyate /
yā tu śarīranirapekṣā bahirbhūtasyaiva manaso bahirvṛttiḥ sā khalv akalpitā tatra kalpitayā sādhayanty akalpitāṃ mahāvidehām iti /
yayā paraśarīrāṇy āviśanti yoginaḥ, tataś ca dhāraṇātaḥ prakāśātmano buddhisattvasya yadāvaraṇaṃ kleśakarmavipākatrayaṃ rajastamomūlaṃ tasya ca kṣayo bhavati.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, consciousness-mind_

terms: [mahāvidehā](../terms/mahavideha.md), [akalpita](../terms/akalpita.md), [kalpita](../terms/kalpita.md), [dhāraṇā](../terms/dharana.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md), [The bodiless and those merged in prakṛti](../concepts/videha-prakrtilaya.md) · practices: [Dhāraṇā (concentration)](../practices/dharana.md)

### 3.44 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-44"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The bhāṣya lays out five forms of the elements. The first, the gross, is the particular things such as sound with shape and other properties. The second, own-form, is the common character: solidity of earth, fluidity of water, heat of fire, motion of air, all-pervasiveness of ākāśa. The third, the subtle, is the subtle element (tanmātra), the cause of the element, one part of which is the atom (paramāṇu). The fourth, pervasion, is the guṇas, whose nature is illumination, activity and stability, following the nature of their effects. The fifth, purposiveness, is the being-for-experience-and-release (bhoga-apavarga) that runs through the guṇas. From saṃyama on these five forms in the five elements, in turn, the seeing of each form's own nature and its mastery arise; having mastered the five forms of the elements, one becomes master of the elements; from that mastery the natures of the elements follow his intention (saṃkalpa) like cows following a calf.

> teṣv idānīṃ bhūteṣu pañcasu pañcarūpeṣu saṃyamāt tasya tasya rūpasya svarūpadarśanaṃ jayaś ca prādurbhavati /
tatra pañca bhūtasvarūpāṇi jitvā bhūtajayī bhavati tajjayād vatsānusāriṇya iva gāvo 'sya saṃkalpānuvidhāyinyo bhūtaprakṛtayo bhavanti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, world-fate_

terms: [bhūta-jaya](../terms/bhuta-jaya.md), [tanmātra](../terms/tanmatra.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md), [The three guṇas](../concepts/three-gunas.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.45 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-45"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Of these, aṇimā is becoming atom-sized; laghimā, light; mahimā, great; prāpti, touching even the moon with the tip of a finger; prākāmya, non-obstruction of will, so that one emerges from and sinks into the earth as into water; vaśitva, becoming master over the elements and their products and not being under another's control; īśitṛtva, lordship over their origin, dissolution and arrangement; and kāmāvasāyitva, the truth of intention, whereby the elements stand as one intends; even so one who can does not upset the order of things, because of another earlier-perfected one with this fulfilment of wishes. 'These are the eight lordly powers (aiśvarya).' Perfection of the body is to be stated next; non-obstruction by its properties means that earth does not block the yogin's bodily action and he enters even rock; water does not wet, fire does not burn, wind does not carry him; in ākāśa, which is unobstructing, his body may be covered and invisible even to the siddhas.

> tatrāṇimā bhavaty aṇuḥ laghimā laghur bhavati mahimā mahān bhavati /
prāptir aṅgulyagreṇāpi spṛśati candramasam /
prākāmyam icchānabhighātaḥ bhūmāv unmajjati nimajjati yathodake /
vaśitvaṃ bhūtabhautikeṣu vaśī bhavaty avaśyaś cānyeṣām īśitṛtvaṃ teṣāṃ prabhavāpyayavyūhānām īṣṭe /
yatra kāmāvasāyitvaṃ satyasaṃkalpatā yathā saṃkalpas tathā bhūtaprakṛtīnām avasthānam /
na ca śakto 'pi padārthaviparyāsaṃ karoti kasmāt anyasya yatra kāmāvasāyinaḥ pūrvasiddhasya tathā bhūteṣu saṃkalpād iti /
etāny aṣṭāv aiśvaryāṇi /
kāyasaṃpad vakṣyamāṇā taddharmānabhighātaś ca pṛthvī mūrtyā na niruṇaddhi yoginaḥ śarīrādikriyāṃ, śilām apy anuviśatīti /
nāpaḥ snigdhāḥ kledayanti nāgnir uṣṇo dahati na vāyuḥ praṇāmī vahati anāvaraṇātmake 'py ākāśe bhavaty āvṛtakāyaḥ siddhānām apy adṛśyo bhavati.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced (prajñājyotis (YBh 3.51)) · types: powers-experiences, body-layers_

terms: [aṇimā](../terms/anima.md), [laghimā](../terms/laghima.md), [mahimā](../terms/mahima.md), [prākāmya](../terms/prakamya.md), [īśitṛtva](../terms/isitrtva.md), [kāmāvasāyitva](../terms/kamavasayitva.md), [aiśvarya](../terms/aisvarya.md), [kāya-sampad](../terms/kaya-sampad.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md), [kāya-sampat](../terms/kaya-sampat.md) · concepts: [The eight lordly powers (aṣṭa-aiśvarya)](../concepts/anima-adi-siddhis.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md)

### 3.46 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-46"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

[The body is] worth looking at, radiant, of surpassing strength and of adamantine firmness.

> darśanīyaḥ kāntimān atiśayabalo vajrasaṃhananaś ceti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, body-layers_

terms: [kāya-sampad](../terms/kaya-sampad.md), [rūpa](../terms/rupa.md), [bala](../terms/bala.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md)

### 3.47 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-47"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The bhāṣya gives five forms of the senses: grasping (grahaṇa), the senses' activity on objects that are general-and-particular such as sound; own-form, the sense as an aggregate of general and particular in the luminous intellect-sattva; third, I-making (ahaṃkāra) characterised by asmitā; fourth, the guṇas of illumination, activity and stability whose transformation is the senses together with ahaṃkāra; fifth, the being-for-the-purpose-of-puruṣa (puruṣārthavattva) running through the guṇas. Saṃyama on these five forms of the senses in order, mastery of each, and mastery of the five forms bring mastery of the senses for the yogin.

> pañcasv eteṣv indriyarūpeṣu yathākramaṃ saṃyamas tatra tatra jayaṃ kṛtvā pañcarūpajayād indriyajayaḥ prādurbhavati yoginaḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, consciousness-mind_

terms: [indriya-jaya](../terms/indriya-jaya.md), [grahaṇa](../terms/grahana.md), [asmitā](../terms/asmita.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.48 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-48"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Manojavitva is the body's unsurpassed attainment of motion; vikaraṇabhāva is the senses' obtaining functioning, without the body, in the desired place, time and object; pradhānajaya is mastery over all modifications of prakṛti. These three powers are called 'madhu-pratīkā' and are attained from mastery of the five forms of the instruments (karaṇa).

> kāyasyānuttamo gatilābho manojavitvam /
videhānām indriyāṇām abhipretadeśakālaviṣayāpekṣo vṛttilābho vikaraṇabhāvaḥ /
sarvaprakṛtivikāravaśitvaṃ pradhānajaya ity etās tisraḥ siddhayo madhupratīkā ucyante etāś ca karaṇapañcarūpajayād adhigamyante.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [manojavitva](../terms/manojavitva.md), [vikaraṇa-bhāva](../terms/vikarana-bhava.md), [pradhāna-jaya](../terms/pradhana-jaya.md), [madhupratīka](../terms/madhupratika.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md), [pradhāna](../terms/pradhana.md) · concepts: [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md)

### 3.49 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-49"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

For the intellect-sattva with the stain of rajas and tamas shaken off, standing in the highest clarity (vaiśāradya) and in what is called supreme mastery (vaśīkāra), and established only in the cognition of the distinctness of sattva and puruṣa, there is overlordship of all states: all the guṇas, whose nature is the determining and the determined, stand before the knower of the field (kṣetrajña), the lord, as entirely his to see. Omniscience is the discrimination-born knowledge, without sequence, of the guṇas in all their forms, established as quiescent, arisen or indeterminable properties. This is the power called viśokā, having attained which the yogin moves as one omniscient, with the bondage of the afflictions destroyed, and master.

> nirdhūtarajastamomalasya buddhisattvasya pare vaiśāradye parasyāṃ vaśīkārasaṃjñāyāṃ vartamānasya sattvapuruṣānyatākhyātimātrarūpapratiṣṭhasya sarvabhāvādhiṣṭhātṛtvam /
sarvātmāno guṇā vyavasāyavyavaseyātmakāḥ svāminaṃ kṣetrajñaṃ pratyaśeṣadṛśyātmatvenopasthitā ity arthaḥ /
sarvajñātṛtvaṃ sarvātmanāṃ guṇānāṃ śāntoditāvyapadeśyadharmatvena vyavasthitānām akramopārūḍhaṃ vivekajaṃ jñānam ity arthaḥ /
ity eṣā viśokā nāma siddhir yāṃ prāpya yogī sarvajñaḥ kṣīṇakleśabandhano vaśī viharati.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, consciousness-mind_

terms: [sarva-bhāvādhiṣṭhātṛtva](../terms/sarvabhava-adhisthatrtva.md), [sarvajñātṛtva](../terms/sarvajnatrtva.md), [viśokā](../terms/visoka.md), [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md), [viveka-khyāti](../terms/viveka-khyati.md) · concepts: [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md), [Omniscience in Yoga](../concepts/omniscience-in-yoga.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md)

### 3.50 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-50"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

When it comes to the yogin that, with the afflictions and karma destroyed, this discrimination-cognition is a property of sattva, that sattva is to be placed on the side of what is to be abandoned, and that puruṣa is unchanging, pure and other than sattva, then, as he becomes dispassionate towards even this, the seeds of affliction, like burnt rice-seeds unable to sprout, go to dissolution together with the mind. When they are dissolved, puruṣa no longer undergoes this threefold suffering (tāpa-traya). When the guṇas, which had manifested in the mind as karma, affliction and ripening, and have served their purpose, return to their source (pratiprasava), there is the absolute separation of puruṣa from the guṇas: kaivalya. Then puruṣa is the power of consciousness (citi-śakti) established in its own form.

> yadāsyaivaṃ bhavati kleśakarmakṣaye sattvasyāyaṃ vivekapratyayo dharmaḥ sattvaṃ ca heyapakṣe nyastaṃ puruṣaś cāpariṇāmī śuddho 'nyaḥ sattvād iti /
evam asya tato virajyamānasya yāni kleśabījāni dagdhaśālibījakalpāny aprasavasamarthāni tāni saha manasā pratyastaṃ gacchanti /
teṣu pralīneṣu puruṣaḥ punar idaṃ tāpatrayaṃ na bhuṅkte /
