tad eteṣāṃ guṇānāṃ manasi karmakleśavipākasvarūpeṇābhivyaktānāṃ caritārthānāṃ pratiprasave puruṣasyātyantiko guṇaviyogaḥ kaivalyaṃ tadā svarūpapratiṣṭhā citiśaktir eva puruṣa iti.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [vairāgya](../terms/vairagya.md), [doṣa-bīja](../terms/dosa-bija.md), [kaivalya](../terms/kaivalya.md), [kleśa](../terms/klesa.md), [pratiprasava](../terms/pratiprasava.md), [citi-śakti](../terms/citi-sakti.md), [dagdha-bīja](../terms/dagdha-bija.md) · concepts: [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md), [Return to source (pratiprasava)](../concepts/pratiprasava.md) · practices: [Dispassion (vairāgya)](../practices/vairagya.md)

### 3.51 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-51"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The bhāṣya says there are four kinds of yogin: the prāthamakalpika, the madhubhūmika, the one whose insight is a light (prajñājyotis) and the one who has passed beyond what is to be cultivated (atikrānta-bhāvanīya). The first is the practiser whose light has just begun; the second is the one of truth-bearing insight (ṛtaṃbharā prajñā); the third is the master of elements and senses, who has made a protective binding over everything cultivated and to be cultivated; for the fourth the one aim is the return of the mind to its source (citta-pratisarga), and his is the sevenfold insight of the last stage. To a brāhmaṇa realising the madhumatī stage, gods of high stations (sthānin), seeing the purity of his sattva, extend invitations with their stations: 'sit here, enjoy here', this pleasure, this maiden, an elixir (rasāyana) that defeats old age and death, a sky-vehicle, wishing-trees, the sacred Mandākinī, siddhas and great seers, agreeable apsarases, divine ear and eye, a body like a diamond: 'all this you have earned by your own qualities; take up this imperishable, ageless, deathless station dear to the gods.'

> catvāraḥ khalv amī yoginaḥ prāthamakalpiko madhubhūmikaḥ prajñājyotir atikrāntabhāvanīyaś ceti /
tatrābhyāsī pravṛttamātrajyotiḥ prathamaḥ ṛtaṃbharaprajño dvitīyaḥ bhūtendriyajayī tṛtīyaḥ sarveṣu bhāviteṣu bhāvanīyeṣu kṛtarakṣābandhaḥ kartavyasādhanād imān /
caturtho yas tv atikrāntabhāvanīyas tasya cittapratisarga eko 'rthaḥ saptavidhāsya prāntabhūmiprajñā /
tatra madhumatīṃ bhūmiṃ sākṣātkurvato brāhmaṇasya sthānino devāḥ sattvaviśuddhim anupaśyantaḥ sthānair upanimantrayante bho ihāsyatām iha ramyatāṃ kamanīyo 'yaṃ bhogaḥ kamanīyeyaṃ kanyā rasāyanam idaṃ jarāmṛtyuṃ bādhate vaihāyasam idaṃ yānam amī kalpadrumāḥ puṇyā mandākinī siddhā maharṣaya uttamā anukūlā apsaraso divye śrotracakṣuṣī vajropamaḥ kāyaḥ svaguṇaiḥ sarvam idam upārjitam āyuṣmatā pratipadyatām idam akṣayam ajaram amarasthānaṃ devānāṃ priyam iti /

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced (madhubhūmika (the honey-stage yogin)) · types: practice, ethics, powers-experiences_

terms: [sthānin](../terms/sthanin.md), [saṅga](../terms/sanga.md), [smaya](../terms/smaya.md), [aniṣṭa-prasaṅga](../terms/anista-prasanga.md), [pramāda](../terms/pramada.md), [prāthamakalpika](../terms/prathamakalpika.md), [madhubhūmika](../terms/madhubhumika.md), [prajñājyotis](../terms/prajnajyotis.md), [atikrāntabhāvanīya](../terms/atikrantabhavaniya.md), [madhumatī bhūmi](../terms/madhumati-bhumi.md) · concepts: [The four kinds of yogin (bhāṣya 3.51)](../concepts/four-yogins-vyasa.md), [The powers as obstacles in samādhi (3.37)](../concepts/siddhis-as-obstacles.md) · practices: [Declining the invitations of the high stations (YS 3.51)](../practices/ys-declining-invitations.md) · obstacles: [Attachment, pride and heedlessness when invited (YS 3.51)](../obstacles/sanga-smaya-pramada.md), [Attachment to powers](../obstacles/siddhi-attachment.md), [Attachment and pride at the celestials' invitation](../obstacles/sanga-smaya.md), [Heedlessness (pramāda)](../obstacles/pramada.md)

### 3.51 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-51-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Thus addressed, he should reflect on the faults of attachment: cooked in the terrible coals of saṃsāra and turning in the darkness of birth and death, he has somehow obtained the lamp of yoga that destroys the darkness of the afflictions, and these winds of sense-objects, born of craving, are its opponents; having obtained the light, how should he, deceived by this mirage of sense-objects, make himself fuel again for the blazing fire of saṃsāra? 'Farewell, sense-objects, like dreams, sought by wretched people.' With his mind thus settled he should cultivate samādhi. Having made no attachment, he should not even feel pride ('even the gods seek me'): through pride he would think himself well established and would not regard himself as seized by death by the hair. And heedlessness (pramāda), watching for a gap and to be guarded against with constant effort, having found an opening would stir up the afflictions, and then again there is the prospect of the undesirable. For one who makes neither attachment nor pride, the cultivated aim will be firm and the aim to be cultivated will come to face him.

> evam abhidhīyamānaḥ saṅgadoṣān bhāvayed ghoreṣu saṃsārāṅgāreṣu pacyamānena mayā jananamaraṇāndhakāre viparivartamānena kathaṃcid āsāditaḥ kleśatimiravināśī yogapradīpas tasya caite tṛṣṇāyonayo viṣayavāyavaḥ pratipakṣāḥ /
sa khalv ahaṃ labdhālokaḥ katham anayā viṣayamṛgatṛṣṇayā vañcitas tasyaiva punaḥ pradīptasya saṃsārāgner ātmānam indhanīkuryām iti /
svasti vaḥ svapnopamebhyaḥ kṛpaṇajanaprārthanīyebhyo viṣayebhya ity evaṃ niścitamatiḥ samādhiṃ bhāvayet /
saṅgam akṛtvā smayam api na kuryād evam ahaṃ devānām api prārthanīya iti smayād ayaṃ susthitaṃmanyatayā mṛtyunā keśeṣu gṛhītam ivātmānaṃ na bhāvayiṣyati /
tathā cāsya cchidrāntaraprekṣī nityaṃ yatnopacaryaḥ pramādo labdhavivaraḥ kleśān uttambhayiṣyati tataḥ punar aniṣṭaprasaṅgaḥ /
evam asya saṅgasmayāv akurvato bhāvito 'rtho dṛḍhībhaviṣyati bhāvanīyaś cārtho 'bhimukhībhaviṣyatīti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice, ethics, powers-experiences_

terms: [sthānin](../terms/sthanin.md), [saṅga](../terms/sanga.md), [smaya](../terms/smaya.md), [aniṣṭa-prasaṅga](../terms/anista-prasanga.md), [pramāda](../terms/pramada.md) · concepts: [The four kinds of yogin (bhāṣya 3.51)](../concepts/four-yogins-vyasa.md), [The powers as obstacles in samādhi (3.37)](../concepts/siddhis-as-obstacles.md) · practices: [Declining the invitations of the high stations (YS 3.51)](../practices/ys-declining-invitations.md) · obstacles: [Attachment, pride and heedlessness when invited (YS 3.51)](../obstacles/sanga-smaya-pramada.md), [Attachment to powers](../obstacles/siddhi-attachment.md)

### 3.52 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-52"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

As the atom is the limit of smallness of substance, so the moment (kṣaṇa) is the limit of smallness of time, or the time in which a moving atom leaves its former place and reaches the next; the unbroken flow of it is sequence (krama).

> yathāpakarṣaparyantaṃ dravyaṃ paramāṇur evaṃ paramāpakarṣaparyantaḥ kālaḥ kṣaṇo yāvatā vā samayena calitaḥ paramāṇuḥ pūrvadeśaṃ jahyād uttaradeśam upasaṃpadyeta sa kālaḥ kṣaṇaḥ /
tatpravāhāvicchedas tu kramaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: practice, consciousness-mind, world-fate, powers-experiences_

terms: [kṣaṇa](../terms/ksana.md), [krama](../terms/krama.md), [viveka-ja jñāna](../terms/vivekaja-jnana.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [kāla](../terms/kala.md) · concepts: [Saṃyama](../concepts/samyama.md), [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md), [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md), [Moment and sequence (time)](../concepts/ksana-krama.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.52 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-52-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

There is no aggregation of things in the moment and its sequence; muhūrta, day and night and the like are aggregations made by the intellect (buddhi-samāhāra). This time, though empty of a real thing (vastu-śūnya), is a construction of the intellect, following word-cognition, and appears to ordinary people with outgoing vision as if it were the own-form of a thing. But the moment is real (vastu-patita) and bound up with sequence (kramāvalambin), and sequence is the immediacy of moments; this the yogins who know time call 'time'. Sequence is the immediacy of a moment after the earlier one; two moments do not exist together, so only the one present moment exists and no earlier or later moments, and hence there is no aggregate of moments. Past and future moments are to be explained as accompanied by transformation; by that one moment the whole world undergoes transformation, and all these properties rest on that moment. From saṃyama on the moment and its sequence comes direct realisation of them, and from that knowledge born of discrimination arises.

> kṣaṇatatkramayor nāsti vastusamāhāra iti buddhisamāhāro muhūrtāhorātrādayaḥ /
sa khalv ayaṃ kālo vastuśūnyo 'pi buddhinirmāṇaḥ śabdajñānānupātī laukikānāṃ vyutthitadarśanānāṃ vastusvarūpa ivāvabhāsate /
kṣaṇas tu vastupatitaḥ kramāvalambī kramaś ca kṣaṇānantaryātmā taṃ kālavidaḥ kāla ity ācakṣate yoginaḥ /
na ca dvau kṣaṇau saha bhavataḥ kramaś ca na dvayoḥ sahabhuvor asaṃbhavāt /
pūrvasmād uttarabhāvino yadānantaryaṃ kṣaṇasya sa kramaḥ tasmād vartamāna evaikaḥ kṣaṇo na pūrvottarakṣaṇāḥ santīti /
tasmān nāsti tatsamāhāraḥ ye tu bhūtabhāvinaḥ kṣaṇās te pariṇāmānvitā vyākhyeyāḥ tenaikena kṣaṇena kṛtsno lokaḥ pariṇāmam anubhavati /
tatkṣaṇopārūḍhāḥ khalv amī sarve dharmāḥ tayoḥ kṣaṇatatkramayoḥ saṃyamāt tayoḥ sākṣātkaraṇam /
tataś ca vivekajaṃ jñānaṃ prādurbhavati.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: practice, consciousness-mind, world-fate_

terms: [kṣaṇa](../terms/ksana.md), [krama](../terms/krama.md), [viveka-ja jñāna](../terms/vivekaja-jnana.md), [saṃyama](../terms/samyama.md), [kāla](../terms/kala.md) · concepts: [Saṃyama](../concepts/samyama.md), [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md), [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md) · practices: [Saṃyama](../practices/samyama.md)

### 3.53 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-53"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence low

Knowledge of the truth should be free of doubt; therefore the sūtra says that the discernment is from knowledge born of discrimination. The bhāṣya illustrates with a cow and a mare (difference of class), two cows alike in class and place (difference of characteristic) and two myrobalan fruits alike in class and characteristic (difference of place); it adds that when, while the knower's attention is elsewhere (anya-vyagra), the first fruit is moved into the second's place, the place being the same, the distinction 'this is earlier, this later' cannot be made. How then? The place together with the moment of the earlier fruit differs from the place together with the moment of the later, and the two fruits differ by their experience of their own place-and-moment. By this example, when an atom alike in class, characteristic and place takes the place of an earlier atom, the masterful yogin (īśvara yogin), having directly perceived the earlier atom's place together with its moment, has the cognition of their otherness from the difference of the co-present moment.

> asaṃdigdhena ca tattvajñānena bhavitavyam ity ata idam uktaṃ tataḥ pratipattir vivekajajñānād iti

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, powers-experiences_

terms: [jāti](../terms/jati.md), [pratipatti](../terms/pratipatti.md), [viveka-ja jñāna](../terms/vivekaja-jnana.md) · concepts: [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md)

### 3.53 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-53-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence low

Others (apare) explain that it is the ultimate particulars (antyā viśeṣāḥ) that produce the notion of otherness. The bhāṣya answers: even there, difference of place and characteristic, and difference of form, interval (vyavadhi) and class, is the reason for otherness, while difference of moment is accessible only to the yogin's intellect; hence it has been said, 'where there is no difference of form, interval and class, there is no root-separateness (mūla-pṛthaktva)': so Vārṣagaṇya.

> apare tu varṇayanti --- ye 'ntyā viśeṣās te 'nyatāpratyayaṃ kurvantīti tatrāpi deśalakṣaṇabhedo mūrtivyavadhijātibhedaś cānyatve hetuḥ /
kṣaṇabhedas tu yogibuddhigamya eveti ata uktaṃ mūrtivyavadhijātibhedābhāvān nāsti mūlapṛthaktvam iti vārṣagaṇyaḥ

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, powers-experiences_

terms: [jāti](../terms/jati.md), [pratipatti](../terms/pratipatti.md), [viveka-ja jñāna](../terms/vivekaja-jnana.md) · concepts: [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md) · teachers: [Vārṣagaṇya](../teachers/varsaganya.md)

### 3.54 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-54"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Tāraka means arisen from one's own prātibha, not taught by instruction (anaupadeśika). 'All objects' means that nothing is outside its range. 'In every way' means that it knows everything, past, future and present, through all their modifications. 'Without sequence' means that it grasps everything in every way in one moment. This discrimination-born knowledge is complete; the lamp of yoga, from the madhumatī stage up to its completion, is a part of it.

> tārakam iti svapratibhottham anaupadeśikam ity arthaḥ sarvaviṣayaṃ nāsya kiṃcid aviṣayībhūtam ity arthaḥ /
sarvathāviṣayam atītānāgatapratyutpannaṃ sarvaṃ paryāyaiḥ sarvathā jānātīty arthaḥ /
akramam ity ekakṣaṇopārūḍhaṃ sarvaṃ sarvathā gṛhṇātīty arthaḥ /
etad vivekajaṃ jñānaṃ paripūrṇam asyaivāṃśo yogapradīpo madhumatīṃ bhūmim upādāya yāvad asya parisamāptir iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, powers-experiences_

terms: [tāraka](../terms/taraka.md), [akrama](../terms/akrama.md), [viveka-ja jñāna](../terms/vivekaja-jnana.md), [madhumatī bhūmi](../terms/madhumati-bhumi.md) · concepts: [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md), [Omniscience in Yoga](../concepts/omniscience-in-yoga.md)

### 3.55 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-3-55"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

When the intellect-sattva, with the stain of rajas and tamas shaken off, has as its only remaining scope the cognition of the distinctness of puruṣa and its seeds of affliction are burnt, it comes as if to a likeness of purity with puruṣa; the purity of puruṣa is the absence of figurative (upacarita) experience. In this state kaivalya occurs, whether for the one who has lordship (īśvara) or not, whether he has shared in discrimination-born knowledge or not; for one whose seeds of affliction are burnt no further need of knowledge remains. The power and knowledge born of samādhi were begun by way of the purity of sattva. In ultimate truth it is by knowledge that non-seeing (adarśana) is removed; with that removed, no further afflictions; with no afflictions, no ripening of karma; and in this state the guṇas, their office finished, no longer present themselves to puruṣa as something seen. That is puruṣa's kaivalya: then puruṣa is a light of its own form alone, stainless, isolated (kevalī).

> yadā nirdhūtarajastamomalaṃ buddhisattvaṃ puruṣasyānyatāpratītimātrādhikāraṃ dagdhakleśabījaṃ bhavati tadā puruṣasya śuddhisārūpyam ivāpannaṃ bhavati, tadā puruṣasyopacaritabhogābhāvaḥ śuddhiḥ /
etasyām avasthāyāṃ kaivalyaṃ bhavatīśvarasyānīśvarasya vā vivekajajñānabhāgina itarasya vā /
na hi dagdhakleśabījasya jñāne punar apekṣā kācid asti sattvaśuddhidvāreṇaitat samādhijam aiśvaryaṃ jñānaṃ copakrāntam /
paramārthatas tu jñānād adarśanaṃ nivartate tasmin nivṛtte na santy uttare kleśāḥ /
kleśābhāvāt karmavipākābhāvaḥ caritādhikārāś caitasyām avasthāyāṃ guṇā na puruṣasya punar dṛśyatvenopatiṣṭhante /
tatpuruṣasya kaivalyaṃ, tadā puruṣaḥ svarūpamātrajyotir amalaḥ kevalī bhavati.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind, powers-experiences_

terms: [kaivalya](../terms/kaivalya.md), [sattva](../terms/sattva.md), [puruṣa](../terms/purusa.md) · concepts: [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md), [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md), [The powers as obstacles in samādhi (3.37)](../concepts/siddhis-as-obstacles.md)

### 4.1 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-1"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The bhāṣya says: the power by birth is that of one who has passed into another body; by herbs, for instance in the dwellings of the asuras through an elixir (rasāyana); by mantras, gaining sky-going and aṇimā and the like; by austerity (tapas), the accomplishment of intention, going anywhere at will and taking any form; the powers born of samādhi have been explained.

> dehāntaritā janmanā siddhiḥ oṣadhibhir asurabhavaneṣu rasāyanenety evamādiḥ /
mantrair ākāśagamanāṇimādilābhaḥ /
tapasā saṃkalpasiddhiḥ, kāmarūpī yatra tatra kāmaga ity evamādi samādhijāḥ siddhayo vyākhyātāḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences_

terms: [siddhi](../terms/siddhi.md), [mantra](../terms/mantra.md), [tapas](../terms/tapas.md), [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md) · concepts: [Five sources of powers (4.1)](../concepts/five-sources-of-siddhis.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md)

### 4.2 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The earlier transformation ceases and the later arises by the entry of new parts; the natures (prakṛti) of body and senses support their own modifications by filling in, waiting on dharma and the like as occasion (nimitta).

> pūrvapariṇāmāpāya uttarapariṇāmopajanas teṣām apūrvāvayavānupraveśād bhavati /
kāyendriyaprakṛtayaś ca svaṃ svaṃ vikāram anugṛhṇanty āpūreṇa dharmādinimittam apekṣamāṇā iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: world-fate, karma-liberation, body-layers_

terms: [jāty-antara-pariṇāma](../terms/jatyantara-parinama.md), [prakṛti](../terms/prakrti.md), [āpūra](../terms/apura.md) · concepts: [The three guṇas](../concepts/three-gunas.md)

### 4.3 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-3"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Dharma and the rest are not the impellers of the natures: the effect does not impel the cause. Instead, like a farmer who, wishing to flood one field from a full one, does not push the water with his hand but breaks the barrier, after which the water floods the other field of itself, dharma breaks the obstructing property of the natures and they themselves flood their own modifications. Or, as the same farmer cannot make water and earth-juices enter the roots of the crop but only removes the weeds, so dharma is the cause only of the removal of adharma, since purity and impurity are utterly opposed, and is not the cause of the natures' activity. Nandīśvara and others are to be cited for this; conversely adharma obstructs dharma and so leads to a transformation of impurity, for which Nahuṣa, the python and others are to be cited.

> na hi dharmādi nimittaṃ tatprayojakaṃ prakṛtīnāṃ bhavati na kāryeṇa kāraṇaṃ pravartyata iti kathaṃ tarhi, varaṇabhedas tu tataḥ kṣetrikavat /
yathā kṣetrikaḥ kedārād apāṃ pūrṇāt kedārāntaraṃ piplāvayiṣuḥ samaṃ nimnaṃ nimnataraṃ vā nāpaḥ pāṇināpakarṣaty āvaraṇaṃ tv āsāṃ bhinatti tasmin bhinne svayam evāpaḥ kedārāntaram āplāvayanti tathā dharmaḥ prakṛtīnām āvaraṇadharmaṃ bhinatti tasmin bhinne svayam eva prakṛtayaḥ svaṃ svaṃ vikāram āplāvayanti /
yathā vā sa eva kṣetrikas tasminn eva kedāre na prabhavaty audakān bhaumān vā rasān dhānyamūlāny anupraveśayituṃ, kiṃ tarhi mudgagavedhukaśyāmākādīṃs tato 'pakarṣati /
apakṛṣṭeṣu teṣu svayam eva rasā dhānyamūlāny anupraviśanti, tathā dharmo nivṛttimātre kāraṇam adharmasya, śuddhyaśuddhyor atyantavirodhāt, na tu prakṛtipravṛttau dharmo hetur bhavatīti /
atra nandīśvarādaya udāhāryāḥ viparyayeṇāpy adharmo dharmaṃ bādhate /
tataś cāśuddhipariṇāma iti /
tatrāpi nahuṣājagarādaya udāhāryāḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, world-fate_

terms: [prakṛti](../terms/prakrti.md), [dharma](../terms/dharma.md), [adharma](../terms/adharma.md) · concepts: [Karma as occasion, not impeller, of the natures (4.3)](../concepts/karma-and-prakrti.md), [The store of karma (karmāśaya)](../concepts/karmasaya.md) · teachers: [Nandīśvara (as named in the Yoga-bhāṣya on 4.3)](../teachers/nandisvara-bhasya-example.md), [Nahuṣa](../teachers/nahusa.md)

### 4.4 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-4"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Taking asmitā alone as the cause of the mind, the yogin makes constructed minds; thereupon the constructed bodies become possessed of minds.

> asmitāmātraṃ cittakāraṇam upādāya nirmāṇacittāni karoti, tataḥ sacittāni bhavantīti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, consciousness-mind_

terms: [nirmāṇa-citta](../terms/nirmana-citta.md) · concepts: [Constructed minds (nirmāṇa-citta)](../concepts/nirmana-citta.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md)

### 4.5 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-5"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The question is how many minds can act in accord with the purpose of a single mind. The answer: the yogin makes one mind that impels all the minds; from that comes the difference of activity.

> bahūnāṃ cittānāṃ katham ekacittābhiprāyapuraḥsarā pravṛttir iti sarvacittānāṃ prayojakaṃ cittam ekaṇ nirmimīte, tataḥ pravṛttibhedaḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: powers-experiences, consciousness-mind_

terms: [nirmāṇa-citta](../terms/nirmana-citta.md) · concepts: [Constructed minds (nirmāṇa-citta)](../concepts/nirmana-citta.md), [The powers of the Vibhūti-pāda (vibhūti)](../concepts/vibhutis.md)

### 4.6 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-6"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The constructed mind is fivefold, as the powers born of birth, herbs, mantras, austerity and samādhi. Of these only the mind born of meditation is without residue: it has no residue (āśaya), no activity of passion and the like, hence no connection with merit and demerit, because the yogin's afflictions are destroyed; in the others there is a store of karma (karmāśaya).

> pañcavidhaṃ nirmāṇacittaṃ janmauṣadhimantratapaḥsamādhijāḥ siddhaya iti /
tatra yad eva dhyānajaṃ cittaṃ tad evānāśayaṃ tasyaiva nāsty āśayo rāgādipravṛttir nātaḥ puṇyapāpābhisaṃbandhaḥ kṣīṇakleśatvād yogina iti itareṣāṃ tu vidyate karmāśayaḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind, powers-experiences_

terms: [anāśaya](../terms/anasaya.md), [nirmāṇa-citta](../terms/nirmana-citta.md) · concepts: [Constructed minds (nirmāṇa-citta)](../concepts/nirmana-citta.md)

### 4.7 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-7"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

This kind of karma is fourfold: black, white-black, white, and neither white nor black. The black is of evil-natured people. The white-black is accomplished by external means, so the accumulation of the store of karma comes only through harming or favouring others. The white is of those devoted to austerity, study and meditation: it depends on the mind alone, is not dependent on external means, and does not arise by harming others. The neither-white-nor-black is of renunciates (saṃnyāsin) whose afflictions are destroyed and whose present body is the last; there, 'not white' belongs to the yogin alone, because of renunciation of the fruit, and 'not black' because of non-appropriation (anupādāna). For other beings it is threefold as before.

> catuṣpadī khalv iyaṃ karmajātiḥ kṛṣṇā śuklakṛṣṇā śuklāśuklākṛṣṇā ceti /
tatra kṛṣṇā durātmanām, śuklakṛṣṇā bahiḥsādhanasādhyā /
tatra parapīḍānugrahadvāreṇaiva karmāśayapracayaḥ śuklā tapaḥsvādhyāyadhyānavatām /
sā hi kevale manasy āyattatvād abahiḥsādhanādhīnā na parān pīḍayitvā bhavati /
aśuklākṛṣṇā saṃnyāsināṃ kṣīṇakleśānāṃ caramadehānām iti tatrāśuklaṃ yogina eva phalasaṃnyāsād akṛṣṇaṃ cānupādānāt itareṣāṃ tu bhūtānāṃ pūrvam eva trividham iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: knowledge, action, meditation · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, ethics_

terms: [karma](../terms/karma.md), [karmāśaya](../terms/karmasaya.md), [aśuklākṛṣṇa](../terms/asuklakrsna.md) · concepts: [The four kinds of karma (4.7)](../concepts/four-kinds-of-karma.md)

### 4.8 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-8"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

'From that' means from the threefold karma. The vāsanās fitted to the ripening are those which follow the ripening of a given kind of karma, and only those are manifested. The ripening of karma of the divine kind cannot be the occasion for the manifestation of the vāsanās of hell-beings, animals or humans; only the vāsanās fitted to the divine are manifested. The same holds in the cases of hell-beings, animals and humans.

> tata iti trividhāt karmaṇaḥ, tadvipākānuguṇānām eveti yajjātīyasya karmaṇo yo vipākas tasyānuguṇā yā vāsanāḥ karmavipākam anuśerate tāsām evābhivyaktiḥ /
na hi daivaṃ karma vipacyamānaṃ nārakatiryaṅmanuṣyavāsanābhivyaktinimittaṃ saṃbhavati kiṃtu daivānuguṇā evāsya vāsanā vyajyante nārakatiryaṅmanuṣyeṣu caivaṃ samānaś carcaḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [vāsanā](../terms/vasana.md), [vipāka](../terms/vipaka.md), [abhivyakti](../terms/abhivyakti.md), [karma](../terms/karma.md) · concepts: [Latent tendencies (vāsanā)](../concepts/vasana.md), [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md)

### 4.9 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-9"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Why does the succession hold? Because memory and saṃskāra have one form: as the experiences, so the saṃskāras; they follow the vāsanās of karma; and as the vāsanās, so the memory. Thus memory arises from saṃskāras separated by birth, place and time, and saṃskāras arise in turn from memory. Memory and saṃskāra are manifested by the force of the store of karma taking up its function. Since the relation of occasion and what depends on it is never cut, immediate succession is established even for those separated.

> kutaś ca, smṛtisaṃskārayor ekarūpatvāt yathānubhavās tathā saṃskārāḥ /
te ca karmavāsanānurūpāḥ yathā ca vāsanās tathā smṛtir iti jātideśakālavyavahitebhyaḥ saṃskārebhyaḥ smṛtiḥ /
smṛteś ca punaḥ saṃskārā ityevam ete smṛtisaṃskārāḥ karmāśayavṛttilābhavaśād vyajyante ataś ca vyavahitānām api nimittanaimittikabhāvānucchedād ānantaryam eva siddham iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [vāsanā](../terms/vasana.md), [saṃskāra](../terms/samskara.md), [smṛti](../terms/smrti.md) · concepts: [Latent tendencies (vāsanā)](../concepts/vasana.md), [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md)

### 4.9 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-9-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The example given is the rise of the ripening of the kind called vṛṣadaṃśa, manifested by its own manifester (vyañjaka). Even if it is separated by a hundred births, by distance, or by a hundred aeons, it arises immediately when its manifester arises again, taking up the vāsanās shaped by the earlier experienced ripening.

> vṛṣadaṃśavipākodayaḥ svavyañjakāñjanābhivyaktaḥ /
sa yadi jātiśatena vā dūradeśatayā vā kalpaśatena vā vyavahitaḥ punaś ca svavyañjakāñjana evodiyād drāg ity evaṃ pūrvānubhūtavṛṣadaṃśavipākābhisaṃskṛtā vāsanā upādāya vyajyeta

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [vāsanā](../terms/vasana.md), [saṃskāra](../terms/samskara.md), [smṛti](../terms/smrti.md) · concepts: [Latent tendencies (vāsanā)](../concepts/vasana.md), [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md)

### 4.10 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-10"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The vāsanās are beginningless because the wish 'may I not cease to be, may I be' is seen in everyone. That is not natural (svābhāvikī): a creature just born, who has not experienced the nature of dying, could not have the terror of death arising from the recollection of hatred and suffering, and a natural thing does not take a cause. Therefore this mind, shot through with beginningless vāsanās, on obtaining certain vāsanās by cause, works for the experience (bhoga) of puruṣa.

> tāsāṃ vāsanānām āśiṣo nityatvād anāditvam /
yeyam ātmāśīr mā na bhūvaṃ bhūyāsam iti.sarvasya dṛśyate sā na svābhāvikī /
kasmāt jātamātrasya jantor ananubhūtamaraṇadharmakasya dveṣaduḥkhānusmṛtinimitto maraṇatrāsaḥ kathaṃ bhavet na ca svābhāvikaṃ vastu nimittam upādatte /
tasmād anādivāsanānuviddham idaṃ cittaṃ nimittavaśāt kāścid eva vāsanāḥ pratilabhya puruṣasya bhogāyopāvartata iti /

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: knowledge, action · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind, dispute, death-dying_

terms: [vāsanā](../terms/vasana.md), [āśis](../terms/asis.md), [citta](../terms/citta.md), [maitrī](../terms/maitri.md) · concepts: [Latent tendencies (vāsanā)](../concepts/vasana.md), [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md), [The beginninglessness of vāsanās (4.10)](../concepts/beginningless-vasana.md), [The four attitudes (maitrī, karuṇā, muditā, upekṣā)](../concepts/four-attitudes.md) · disputes: [Is the mind (citta) only of the size of the body, contracting and expanding, or all-pervading with only its function contracting and expanding?](../debates/size-of-citta.md)

### 4.10 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-10-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Others hold that the mind, like a lamp in a jar or a palace, contracts and expands and is only of the size of the body, so that an intermediate state (antarābhāva) and saṃsāra are logical. The teacher (ācārya) says that it is the function (vṛtti) of the all-pervading (vibhu) mind that contracts and expands, and this depends on dharma and the like as occasion. Occasion is twofold, external and internal: external depends on means such as the body (praise, gift, salutation), internal depends on the mind alone (faith and the like). As was said, the 'abodes' of meditators (friendliness and the rest) bring about exalted dharma without help from external means; of the two the mental is stronger: what surpasses knowledge and dispassion, and who else could empty the Daṇḍaka forest by bodily action without the strength of mind, or drink the ocean like Agastya?

> ghaṭaprāsādapradīpakalpaṃ saṃkocavikāsi cittaṃ śarīraparimāṇākāramātram ity apare pratipannāḥ tathā cāntarābhāvaḥ saṃsāraś ca yukta iti /
vṛttir evāsya vibhunaś cittasya saṃkocavikāsinīty ācāryaḥ /
tac ca dharmādinimittāpekṣam /
nimittaṃ ca dvividham --- bāhyam ādhyātmikaṃ ca /
śarīrādisādhanāpekṣaṃ bāhyaṃ stutidānābhivādanādi, cittamātrādhīnaṃ śraddhādy adhyātmikam /
tathā coktam --- ye caite maitryādayo dhyāyināṃ vihārās te bāhyasādhananiranugrahātmānaḥ prakṛṣṭaṃ dharmam abhinirvartayanti /
tayor mānasaṃ balīyaḥ /
kathaṃ, jñānavairāgye kenātiśayyete, daṇḍakāraṇyaṃ ca cittabalavyatirekeṇa śārīreṇa karmaṇā śūnyaṃ kaḥ kartum utsaheta samudram agastyavad vā pibet.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind, dispute_

terms: [vāsanā](../terms/vasana.md), [āśis](../terms/asis.md), [citta](../terms/citta.md) · concepts: [Latent tendencies (vāsanā)](../concepts/vasana.md), [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md), [The beginninglessness of vāsanās (4.10)](../concepts/beginningless-vasana.md) · teachers: [Agastya](../teachers/agastya.md) · disputes: [Is the mind (citta) only of the size of the body, contracting and expanding, or all-pervading with only its function contracting and expanding?](../debates/size-of-citta.md)

### 4.11 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-11"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

The cause (hetu): from dharma, pleasure; from adharma, pain; from pleasure, attachment; from pain, aversion; from these, effort, by which, moving by mind, speech or body, one favours or harms another; from that again dharma and adharma, pleasure and pain, attachment and aversion. Thus this six-spoked wheel of saṃsāra turns; ignorance (avidyā) is its leader and the root of all the afflictions. The fruit is that on which the presentness of dharma and the rest depends. The support (āśraya) is the mind with its office still to be done: when the mind's office is finished, the vāsanās, having no support, cannot remain. The object (ālambana) is whatever thing coming to the fore manifests a vāsanā. All vāsanās are held together by these four, and when they are absent the vāsanās depending on them are absent.

> hetur dharmāt sukham adharmād duḥkhaṃ sukhād rāgo duḥkhād dveṣas tataś ca prayatnas tena manasā vācā kāyena vā parispandamānaḥ param anugṛhṇāty upahanti vā tataḥ punar dharmādharmau sukhaduḥkhe rāgadveṣāv iti pravṛttam idaṃ ṣaḍaraṃ saṃsāracakram /
asya ca pratikṣaṇam āvartamānasyāvidyā netrī mūlaṃ sarvakleśānām ity eṣa hetuḥ /
phalaṃ tu yam āśritya yasya pratyutpannatā dharmādeḥ, na hy apūrvopajanaḥ /
manas tu sādhikāram āśrayo vāsanānām /
na hy avasitādhikāre manasi nirāśrayā vāsanāḥ sthātum utsahante /
yad abhimukhībhūtaṃ vastu yāṃ vāsanāṃ vyanakti tasyās tadālambanam /
evaṃ hetuphalāśrayālambanair etaiḥ saṃgṛhītāḥ sarvā vāsanāḥ eṣām abhāve tatsaṃśrayāṇām api vāsanānām abhāvaḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: causal · path: knowledge, action · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [vāsanā](../terms/vasana.md), [hetu](../terms/hetu.md), [phala](../terms/phala.md), [ālambana](../terms/alambana.md), [avidyā](../terms/avidya.md) · concepts: [Latent tendencies (vāsanā)](../concepts/vasana.md), [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md), [The six-spoked wheel of saṃsāra (bhāṣya 4.11)](../concepts/samsara-wheel-six-spoked.md), [The store of karma (karmāśaya)](../concepts/karmasaya.md) · practices: [Cessation of vāsanās and saṃskāras as the text states it](../practices/vasana-ksaya.md) · obstacles: [Ignorance (avidyā)](../obstacles/avidya.md)

### 4.12 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-12"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The future is that whose manifestation is to come; the past that whose manifestation has been experienced; the present that which has risen to its own function; these three are objects of knowledge. If they did not exist in their own form, this cognition would arise without an object; therefore the past and future exist in their own form. Also, if the fruit of karma about to arise were unreal (nirupākhya), skilful action aimed at it would not be fitting. The cause is able to make an existing fruit present, not to produce it anew; it only gives a special favour to what depends on it. The bearer (dharmin) has many properties, established in the different paths. The past and future are not present in the manner of the present, with a special manifestation; they exist by the own-form that is to be manifested, or by the own-form of manifestation once experienced. The own-form-manifestation belongs to the present path only. At any time two paths are always attended by the bearer, so the three paths do not come into being from non-existence.

> bhaviṣyadvyaktikam anāgatam anubhūtavyaktikam atītaṃ svavyāpāropārūḍhaṃ vartamānaṃ, trayaṃ caitad vastu jñānasya jñeyam /
yadi caitat svarūpato nābhaviṣyan nedaṃ nirviṣayaṃ jñānam udapatsyata /
tasmād atītānāgataṃ svarūpato 'stīti /
kiṃca bhogabhāgīyasya vāpavargabhāgīyasya vā karmaṇaḥ phalam utpitsu yadi nirupākhyam iti taduddeśena tena nimittena kuśalānuṣṭhānaṃ na yujyeta /
sataś ca phalasya nimittaṃ vartamānīkaraṇe samarthaṃ nāpūrvopajanane siddhaṃ nimittaṃ naimittikasya viśeṣānugrahaṇaṃ kurute nāpūrvam utpādayatīti /
dharmī cānekadharmasvabhāvas tasya cādhvabhedena dharmāḥ pratyavasthitāḥ na ca yathā vartamānaṃ vyaktiviśeṣāpannaṃ dravyato 'sty evam atītam anāgataṃ ca /
kathaṃ tarhi, svenaiva vyaṅgyena svarūpeṇānāgatam asti /
svena cānubhūtavyaktikena svarūpeṇātītam iti vartamānasyaivādhvanaḥ svarūpavyaktir iti na sā bhavaty atītānāgatayor adhvanoḥ /
ekasya cādhvanaḥ samaye dvāv adhvanau dharmisamanvāgatau bhavata eveti nābhūtvā bhāvas trayāṇām adhvanām iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, world-fate_

terms: [atīta](../terms/atita.md), [anāgata](../terms/anagata.md), [adhvan](../terms/adhvan.md), [dharma](../terms/dharma.md), [dharmin](../terms/dharmin.md) · concepts: [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md), [Past and future exist in their own form](../concepts/existence-of-past-and-future.md) · disputes: [Does the effect pre-exist in its cause (satkārya), is it newly produced (asatkārya/ārambha), is it an apparent transformation of an unchanging cause (vivarta), a real manifestation of consciousness (ābhāsa), a transformation of God that leaves him unchanged (avikṛta-pariṇāma), or dependently arisen and ultimately unarisen?](../debates/causation.md)

### 4.13 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-13"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

These properties of the three paths are manifest when present and subtle when past or future, having the six non-particulars as their form. All this is only a particular arrangement (saṃniveśa-viśeṣa) of the guṇas, so in ultimate truth (paramārthataḥ) they are of the nature of the guṇas. This is as the teaching of the treatise says: 'The highest form of the guṇas does not come into the range of sight; what comes into the range of sight is like māyā, very trifling.'

> te khalv amī tryadhvano dharmā vartamānā vyaktātmāno 'tītānāgatāḥ sūkṣmātmānaḥ ṣaḍaviśeṣarūpāḥ /
sarvam idaṃ guṇānāṃ saṃniveśaviśeṣamātram iti paramārthato guṇātmānaḥ /
tathā ca śāstrānuśāsanam ---
"guṇānāṃ paramaṃ rūpaṃ na dṛṣṭipatham ṛcchati /
yat tu dṛṣṭipathaṃ prāptaṃ tan māyeva sutucchakam //" iti.

_level: bridging · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, world-fate_

terms: [vyakta](../terms/vyakta.md), [guṇa](../terms/guna.md) · concepts: [The three guṇas](../concepts/three-gunas.md)

### 4.14 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-14"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The guṇas of illumination, activity and stability have one transformation as the instrument in the form of the sense of hearing, and one transformation as the object in the form of sound (śabda-tanmātra); likewise for earth atoms and the rest, the one transformation of like-natured things ends in earth, cow, tree, mountain. The bhāṣya then reports the argument 'there is no object unaccompanied by cognition, but there is cognition unaccompanied by an object, imagined in dream and the like'; those who by this line deny the own-nature of a thing, saying it is a mere conceptual construction like a dream object and does not exist in ultimate truth, are asked how, giving up the thing's own form by the force of conceptual cognition that is not a means of knowledge (apramāṇa), and denying that very thing which stands present in its own greatness, they could be worthy of belief.

> prakhyākriyāsthitiśīlānāṃ guṇānāṃ grahaṇātmakānāṃ karaṇabhāvenaikaḥ pariṇāmaḥ śrotram indriyaṃ, grāhyātmakānāṃ śabdatanmātrabhāvenaikaḥ pariṇāmaḥ śabdo viṣaya iti, śabdādīnāṃ mūrtisamānajātīyānām ekaḥ pariṇāmaḥ pṛthivīparamāṇus tanmātrāvayavas teṣāṃ caikaḥ pariṇāmaḥ pṛthivī gaur vṛkṣaḥ parvata ityevamādir bhūtāntareṣv api snehauṣṇyapraṇāmitvāvakāśadānāny upādāya sāmānyam ekavikārārambhaḥ samādheyaḥ /
nāsty artho vijñānavisahacaraḥ, asti tu jñānam arthavisahacaraṃ svapnādau kalpitam ity anayā diśā ye vastusvarūpam apahnuvate jñānaparikalpanāmātraṃ vastu svapnaviṣayopamaṃ na paramārthato 'stīti ya āhus te tatheti pratyupasthitam idaṃ svamāhātmyena vastu katham apramāṇātmakena vikalpajñānabalena vastusvarūpam utsṛjya tad evāpalapantaḥ śraddheyavacanāḥ syuḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, world-fate, dispute_

terms: [vijñāna](../terms/vijnana.md) · concepts: [The three guṇas](../concepts/three-gunas.md), [The object is independent of any one mind (4.15-4.16)](../concepts/object-independent-of-mind.md) · disputes: [Is the object of cognition independent of any mind, or only a construction of cognition?](../debates/ys-object-independent-of-mind.md), [Do objects exist independently of the mind, or are they only constructions of cognition?](../debates/object-independent-of-mind.md)

### 4.15 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-15"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

One common object is the support of many minds; it is not imagined by one mind nor by many minds but stands on itself (sva-pratiṣṭha). How: with the object the same, the cognition differs by the mind: one relying on dharma has cognition of pleasure, one relying on adharma has cognition of pain, one relying on ignorance has deluded cognition, one relying on right seeing has cognition of indifference. An object imagined by one mind cannot colour another's mind. So the path of the object and the path of the cognition, distinguished as grasped and grasping, are separate, with no trace of mixing. On the Sāṃkhya side the object is of three guṇas and moving, connected with minds according to dharma and the like as occasion, and is the cause of the cognition that arises in the form fitting that occasion. Some say the object arises together with cognition because it is enjoyable like pleasure; by this line, obstructing commonness, they deny the form of the object in the earlier and later moments.

> bahucittālambanībhūtam ekaṃ vastu sādhāraṇaṃ, tat khalu naikacittaparikalpitaṃ nāpy anekacittaparikalpitaṃ kiṃtu svapratiṣṭham /
kathaṃ, vastusāmye cittabhedāt dharmāpekṣaṃ cittasya vastusāmye 'pi sukhajñānaṃ bhavaty adharmāpekṣaṃ tata eva duḥkhajñānam avidyāpekṣaṃ tata eva mūḍhajñānaṃ samyagdarśanāpekṣaṃ tata eva mādhyasthyajñānam iti /
kasya tac cittena parikalpitam /
na cānyacittaparikalpitenārthenānyasya cittoparāgo yuktaḥ /
tasmād vastujñānayor grāhyagrahaṇabhedabhinnayor vibhaktaḥ panthāḥ /
nānayoḥ saṃkaragandho 'py astīti /
sāṃkhyapakṣe punar vastu triguṇaṃ calaṃ ca guṇavṛttam iti dharmādinimittāpekṣaṃ cittair abhisaṃbadhyate /
nimittānurūpasya ca pratyayasyotpadyamānasya tena tenātmanā hetur bhavati /
kecid āhuḥ /
jñānasahabhūr evārtho bhogyatvāt sukhādivad iti ta etayā dvārā sādhāraṇatvaṃ bādhamānāḥ pūrvottarakṣaṇeṣu vasturūpam evāpahnuvate.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, dispute, world-fate_

concepts: [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md), [The object is independent of any one mind (4.15-4.16)](../concepts/object-independent-of-mind.md) · disputes: [Is the object of cognition independent of any mind, or only a construction of cognition?](../debates/ys-object-independent-of-mind.md), [Do objects exist independently of the mind, or are they only constructions of cognition?](../debates/object-independent-of-mind.md)

### 4.16 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-16"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

If the object depended on one mind, then when that mind was distracted or restrained, the object's own form, untouched by it, not the object of another, without evidence and not grasped by anyone, would then be what? And when connected again with the mind, whence would it arise? Its parts not then present would not exist, so there would be no back and even the belly would not be grasped. Therefore the object is independent, common to all puruṣas, and minds are independent, working separately in each puruṣa; from the connection of the two comes apprehension, which is the experience (bhoga) of puruṣa.

> ekacittatantraṃ ced vastu syāt tadā citte vyagre niruddhe vāsvarūpam eva tenāparāmṛṣṭam anyasyāviṣayībhūtam apramāṇakam agṛhītasvabhāvakaṃ kenacit tadānīṃ kiṃ tat syāt /
saṃbadhyamānaṃ ca punaś cittena kuta utpadyeta ye cāsyānupasthitā bhāgās te cāsya na syur evaṃ nāsti pṛṣṭham ity udaram api na gṛhyeta /
tasmāt svatantro 'rthaḥ sarvapuruṣasādhāraṇaḥ svatantrāṇi ca cittāni pratipuruṣaṃ pravartante tayoḥ saṃbandhād upalabdhiḥ puruṣasya bhoga iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, dispute_

concepts: [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md), [The object is independent of any one mind (4.15-4.16)](../concepts/object-independent-of-mind.md) · disputes: [Is the object of cognition independent of any mind, or only a construction of cognition?](../debates/ys-object-independent-of-mind.md)

### 4.17 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-17"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Objects, like a magnet (ayaskānta), connect with the mind, whose nature is like iron, and colour it. The object by which the mind is coloured is known; another is unknown. Because a thing is of the nature of known and unknown, the mind is changing (pariṇāmin).

> ayaskāntamaṇikalpā viṣayā ayaḥsadharmakaṃ cittam abhisaṃbandhyoparañjayanti /
yena ca viṣayeṇoparaktaṃ cittaṃ sa viṣayo jñātas tato 'nyaḥ punar ajñātaḥ vastuno jñātājñātasvarūpatvāt pariṇāmi cittam.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [uparāga](../terms/uparaga.md), [citta](../terms/citta.md) · concepts: [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md), [The object is independent of any one mind (4.15-4.16)](../concepts/object-independent-of-mind.md)

### 4.18 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-18"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

If puruṣa, the lord, changed like the mind, the mind's modifications, being its objects, would be known and unknown like objects such as sound. But that the mind's modifications are always known shows that puruṣa, the lord of the mind, is unchanging.

> yadi cittavat prabhur api puruṣaḥ pariṇamet tatas tadviṣayāś cittavṛttayaḥ śabdādiviṣayavaj jñātājñātāḥ syuḥ sadājñātatvaṃ tu manasas tatprabhoḥ puruṣasyāpariṇāmitvam anumāpayati.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate_

terms: [puruṣa](../terms/purusa.md) · concepts: [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md), [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md)

### 4.19 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-19"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

As the other senses and their objects, being seen, are not self-luminous, so the mind is to be understood. Fire is no example: fire does not illuminate a form of its own that was unlit; light is seen in the conjunction of what is illuminated and what illuminates, and there is no conjunction in mere own-form. If the mind were self-luminous it would be ungraspable by anyone, as ākāśa is established in itself and not in another. But the activity of beings is seen from the awareness of the course of their own intellect ('I am angry, I am afraid, here is my attachment, there my anger'), which would not be fitting if one's own intellect were not grasped.

> yathetarāṇīndriyāṇi śabdādayaś ca dṛśyatvān na svābhāsāni tathā mano 'pi pratyetavyam /
na cāgnir atra dṛṣṭāntaḥ na hy agnir ātmasvarūpam aprakāśaṃ prakāśayati prakāśaś cāyaṃ prakāśyaprakāśakasaṃyoge dṛṣṭaḥ /
na ca svarūpamātre 'sti saṃyogaḥ kiṃca svābhāsaṃ cittam ity agrāhyam eva kasyacid iti śabdārthaḥ /
tadyathā svātmapratiṣṭham ākāśaṃ na parapratiṣṭham ity arthaḥ svabuddhipracārapratisaṃvedanāt sattvānāṃ pravṛttir dṛśyate --- kruddho 'haṃ bhīto 'ham amutra me rāgo 'mutra me krodha iti etat svabuddher agrahaṇe na yuktam iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, dispute_

terms: [sva-ābhāsa](../terms/svabhasa.md), [dṛśyatva](../terms/drsyatva.md), [citta](../terms/citta.md) · concepts: [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md), [The mind is not self-luminous (4.19-4.21)](../concepts/citta-not-self-luminous.md) · disputes: [Is the mind self-luminous (illuminating itself and its objects), or is it seen (an object of the seer)?](../debates/ys-citta-self-luminous-or-seen.md)

### 4.20 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-20"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

It is not fitting that in a single moment there be ascertainment of one's own form and another's, for the momentarist (kṣaṇika-vādin) holds that the very becoming (bhavana) is the action and is also the agent (kāraka).

> na caikasmin kṣaṇe svapararūpāvadhāraṇaṃ yuktaṃ, kṣaṇikavādino yad bhavanaṃ saiva kriyā tad eva ca kārakam ity abhyupagamaḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, dispute_

terms: [kṣaṇika-vāda](../terms/ksanika-vada.md) · concepts: [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md) · disputes: [Is the mind self-luminous (illuminating itself and its objects), or is it seen (an object of the seer)?](../debates/ys-citta-self-luminous-or-seen.md)

### 4.21 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-21"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

If a mind were grasped by another mind, by what would the cognition of cognition (buddhi-buddhi) be grasped, and that by another, and that by another: a regress. And memory would be confused: as many memories as there are experiences of these cognitions, and from the confusion there would be no ascertainment of one memory. Thus by the annihilationists (vaināśika), who deny the puruṣa who is aware of the intellect, everything is thrown into confusion; they, imagining an experiencer anywhere, do not cohere by reason. Some, imagining even a 'being' (sattva) who lays down these five aggregates and takes up others, still fear it; and having said 'I shall live the celibate life at the teacher's feet for great disgust with the aggregates, dispassion, non-arising and tranquillity', they then deny the being. The Sāṃkhya-Yoga and other doctrines, by their own word, accept puruṣa as the lord of the mind and the experiencer.

> atha cittaṃ cec cittāntareṇa gṛhyeta buddhibuddhiḥ kena gṛhyate, sāpy anyayā sāpy anyayety atiprasaṅgaḥ /
smṛtisaṃkaraś ca, yāvanto buddhibuddhīnām anubhavās tāvatyaḥ smṛtayaḥ prāpnuvanti /
tatsaṃkarāc caikasmṛtyanavadhāraṇaṃ ca syād ity evaṃ buddhipratisaṃvedinaṃ puruṣam apalapadbhir vaināśikaiḥ sarvam evākulīkṛtam /
te tu bhoktṛsvarūpaṃ yatra kvacana kalpayanto na nyāyena saṃgacchante kecit tu sattvamātram api parikalpyāsti sa sattvo ya etān pañca skandhān nikṣipyānyāṃś ca pratisaṃdadhātīty uktvā tata eva punas trasyanti /
tathā skandhānāṃ mahannirvedāya virāgāyānutpādāya praśāntaye guror antike brahmacaryaṃ cariṣyāmīty uktvā sattvasya punaḥ sattvam evāpahnuvate /
sāṃkhyayogādayas tu pravādāḥ svaśabdena puruṣam eva svāminaṃ cittasya bhoktāram upayantīti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, dispute_

terms: [vaināśika](../terms/vainasika.md) · concepts: [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md), [The mind is not self-luminous (4.19-4.21)](../concepts/citta-not-self-luminous.md), [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md) · disputes: [Is the mind self-luminous (illuminating itself and its objects), or is it seen (an object of the seer)?](../debates/ys-citta-self-luminous-or-seen.md), [Is the mind (cognition) self-luminous, or is it seen by a distinct, unchanging seer?](../debates/is-mind-self-luminous.md), [Is there a self (ātman, puruṣa, jīva) — an enduring conscious subject distinct from body and changing cognitions — or only a stream of impersonal factors designated 'person'?](../debates/is-there-a-self.md)

### 4.22 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-22"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The power of the experiencer is unchanging and not transferred, and yet, as if transferred into the changing object, it follows that object's modification. The cognitive function (jñāna-vṛtti), which by mere imitation is undistinguished from the modification of the intellect that has received the support of consciousness, is so called. As is also said: not in the netherworld, nor in a cleft of the mountains, nor in darkness, nor in the hollows of the oceans: the cave in which eternal Brahman is placed is what the wise declare to be the intellect's modification, undistinguished.

> apariṇāminī hi bhoktṛśaktir apratisaṃkramā ca pariṇāminy arthe pratisaṃkrānteva tadvṛttim anupatati /
tasyāś ca prāptacaitanyopagrahasvarūpāyā buddhivṛtter anukārimātratayā buddhivṛttyaviśiṣṭā hi jñānavṛttir ākhyāyate /
tathā coktam /
"na pātālaṃ na ca vivaraṃ girīṇāṃ naivāndhakāraṃ kukṣayo nodadhīnām / guhā yasyāṃ nihitaṃ brahma śāśvataṃ buddhivṛttim aviśiṣṭāṃ kavayo vedayante" iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate_

terms: [apratisaṃkramā](../terms/apratisamkrama.md), [buddhi-vṛtti](../terms/buddhi-vrtti.md), [bhoktṛ-śakti](../terms/bhoktr-sakti.md) · concepts: [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md), [Reflection of consciousness in buddhi](../concepts/reflection-of-purusa-in-buddhi.md), [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md)

### 4.23 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-23"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The mind is coloured by the object to be thought, and, itself being an object, is connected by its own function with puruṣa the subject. This one mind, coloured by seer and seen, shining as both object and subject, having taken on the form of the sentient and the insentient (cetana-acetana), object-natured yet as if not, insentient yet as if sentient, like a crystal jewel, is called 'all-objective'. Deluded by this likeness of the mind, some say the mind itself is the sentient one; others say all this is mind only and there is no world of cows and pots with causes; they are to be pitied, because they have as their seed of delusion the mind shining with the forms of all things. In the insight of samādhi the known object, reflected, is other than the mind, since it is its support; if that object were mind alone, how could the insight determine its own form by insight itself? Therefore the puruṣa by whom the reflected object in the insight is determined exists. Those who distinguish, by class, grasper, grasping and grasped as three differing forms of the mind are right-seers, and by them puruṣa is reached.

> mano hi mantavyenārthenoparaktaṃ, tat svayaṃ ca viṣayatvād viṣayiṇā puruṣeṇātmīyayā vṛttyābhisaṃbaddhaṃ, tad etac cittam eva draṣṭṛdṛśyoparaktaṃ viṣayaviṣayinirbhāsaṃ cetanācetanasvarūpāpannaṃ viṣayātmakam apy aviṣayātmakam ivācetanaṃ cetanam iva sphaṭikamaṇikalpaṃ sarvārtham ity ucyate /
tad anena cittasārūpyeṇa bhrāntāḥ kecit tad eva cetanam ity āhuḥ apare cittamātram evedaṃ sarvaṃ nāsti khalv ayaṃ gavādir ghaṭādiś ca sakāraṇo loka iti /
anukampanīyās te /
kasmāt, asti hi teṣāṃ bhrāntibījaṃ sarvarūpākāranirbhāsaṃ cittam iti /
samādhiprajñāyāṃ prajñeyo 'rthaḥ pratibimbībhūtas tasyālambanībhūtatvād anyaḥ /
sa ced arthaś cittamātraṃ syāt kathaṃ prajñayaiva prajñārūpam avadhāryeta /
tasmāt pratibimbībhūto 'rthaḥ prajñāyāṃ yenāvadhāryate sa puruṣa iti /
evaṃ grahītṛgrahaṇagrāhyasvarūpacittabhedāt trayam apy etaj jātitaḥ pravibhajante te samyagdarśinas tair adhigataḥ puruṣaḥ.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, dispute_

terms: [citta](../terms/citta.md), [sarvārtha](../terms/sarvartha.md), [cittamātra](../terms/cittamatra.md) · concepts: [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md), [Reflection of consciousness in buddhi](../concepts/reflection-of-purusa-in-buddhi.md), [The object is independent of any one mind (4.15-4.16)](../concepts/object-independent-of-mind.md), [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md) · disputes: [Is the object of cognition independent of any mind, or only a construction of cognition?](../debates/ys-object-independent-of-mind.md), [Do objects exist independently of the mind, or are they only constructions of cognition?](../debates/object-independent-of-mind.md)

### 4.24 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-24"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

This mind, though variegated by countless vāsanās, is for another, for that other's experience and release, not for itself, because it acts in combination, like a house. A mind that acts in combination cannot be for its own sake: the mind of pleasure is not for pleasure, knowledge is not for knowledge; both are for another. The puruṣa who is meaningful by experience and release is the other; not the other as mere generality. Whatever mere-generality 'other' an annihilationist might offer would itself, acting in combination, be only for another. But this special other is puruṣa, who does not act in combination.

> tad etac cittam asaṃkhyeyābhir vāsanābhir eva citrīkṛtam api parārthaṃ parasya bhogāpavargārthaṃ na svārthaṃ saṃhatyakāritvād gṛhavat /
saṃhatyakāriṇā cittena na svārthena bhavitavyaṃ, na sukhacittaṃ sukhārthaṃ na jñānaṃ jñānārtham ubhayam apy etat parārtham /
yaś ca bhogenāpavargeṇa cārthenārthavān puruṣaḥ sa eva paro na paraḥ sāmānyamātram /
yat tu kiṃcit paraṃ sāmānyamātraṃ svarūpeṇodāhared vaināśikas tat sarvaṃ saṃhatyakāritvāt parārtham eva syāt /
yas tv asau paro viśeṣaḥ sa na saṃhatyakārī puruṣa iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, ultimate_

terms: [vāsanā](../terms/vasana.md), [parārtha](../terms/parartha.md), [saṃhatya-kāritva](../terms/samhatya-karitva.md), [citta](../terms/citta.md), [puruṣa](../terms/purusa.md) · concepts: [The mind (citta) in YS 4.15-4.24](../concepts/citta.md), [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md), [Latent tendencies (vāsanā)](../concepts/vasana.md)

### 4.25 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-25"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

As in the rainy season the seed of grass is inferred from the sprouting, so where hair stands on end and tears fall at hearing the path of liberation, a seed of seeing the distinction belonging to release and produced by karma is inferred. For that person the cultivation of self-becoming arises naturally: 'who was I, how was I, what is this, how is this, what shall we be, how shall we be?'. For one who sees the distinction it ceases: this variegated transformation is of the mind alone, and puruṣa, when ignorance is absent, is pure and untouched by the properties of the mind; so for the skilled one the cultivation of self-becoming ceases.

> yathā prāvṛṣi tṛṇāṅkurasyodbhedena tadbījasattānumīyate tathā mokṣamārgaśravaṇena yasya romaharṣāśrupātau dṛśyete tatrāpy asti viśeṣadarśanabījam apavargabhāgīyaṃ karmābhinirvartitam ity anumīyate /
tasyātmabhāvabhāvanā svābhāvikī pravartate yasyābhāvād idam uktaṃ svabhāvaṃ muktvā doṣādyeṣāṃ pūrvapakṣe rucir bhavaty aruciś ca nirṇaye bhavati tatrātmabhāvabhāvanā ko 'ham āsaṃ katham aham āsaṃ kiṃsvid idaṃ kathaṃsvid idaṃ ke bhaviṣyāmaḥ kathaṃ vā bhaviṣyāma iti /
sā tu viśeṣadarśino nivartate kutaḥ cittasyaivaiṣa vicitraḥ pariṇāmaḥ, puruṣas tv asatyām avidyāyāṃ śuddhaś cittadharmair aparāmṛṣṭa iti /
tato 'syātmabhāvabhāvanā kuśalasya nivartata iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: knowledge, meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, powers-experiences_

terms: [ātma-bhāva-bhāvanā](../terms/atmabhava-bhavana.md), [avidyā](../terms/avidya.md), [viveka-khyāti](../terms/viveka-khyati.md) · concepts: [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md)

### 4.26 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-26"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Then the mind that had been sloping towards objects and inclined to ignorance becomes otherwise: sloping towards kaivalya and inclined to the knowledge born of discrimination.

> tadānīṃ yad asya cittaṃ viṣayaprāgbhāram ajñānanimnam āsīt tad asyānyathā bhavati kaivalyaprāgbhāraṃ vivekajajñānanimnam iti.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

terms: [viveka-nimna](../terms/viveka-nimna.md), [kaivalya-prāgbhāra](../terms/kaivalya-pragbhara.md), [citta](../terms/citta.md), [kaivalya](../terms/kaivalya.md) · concepts: [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md), [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md)

### 4.27 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-27"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

For the mind that flows only as the cognition of the distinctness of sattva and puruṣa, sloping towards the discrimination of cognitions, there are other cognitions in its gaps: 'I am', 'mine', 'I know', 'I do not know'. From what? From earlier saṃskāras whose seeds are wearing away.

> pratyayavivekanimnasya sattvapuruṣānyatākhyātimātrapravāhiṇaś cittasya tacchidreṣu pratyayāntarāṇy asmīti vā mameti vā jānāmīti vā na jānāmīti vā /
kutaḥ, kṣīyamāṇabījebhyaḥ pūrvasaṃskārebhya iti.

_level: conventional · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind_

terms: [chidra](../terms/chidra.md), [pratyayāntara](../terms/pratyayantara.md), [saṃskāra](../terms/samskara.md) · concepts: [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md), [Latent tendencies (vāsanā)](../concepts/vasana.md) · obstacles: [Other cognitions in the gaps of discernment](../obstacles/pratyayantara.md)

### 4.28 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-28"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

As the afflictions come to the state of burnt seeds and are unable to sprout, so the earlier saṃskāra, its seed burnt by the fire of knowledge, no longer gives rise to cognition. The saṃskāras of knowledge, however, remain until the completion of the mind's office and so are not a concern.

> yathā kleśā dagdhabījabhāvā na prarohasamarthā bhavanti tathā jñānāgninā dagdhabījabhāvaḥ pūrvasaṃskāro na pratyayaprasūr bhavati /
jñānasaṃskārās tu cittādhikārasamāptim anuśerata iti na cintyante.

_level: conventional · standpoint: seeker · path: meditation · stage: advanced · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation_

terms: [hāna](../terms/hana.md), [saṃskāra](../terms/samskara.md), [kleśa](../terms/klesa.md), [dagdha-bīja](../terms/dagdha-bija.md) · concepts: [Impressions (saṃskāra)](../concepts/samskara.md), [Latent tendencies (vāsanā)](../concepts/vasana.md) · practices: [Cessation of vāsanās and saṃskāras as the text states it](../practices/vasana-ksaya.md)

### 4.29 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-29"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

When this brāhmaṇa is without desire even in prasaṃkhyāna, he asks nothing even of that; being dispassionate there too, discrimination-vision in every way arises, and, with the seeds of saṃskāra destroyed, no other cognitions arise for him; then for him there is the samādhi called dharma-megha.

> yadāyaṃ brāhmaṇaḥ prasaṃkhyāne 'py akusīdas tato 'pi na kiṃcit prārthayate /
tatrāpi viraktasya sarvathā vivekakhyātir eva bhavatīti saṃskārabījakṣayān nāsya pratyayāntarāṇy utpadyante tadāsya dharmamegho nāma samādhir bhavati.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, consciousness-mind_

terms: [prasaṃkhyāna](../terms/prasamkhyana.md), [akusīda](../terms/akusida.md), [viveka-khyāti](../terms/viveka-khyati.md), [dharma-megha](../terms/dharmamegha.md), [samādhi](../terms/samadhi.md), [prasaṃkhyāna](../terms/prasankhyana.md) · concepts: [Dharma-megha samādhi (4.29)](../concepts/dharmamegha-samadhi.md), [Discriminative vision (viveka-khyāti)](../concepts/viveka-khyati.md), [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md) · practices: [Samādhi (in the Vibhūti-pāda account)](../practices/samadhi.md)

### 4.30 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-30"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

From obtaining that, the afflictions, ignorance and the rest, are worked out with their roots, and the stores of karma, good and bad, are struck down with their roots. With the cessation of afflictions and karma, the knowing one is liberated even while living (jīvann eva vidvān vimukto bhavati), because error (viparyaya) is the cause of becoming (bhava); no one whose error has been destroyed is seen born anywhere by anyone.

> tallābhād avidyādayaḥ kleśāḥ samūlakāṣaṃ kaṣitā bhavanti kuśalākuśalāś ca karmāśayāḥ samūlaghātaṃ hatā bhavanti /
kleśakarmanivṛttau jīvann eva vidvān vimukto bhavati kasmāt, yasmād viparyayo bhavasya kāraṇam /
na hi kṣīṇaviparyayaḥ kaścit kenacit kvacij jāto dṛśyata iti.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: experiential · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: realized · types: karma-liberation_

terms: [kleśa](../terms/klesa.md), [karma](../terms/karma.md), [jīvanmukti](../terms/jivanmukti.md), [karmāśaya](../terms/karmasaya.md) · concepts: [Dharma-megha samādhi (4.29)](../concepts/dharmamegha-samadhi.md), [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md), [Liberation while living (jīvanmukti)](../concepts/jivanmukti.md) · obstacles: [The five afflictions (kleśa)](../obstacles/five-klesas.md)

### 4.31 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-31"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

For knowledge freed from all coverings of affliction and karma there is endlessness. Endless knowledge-sattva, covered by veiling tamas, is opened up and capable of grasping only somewhere, when stirred by rajas; when it is free of all coverings and stains, then it is endless. From the endlessness of knowledge the knowable becomes little, like a firefly in the sky. Hence the quoted verse: 'The blind man pierced the jewel; the fingerless threaded it; the neckless wore it; the tongueless praised it.'

> sarvaiḥ kleśakarmāvaraṇair vimuktasya jñānasyānantyaṃ bhavati /
āvarakeṇa tamasābhibhūtam āvṛtam anantaṃ jñānasattvaṃ kvacid eva rajasā pravartitam udghāṭitaṃ grahaṇasamarthaṃ bhavati /
tatra yadā sarvair āvaraṇamalair apagataṃ bhavati tadā bhavaty asyānantyam /
jñānasyānantyāj jñeyam alpaṃ saṃpadyate yathākāśe khadyotaḥ /
yatredam uktam ---
"andho maṇim avidhyat tam anaṅgulir āvayat /
agrīvas taṃ pratyamuñcat tam ajihvo 'bhyapūjayat //" iti.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: experiential · path: knowledge · stage: realized · types: consciousness-mind, karma-liberation, powers-experiences_

terms: [āvaraṇa](../terms/avarana.md), [mala](../terms/mala.md), [jñeya](../terms/jneya.md) · concepts: [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md), [Omniscience in Yoga](../concepts/omniscience-in-yoga.md)

### 4.32 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-32"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

With the arising of dharma-megha, the sequence of transformation of the guṇas whose purposes are fulfilled is brought to its complete end; those who have accomplished experience and release, their sequence complete, cannot stay even for a moment.

> tasya dharmameghasyodayāt kṛtārthānāṃ guṇānāṃ pariṇāmakramaḥ parisamāpyate na hi kṛtabhogāpavargāḥ parisamāptakramāḥ kṣaṇam apy avasthātum utsahante.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: realized · types: karma-liberation, world-fate_

terms: [kṛtārtha](../terms/krtartha.md), [guṇa](../terms/guna.md) · concepts: [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md), [The three guṇas](../concepts/three-gunas.md)

### 4.33 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-33"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

Sequence, of the nature of the immediacy of moments, is grasped by the final end of a transformation; the oldness of a cloth does not come at its end without moments of sequence experienced. Sequence is seen even in the eternal. Eternity is twofold: immovable eternity (kūṭastha-nityatā) and transforming eternity (pariṇāmi-nityatā).

> kṣaṇānantaryātmā pariṇāmasyāparāntenāvasānena gṛhyate kramaḥ na hy ananubhūtakramakṣaṇā purāṇatā vastrasyānte bhavati nityeṣu ca kramo dṛṣṭaḥ /
dvayī ceyaṃ nityatā kūṭasthanityatā pariṇāminityatā ca

_level: bridging · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, world-fate_

terms: [krama](../terms/krama.md), [kūṭastha-nityatā](../terms/kutastha-nityata.md), [pariṇāmi-nityatā](../terms/parinami-nityata.md), [avacanīya](../terms/avacaniya.md), [vyākaraṇīya](../terms/vyakaraniya.md) · concepts: [Two kinds of eternity (bhāṣya 4.33)](../concepts/two-kinds-of-eternity.md), [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md), [Moment and sequence (time)](../concepts/ksana-krama.md), [Plurality of puruṣas](../concepts/plurality-of-purusas.md)

### 4.33 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-33-2"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence moderate

The immovable eternity is puruṣa's, the transforming eternity that of the guṇas: that in which, while it transforms, the reality (tattva) is not destroyed is eternal, and both are eternal because their reality is not struck. In the properties of the guṇas such as the intellect, sequence is grasped by the end of transformation and has a final limit; in the guṇas as eternal bearers it has no final limit. For liberated puruṣas, eternal and immovable, established in mere own-form, the being in own-form is experienced only as sequence, imagined by following the word for existence, and there too has no final limit. The bhāṣya then treats the question whether saṃsāra, abiding and moving in the guṇas, has an end of sequence. It sorts questions: 'will everyone born die?' is answered categorically ('yes'); 'will everyone who dies be born?' is answered by distinction (the skilled one whose discrimination-vision has arisen and whose thirst is exhausted will not be born, the other will); 'is human birth better?' is answered by distinction (better than animals, not better than gods and seers). The question 'is this saṃsāra finite or endless?' is called one not answerable as posed (avacanīya); for the skilled one there is an end of the sequence of saṃsāra, not for the other, and fixing on either alone is a fault. Therefore this question is to be answered by analysis (vyākaraṇīya).

> tatra kūṭasthanityatā puruṣasya /
pariṇāminityatā guṇānām /
yasmin pariṇamyamāne tattvaṃ na vihanyate tan nityam ubhayasya ca tattvānabhighātān nityatvam /
tatra guṇadharmeṣu buddhyādiṣu pariṇāmāparāntanirgrāhyaḥ kramo labdhaparyavasāno nityeṣu dharmiṣu guṇeṣv alabdhaparyavasānaḥ /
kūṭasthanityeṣu svarūpamātrapratiṣṭheṣu muktapuruṣeṣu svarūpāstitā krameṇaivānubhūyata iti tatrāpy alabdhaparyavasānaḥ śabdapṛṣṭhenāstikriyām upādāya kalpita iti /
athāsya saṃsārasya sthityā gatyā ca guṇeṣu vartamānasyāsti kramasamāptir na veti /
avacanīyam etat katham asti praśna ekāntavacanīyaḥ sarvo jāto mariṣyatīti oṃ bhoḥ iti.
atha sarvo mṛtvā janiṣyata iti vibhajyavacanīyam etat /
pratyuditakhyātiḥ kṣīṇatṛṣṇaḥ kuśalo na janiṣyata itaras tu janiṣyate /
tathā manuṣyajātiḥ śreyasī na vā śreyasīty evaṃ paripṛṣṭe vibhajya vacanīyaḥ praśnaḥ paśūn adhikṛtya śreyasī devān ṛṣīṃś cādhikṛtya neti /
ayaṃ tv avacanīyaḥ praśnaḥ saṃsāro 'yam antavān athānanta iti /
kuśalasyāsti saṃsārakramasamāptir netarasyeti anyatarāvadhāraṇe doṣaḥ tasmād vyākaraṇīya evāyaṃ praśna iti.

_level: bridging · standpoint: analytic · path: knowledge · stage: advanced · types: karma-liberation, world-fate_

terms: [krama](../terms/krama.md), [kūṭastha-nityatā](../terms/kutastha-nityata.md), [pariṇāmi-nityatā](../terms/parinami-nityata.md), [avacanīya](../terms/avacaniya.md), [vyākaraṇīya](../terms/vyakaraniya.md) · concepts: [Two kinds of eternity (bhāṣya 4.33)](../concepts/two-kinds-of-eternity.md), [The three transformations of the mind](../concepts/three-parinamas-of-citta.md)

### 4.34 <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-4-34"></a>
`text-verified` · confidence high

Kaivalya is the return to source of the guṇas, of the nature of cause and effect, which have accomplished experience and release and are empty of the purpose of puruṣa. The establishment in own-form is the power of consciousness of puruṣa alone and isolated, because of no connection with the intellect-sattva; its abiding always so is kaivalya.

> kṛtabhogāpavargāṇāṃ puruṣārthaśūnyānāṃ yaḥ pratiprasavaḥ kāryakāraṇātmakānāṃ guṇānāṃ tat kaivalyaṃ, svarūpapratiṣṭhā punar buddhisattvānabhisaṃbandhāt puruṣasya citiśaktir eva kevalā, tasyāḥ sadā tathaivāvasthānaṃ kaivalyam iti.

_level: ultimate · standpoint: apophatic · path: meditation, knowledge · stage: realized · types: karma-liberation, ultimate_

terms: [kaivalya](../terms/kaivalya.md), [pratiprasava](../terms/pratiprasava.md), [svarūpa-pratiṣṭhā](../terms/svarupa-pratistha.md), [citi-śakti](../terms/citi-sakti.md) · concepts: [Kaivalya (YS 3.50, 3.55, 4.26-4.34)](../concepts/kaivalya.md), [Puruṣa (the conscious self)](../concepts/purusa.md), [The three guṇas](../concepts/three-gunas.md)

### mangala <a id="tea-yoga-bhasya-mangala"></a>
`skeleton` · confidence high

Invocation: may the god, the lord of serpents, protect you — he who, leaving his first form, appears in many ways to favour the world; whose mass of afflictions is destroyed; many-mouthed, the source of all knowledge, white and spotless in body, joined with yoga and the giver of yoga.

_level: conventional · standpoint: devotional · path: devotion · stage: all · types: teacher-transmission_
